Hypotrail · Handbook

Reference · for whoever takes the project over

Hypotrail handbook

What each part does, how to run it, its limits, and which decision record explains it. It describes the current state and is rewritten when that changes.

01

Spike

Feasibility spike

The spike proved on the target machine that the chosen stack works before anything general is built: a locked Python 3.13 environment, both public datasets verified, an Inspect task where a model calls a read-only SQL tool on Chinook, and a live trace of a real model doing it. This chapter is the reference for what exists, how to run it and where it stops.

Install and run

The project is a uv project on Python 3.13 with a committed lock. The core needs the standard library and one schema validator, jsonschema; the Inspect and the machine-learning dependencies are optional extras (agent and ml).

# core only: the standard library and jsonschema
uv sync --locked
uv run python -m hypotrail --help

# everything the spike needs, then the offline test suite (no network, no model call)
uv sync --locked --all-extras
uv run --locked --all-extras pytest

# the Python version is pinned by .python-version and requires-python (3.13 only)

The default test command never calls a model, even when a model key is present: every test runs with outbound network access blocked, and an attempt fails the test. Live calls are opt-in and never part of the suite.

Data verification

The two public datasets live in the declarative Workloads examples/bike_day and examples/chinook, each with the slice of data-sources.lock.json that covers it and a data-checks.json that declares what to check. Verification works offline, in a fixed order: the bytes of every selected dataset first, then the licence notices, then the content.

uv run python -m hypotrail data verify                  # both datasets
uv run python -m hypotrail data verify --workload chinook --json
DatasetChecked
Bike (day.csv)SHA-256 and size from the lock; CC BY 4.0 attribution notice present; 731 rows, exactly one per date from 2011-01-01 to 2012-12-31, no gap or duplicate; cnt a non-negative integer and equal to casual + registered.
Chinook (SQLite)SHA-256 and size from the lock; MIT notice present and equal to the lock's notice_sha256; SQLite integrity check ok; the eleven tables have their listed row counts. The file is opened read-only (mode=ro).
Exit codesA flipped byte in either data file exits with 3 (integrity refused) and names the file, before any calendar or table check runs. An unknown Workload exits with 2, and so does data verify when it finds no Workload to verify: it then reports NO_WORKLOAD rather than success. An error nobody planned for exits with 4 and UNEXPECTED_ERROR; only the exception's type is reported, never its text, which could carry a key. With --json, stdout holds one JSON object and every error has code, message, stage and details.

The Inspect spike

The agentic side lives in src/hypotrail/agentic/; the core never imports Inspect (ADR 0001). Inspect owns the agent loop, the tool calls, its limits and its log; Hypotrail owns the scoring function and reads the log only through Inspect's public log API.

  • Task (runner.py): a system message and a user message per Case, the tools, then Inspect's own generate-and-call-tools loop. The task is now built from the Workload declaration; see the chapter on the agentic Track.
  • Tool (sql_tool.py): query_readonly(sql) opens the Chinook file read-only at a path fixed by the application, one connection per execution, execute and never executescript, extensions disabled. mode=ro covers only the main database, so three native SQLite mechanisms decide what runs, and no SQL text is inspected (no regular expression, no SELECT test): the limit on attached databases is 0; execute takes one statement; and an authorizer holds the allow-list, which is SELECT, reads of the tables the file itself declares (read from the file, never named in code, so its system tables such as sqlite_master stay refused) and the functions of the allow-list table in the chapter on the agentic Track (like and glob were added to AG-03's list by the owner's revision of 2026-11-07, #51). Everything else is denied: writes and DDL, ATTACH, DETACH, PRAGMA, transactions, VACUUM (also INTO), extension loading, recursive queries and any other function, so a statement that starts with WITH or SELECT but needs a denied action is refused. It returns columns, rows, row_count, truncated, error and a tool_call_id. A refused or failed statement sets error to a code (DENIED_ACTION, SQL_ERROR or MULTIPLE_STATEMENTS), a type and a message; the code is what to rely on. Four per-query caps bound every call (row, size, time and instruction count; see the agentic Track chapter).
  • Projection (projection.py): one record per Case x Repeat, in the order model turn, tool call, tool result, model turn, final answer. A Repeat is 0-based; Inspect's epoch is 1-based. An Episode is produced, failed (execution stopped with an error, at one of our limits before a final answer, or with its answer cut by the output cap) or missing (absent from the log).
  • Scoring (core/answer_scoring.py): compares status, reason, columns, rows and unit with the frozen Reference. An answer missing one of the six required fields (status, reason_code, columns, rows, unit, evidence) is malformed: parsed is false and every component is false, even when the Reference holds null there. No string-to-number conversion, a boolean is not an integer, NULL stays NULL, non-finite numbers are refused, and the Reference's absolute tolerance applies. agentic/rescore.py rescores a kept log without the model, the tool or the Chinook file; since ticket #45 it goes through the chinook_contract_v1 Scorer (see the agentic Track chapter).
  • Limits (limits.py): set through Inspect's own options and read back from the log.
  • Live execution (live.py): python -m hypotrail spike live --config plain --model anthropic/<model> --out DIR. Opt-in and paid. See below, and the agentic Track chapter for the two commands.

Python 3.13 runs Inspect; there was no need for the 3.12 fallback. The pinned versions are those of uv.lock, and a test compares the four package versions with the lock and the Python version with the running interpreter on major.minor only, and checks that the SQLite version is recorded and well formed without comparing it to the current machine's (the patch level and the SQLite build belong to the machine that wrote the sheet). The sheet therefore fails loudly when a pinned package or the Python minor changes, not when only the machine does.

Effective limits

The column "Effective" is what the live execution's Inspect log recorded, read back through the public log API. A limit is either shown enforced (a named offline test makes a scripted model exceed it), configured, not witnessed, not enforceable, or superseded: a limit the spike ran under that a later owner decision replaced. A superseded row keeps what the spike's own execution recorded and names the key that replaced it, the decision and the chapter that describes it; it claims no witness test.

What the limits do not coverTool calls: Inspect has no limit on them; only the turn limit bounds them indirectly (one call per turn, unless the model calls tools in parallel). Turns: Inspect checks after a generation completes, so the generation that exceeds the limit is still made: a limit of 6 allows 7 provider calls, and the seventh is not kept. Anthropic SDK retries: the SDK retries twice by default, below Inspect's own retry loop; the live execution sets the client's max_retries to 0, which a test checks on the client and which a real provider failure would be the only witness of. Time: 60 s is configured, never reached by a test; the mechanism is shown with a 1 s limit.

The live execution

spike live runs one named Configuration of the spike task on at most two dev Cases (D03 and D05 by default), one Repeat each, under the limits above (the token budget since revised: see the table). It scores nothing against References; it only shows that a real model calls the tool and uses its result.

  • Key: read only from HYPOTRAIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and passed explicitly to the provider; ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is ignored. It is never logged or persisted, and the output directory, including the decoded Inspect log, is scanned for its value before the execution reports, and the scan also runs when a step of the execution fails (a leak is then the error reported). Each log is read back through Inspect's read_eval_log and searched, and its raw bytes are searched too; the log's storage format is never opened by hand (ADR 0001). A log that Inspect cannot read back does not stop the scan of the other files; if no leak is found anywhere and some log could not be read, the execution fails with LOG_NOT_FULLY_SCANNED and the files named, and is never reported clean. Without the key the command exits 5 with the named blocker LIVE_NOT_VALIDATED before any network call. Inspect's Anthropic provider prefers ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN to an explicit key, so the execution refuses to start when that variable is set.
  • Model: an explicit input of the form anthropic/<model>; the code holds no default, and another provider or a cloud-platform variant is refused so the key cannot travel there.
  • Bounds: non-dev Cases and more than two Episodes are refused.
  • Record: run.json holds the provider, the model identifier, the served model, the request parameters the log recorded, the engine version, the effective limits and the hardware; projection.json holds the Episodes; inspect/ holds the Inspect log.

The recorded execution used anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5. Both Episodes ran model, tool call executed on Chinook, model, final answer, and both final answers repeat rows the tool returned. The request carried the provider's defaults for this model: adaptive thinking and a top-level ephemeral cache control, with max_tokens 1024 shared between thinking and the answer.

Trace: run.json, projection.json and the Inspect log under traces/live-spike/inspect/.

Report on the six spike checks

They differ in structured fields: a scorer crash happens after a scorers span began in the sample's events and leaves a complete conversation; an execution failure has no such span and no final answer. The projection reads those fields only and gives a produced Episode with an errored Criterion (reason scoring_failed) versus a failed Item (reason execution_failed).tests/test_answer_scoring.py::test_a_scorer_crash_and_an_execution_failure_project_differently

Choices and limits worth knowing

Tool-call IDs

Inspect gives a tool no public way to read its own call ID while it runs. The tool mints an application ID, puts it in the result the model sees, and the projection keeps the mapping to the native ID.

Scripted executions

Offline executions use Inspect's mock model, recorded as mockllm/model, so no projection of such a log can be taken for a live model.

Rehearsal

The live path is exercised in the suite against a fake endpoint on loopback, so the real Anthropic client, the key header and the key scan are tested without a real call.

Not built

The Import and Assessment contracts.

Refusals of data verify

02

Track

Temporal regression Track

The temporal regression Track runs the daily bike-rental Workload end to end on the Partition dev: it derives the Items of each Partition, builds the References, produces the predictions of two declared Configurations, seasonal_7 and ridge_v1, scores them and writes a first Comparison with per-date paired differences and delta_mae. It is a library driven from Python; init and run ml drive it from the command line and record the result as an Attempt of a Study (chapter 04).

The Workload declaration

The Workload is declarative and holds no Python (ADR 0002). Everything the Track needs is a parameter read from examples/bike_day/workload.json, next to its data (data/day.csv) and licence notice. The Track code in src/hypotrail/ml/ fixes only two things: the Partition roles train, dev and public_check (protocol vocabulary, defined once in partitions.py) and the two kinds of Configuration it knows, with the keys each takes. Every other rule is checked against the declarations below.

The shape of workload.json is described once, as a JSON Schema (src/hypotrail/ml/workload.schema.json) checked by jsonschema, the one schema validator of the core (jsonschema is a pinned dependency of the core, not of an extra). The schema lists every key at every level, each kind of Configuration with the keys it takes, and the type of every value, leaves included: a column name is a string, a period is a whole number (7.0 and true are not), clip_min is a number, sparse_output is a boolean. It also lists the values the Track can run: ridge.solver is one of auto, svd, cholesky, lsqr, sparse_cg, sag, saga; encoder.handle_unknown is ignore or error; encoder.sparse_output is false (the Track feeds a dense matrix to scikit-learn); ridge.alpha is at least 0; the root period_k is at least 1; id_prefix, target_unit, the file and column names, the id of a Configuration and the name of a Partition are not empty (a Partition named "" would otherwise load and lose its Items from the References); the dates of a Partition are calendar dates YYYY-MM-DD; track, when present, is temporal_regression. A declaration that does not match is refused when it is loaded, with a code and the path of the faulty value, before any data is read, any model is fitted, any prediction is made or any file is written. The Track keeps no hand-written check of what the schema guarantees.

Only partitions is required by the schema, because it is the one key every public entry point reads (memberships, References, the seasonal baseline, the Ridge outputs, metrics and the Comparison). Any other key is required by the entry points that read it, which refuse its absence with DECLARATION_INCOMPLETE before they write anything: data_file, date_column, id_prefix and period_k by memberships (and so by References and by the two Configurations); target_column by References and the two Configurations; forbidden_features, allowed_target_features and configurations by the two Configurations; target_unit by metrics and the Comparison. schema_version, track and licence_notice are read by no Track code and may be absent. question, objective and limitations are read by init only, for the StudySpec (chapter 04), and may be absent from a Workload that is never given a Study. The rules that relate values to each other or to the protocol stay in Track code and run after the schema (see Refusals).

The declaration is read once. declaration.load_declaration(workload_dir) is the one loader: it reads workload.json, checks it against the schema and returns a Declaration, a tree of frozen dataclasses (tuples and read-only mappings; a key the schema leaves optional is None when the file lacks it). Every entry point calls it once and every computation starts from the typed object: memberships_of, read_table, read_target_series, validate_configuration, inputs_of and the others take a Declaration and refuse a raw dict with a TypeError. No other module indexes the raw dictionary.

A period is an object too. periods.Period holds a number of days with the key and the path where it was declared, and shifts a date itself: earlier(date) is the date one period before, and window(date) yields the dates 1 to k days before, nearest first. Memberships (the warm-up), the lags and the seasonal baseline all shift through it, and a shift that leaves the calendar is refused there (see Refusals). Two periods of the same length are equal wherever they are declared.

KeyValue for bike_dayUsed for
data_file, date_column, target_column, target_unitdata/day.csv, dteday, cnt, rentals per dayThe table is read by date, never by row position; the unit is carried into every metric.
question, objective, limitationsThe question of SPEC section 2, the comparison of ridge_v1 with seasonal_7 by MAE on dev, and four limitationsCopied into the StudySpec by init; no computation reads them.

The roles are the Track's: fitting is on train, metrics and Comparisons are on dev, public_check is the held-out check. configurationsseasonal_7, ridge_v1The key is the Configuration's id. Each declares its kind: seasonal_baseline or ridge_pipeline.

Memberships and References

write_memberships(workload_dir, out_dir) derives one row per date of the data into memberships.csv with the columns row_id, date, partition and exclusion_reason. A date inside a declared range gets that Partition. A date whose history is incomplete gets no Partition and the reason insufficient_history; for Bike these are the seven first days, 2011-01-01 to 2011-01-07. A declared range may not cover such a day, and a date that falls in no range is refused, so the declaration and the data are always consistent. The result does not depend on the order of the input rows.

write_references(workload_dir, out_dir) writes references.csv (row_id, y_true): the target of every Item that belongs to a Partition. Predictions never carry their own target; metrics join this file by row_id.

PartitionItems
train358
dev182
public_check184
excluded (insufficient_history)7
rows of memberships.csv731

The two Configurations

  • seasonal_7 (kind seasonal_baseline; baselines.py, needs neither scikit-learn nor NumPy): the prediction for date t is the target 7 days earlier. Nothing is fitted. Its declaration holds period_k and post_processing.clip_min. A period must be a declared lag of allowed_target_features.
  • ridge_v1 (kind ridge_pipeline; ridge.py, needs the ml extra: it imports scikit-learn and NumPy, and nothing in the core does): the inputs are the lag features lag_1, lag_7, mean_previous_7, computed from dates in lags.py (only values strictly before t are read), and the calendar columns mnth, weekday, holiday, workingday read as they are. A scikit-learn Pipeline holds a ColumnTransformer (a OneHotEncoder on the calendar columns, handle_unknown=ignore, dense output; a StandardScaler on the lags) and a Ridge (alpha 1.0, intercept, solver svd). The pipeline is fitted once, on the train Partition, always. Predicting any other Partition fits nothing.

Both apply the same post-processing: y_pred is y_pred_raw bounded below at the declared clip_min (0), applied once when the predictions are written. Both go through the same function (configuration.load_configuration) before anything is fitted or predicted. The declared kind picks the keys a Configuration may carry; the schema refuses an unknown kind, or a key that does not belong to its kind, when the declaration is loaded. The checks that apply to every Configuration (forbidden features, target transformations that reach date t or later, target transformations the Workload does not declare) run for every kind.

Package versions are frozen by uv.lock, not recorded here.
comparison.jsoncomparison.comparemode (native_ml), population (Partition, count, row_ids), metric, control and candidate (each with its config_id and the metrics below), delta_mae and differences.

Metrics

metrics.compute_metrics(predictions, references, memberships, workload_dir, partition="dev") joins predictions, References and memberships by row_id and scores y_pred. The predictions must be exactly the Items of the Partition, each with a reference; a repeated row_id, a value that is not a finite number or a missing reference is refused. Reported: mae (the primary metric), rmse, mae_by_month and clipped_count (the number of Items where y_pred differs from y_pred_raw). Each carries the declared unit and a direction (lower_is_better); the order of the files changes nothing.

The function computes metrics on dev only. Any other Partition is refused; public_check has its own code, because the function takes no Study and so has no Exposure journal to write to. Inside a Study, assess accepts public_check Items and records the Exposure (chapter 12).

Comparison on dev

comparison.compare(control_predictions, candidate_predictions, references, memberships, out_dir, workload_dir, partition="dev") pairs the two predictions files by row_id. They must cover exactly the same Items: there is no silent intersection. It reports the metrics of each side over the same scored Items and, for each Item, control_abs_error, candidate_abs_error and delta_abs_error (Candidate minus Control). delta_mae is MAE(Candidate) - MAE(Control) taken from the two reported MAEs: a positive value means the Candidate is worse. An improvement is never required. Here seasonal_7 is the Control and ridge_v1 the Candidate. The command compare (chapter 07) gives the same per-date differences and the same delta_mae (to 1e-9) from the two Assessments of a Study, after the comparability checks.

Numbers produced by running the code on the bike Workload (unit: rentals per day, 182 Items of dev, clipped_count 0 for both):

Refusals

Each refusal is an InvalidInputError (exit code 2 on the command line) with a code, a message, a stage and details. Nothing is dropped or included silently, and a declaration or Configuration is refused before anything is fitted or predicted. Every refusal raised while the declaration is loaded has the stage declaration and its details give key and path (the dotted path of the faulty value, empty at the root). A declaration file that is missing or not JSON is refused with FILE_NOT_FOUND or FILE_NOT_JSON: key is the file name, path is empty (the whole document) and file is its location. A period that no date can apply (it reaches before the first date of the calendar) passes the schema, since it depends on the dates; it is refused when a date is shifted, in memberships, lags and the seasonal baseline, with DECLARATION_VALUE_NOT_ALLOWED, stage periods, and in details the key and path of the declared period, its length in days (days, the declared number, never the offset the loop had reached) and the date being shifted. The same code at load has the stage declaration, so the two are told apart by their stage. An empty name of a Partition or of a Configuration is refused at load with key set to the empty name and path the object that holds it (partitions or configurations).

A key the declaration does not define, in a closed object at any level (for example a stale top-level fit_partition)DECLARATION_KEY_UNKNOWN

A value of the right type that the schema does not allow (period_k of 0 or -1, an empty id_prefix, target_unit, Configuration id or Partition name, ridge.solver: "bogus", encoder.sparse_output: true, a negative alpha, a lag kind other than lag or mean_previous, an empty feature, column or forbidden-feature name, a Partition date such as 2011-02-30, a Partition range that is not two dates, a track other than temporal_regression)DECLARATION_VALUE_NOT_ALLOWED

Ranges that overlap, end before they start, cover a warm-up day, or leave a day in no PartitionPARTITIONS_OVERLAP, BAD_PARTITION_RANGE, PARTITION_COVERS_WARMUP, DATE_IN_NO_PARTITION

A date-keyed lag cannot read the future: a lag whose source date is absent from the series is refused with LAG_SOURCE_MISSING. Other input errors (unreadable or duplicated dates, a missing column, a target that is not a finite number) carry their own codes and name the column or date.

How to run it

Two commands run this Track into a Study and verify the data against its lock first: init and run ml (chapter 04). The functions below are also called from Python, after the data has been checked with uv run python -m hypotrail data verify --workload bike_day; the Track functions themselves do not verify the data. ridge_v1 needs the ml extra (uv sync --locked --all-extras).

# from the repository root
from pathlib import Path
from hypotrail.ml.memberships import write_memberships
from hypotrail.ml.references import write_references
from hypotrail.ml.baselines import write_seasonal_baseline
from hypotrail.ml.ridge import write_outputs
from hypotrail.ml.comparison import compare

workload, out = Path("examples/bike_day"), Path("out")
write_memberships(workload, out)
write_references(workload, out)
write_seasonal_baseline(workload, out / "control", "seasonal_7", "dev")
write_outputs(workload, out / "candidate", "ridge_v1", "dev")   # also writes sklearn_params.json
result = compare(
    out / "control" / "predictions.csv", out / "candidate" / "predictions.csv",
    out / "references.csv", out / "memberships.csv", out / "comparison", workload,
)
print(result["delta_mae"]["value"])

The tests that run the real Workload through both Configurations are tests/test_ml_bike_end_to_end.py; the causality, refusal and Comparison tests are in the other tests/test_ml_*.py files. Like the rest of the suite they run offline and call no model.

The data profile

profile reads a Study of this Track and gives its data profile: the target over time under the Partition band, one distribution per profiled column, the association matrix of the profiled columns in clustering order, and the target against each allowed column and each allowed target feature. Every value comes from the train Items only, read from the Study's own dataset copy and memberships, each read back against its digest. The Workload gives only the names, the marks (forbidden_features, allowed_target_features) and the band. It is a read, as figures is (chapter 17): it takes no lock, writes nothing, records no Exposure and leaves the Write sequence where it was. No value of a dev or public_check day is given, and the series the lags of the train Items are computed from holds no such day. A Study of the Agent Track is refused with UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY, and a target column the Study's dataset copy does not hold with COLUMN_MISSING.

The read computes the Workload's Protocol digest as the freeze record does (chapter 12). workload_protocol is same when it is the digest of the Study's current revision, and differs when the Workload as declared now no longer gives this Study's Protocol. The read answers in both cases: the values still come from the Study's own data, and only the names, the marks and the band are the Workload's of now.

The columns and their kinds are derived, and no Workload key declares them. Every data column but the date and the target is profiled. A column whose train cells are not all finite numbers is categorical; an integer column with at most 12 distinct values on train is categorical too; the others are numeric. On Bike that gives 14 columns: season, yr, mnth, holiday, weekday, workingday and weathersit are categorical, and instant, temp, atemp, hum, windspeed, casual and registered are numeric. instant is the row number of the data file: it is shown, and it rises with the date. yr is 0 on every train day, so each of its pairs has no value. The forbidden columns stay in the distributions and in the matrix, marked not available at prediction time. casual + registered = cnt holds on every row of the file, so these two columns are the target in two parts: they are in the matrix as forbidden columns, and the target itself is in no pair.

The measures are those of the earlier implementation, ported to SciPy: Spearman's rank correlation for two numeric columns, Cramér's V with Bergsma's bias correction for two categorical columns, and the correlation ratio (eta) of the numeric column's ranks across the categories for one of each. A pair is measured on at least 10 rows and is flagged when its absolute value reaches 0.8. The order clusters the columns by average linkage on 1 − |value|, a pair with no value at distance 1, and gives the leaves of the tree. Against the target, a numeric feature gives its points, and a categorical feature gives its count and the figures of the target per value, not points. No p-value is computed, kept or printed, and an association is not an effect on the target.

With --json, stdout holds one object whose key paths are those of the table below. Without it, the read prints one short line per part.

Limits

Study

A Study, the Attempt manifest, the Observation set and the request_id identity exist and are driven by init and run ml (chapter 04). The library functions of this chapter write plain files and know nothing of a Study. Assessments and Comparisons in a Study are chapter 05 and chapter 07.

No Study, no Exposure

The library functions of this chapter know nothing of a Study, so no use of public_check through them can be recorded, and every metric and Comparison on it is refused. In a Study, the freeze record and the Exposure journal allow it through assess and compare (chapter 12).

Data lock

The Track functions do not verify the data against its lock; the caller does. data verify does, and so do init and run ml, which refuse altered data with DATA_INTEGRITY (exit 3) before anything is computed or written.

Comparison contract

The function of this chapter, comparison.compare, compares two predictions files and checks no comparability: mode is a constant, native_ml. The Comparison of two Assessments of a Study, with its strict comparability checks, its descriptive case for an Import and the records of the contract, is the core command compare (hypotrail/core/comparison.py, chapter 07; ADR 0001 names the Comparison as a core concept). This file-based function is kept as the library entry point and as the oracle its numbers are checked against.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0002Why the Partition date ranges, the target, the lag period, the forbidden features and the Configurations are parameters in workload.json. The Partition roles and the Configuration kinds are the Track's vocabulary, not Workload parameters.
0001Why the core does not import Inspect; this Track does not use Inspect, and scikit-learn is imported by ridge.py only.
0003Why this handbook is a reference for behaviour, updated in the pull request that changes it.

Refusals of the Track's library functions

The Track's library functions refuse with these codes, all with exit 2. run ml reaches the codes of the table, series, features, seasonal_baseline and predictions stages. The metrics and comparison stages (DUPLICATE_ROW_ID, REFERENCE_MISSING, CONFIG_ID_NOT_UNIQUE and the metrics side of NOT_A_NUMBER and NON_FINITE_NUMBER) belong to the library comparison of the Track, and the score stage to the scoring function of the spike (regression.py): no command calls them. assess and compare refuse with the codes of chapter 05 and chapter 07.

03

Track

Agentic Track

The agentic Track asks a model Chinook questions and scores the answer it keeps. This chapter describes the part that exists: the Workload declaration and the task built from it, and the chinook_contract_v1 Scorer, which turns one Episode's raw final answer, its Reference and its projected trace into four Criteria, exact_table, format_valid, evidence_link_valid and contract_success, and keeps failures of the instrument apart from failures of the evaluated system. The spike chapter covers the executions and the log projection.

Where the Scorer lives

The Scorer is src/hypotrail/core/chinook_contract.py. It needs the standard library and jsonschema only; the core never imports Inspect (ADR 0001). Rescoring a kept log (agentic/rescore.py) projects the log, then applies this Scorer to each Episode. It calls neither the model nor a tool and never opens the Chinook file: the frozen References are enough. The output of every Episode records the Scorer's name, chinook_contract_v1, and its version, and two rescorings of one log give the same verdicts.

The Scorer takes the raw final answer unchanged and keeps it unchanged in its output, whether or not it respects the schema.

The Workload declaration

The Workload is declarative and holds no Python (ADR 0002). examples/chinook/workload.json is checked as a whole against one JSON Schema, src/hypotrail/agentic/workload.schema.json, by jsonschema. declaration.load_workload(directory) reads and checks it and returns a Workload; it imports neither Inspect nor any Reference, so a refusal comes before Inspect runs. The schema requires every key except scenarios, answer_schema and the Partitions under cases (cases must hold at least one); the table below says which entry point refuses each absent key (ADR 0002, clarification of 2026-11-04). Every object is closed, except configurations, whose keys are the Workload's own Configuration names; the Partition names under cases are closed to the Track's two roles.

KeyValue for chinookUsed for
schema_version, track, workload_idworkload/1, tool_using_agent, chinookDescriptive, except workload_id, which names the task (chinook_plain).
licence_noticelicenses/Chinook-MIT.txtThe licence notice of the database.
databasefile: data/Chinook_Sqlite.sqlite; dataset_idThe one database path both tools use. The model never chooses a path.
casesdev and public_check, each a JSON Lines fileThe Cases of each Partition. The two roles are the Track's (agentic/partitions.py): a Workload sets the file of each but cannot name another Partition (ADR 0002, amendment of 2026-11-04). The builder takes dev unless told otherwise; the file also tells which Partition a Case is in, which the refusal of public_check Items reads.
referencesreferences/chinook-references.jsonlNames the References file. The task builder never reads it. The numeric tolerance (E08) is held by the References, not repeated here. A record's reference_version is the set version in which this record was written: chinook-references/1 for the 20 records of the kit, chinook-references/2 for the 92 appended after them. The review record of the second set, references/chinook-references-2.review.jsonl, lists every candidate Case with both writers' answers and its outcome; no key of the declaration names it.

A Case ID listed in two Partitions, refused whenever a Workload is constructed (a Partition is a disjoint subset)PARTITIONS_NOT_DISJOINT
workload.json, the answer schema, the scenarios file or a declared Cases file absent, or not JSON (a Cases line that is not a JSON object with a case_id is FILE_NOT_JSON)FILE_NOT_FOUND, FILE_NOT_JSON
The scenarios file does not match its schemaSCENARIOS_INCOMPLETE, SCENARIOS_KEY_UNKNOWN, SCENARIOS_WRONG_TYPE, SCENARIOS_VALUE_NOT_ALLOWED (stage fixture)

Each refusal is an InvalidInputError (exit code 2 once a command line exists) with a code, a message, a stage and details. The stage is declaration, except for the scenarios file, whose refusals have the stage fixture. The details depend on the refusal. ANSWER_SCHEMA_NOT_DECLARED (stage declaration) and SCENARIOS_NOT_DECLARED (stage fixture) give only the path, answer_schema or scenarios. A schema refusal (DECLARATION_*, SCENARIOS_*) gives the key (null when the fault is the whole document) and the path, and DECLARATION_WRONG_TYPE and SCENARIOS_WRONG_TYPE also the expected type. A Configuration refusal gives the configuration and the path. PARTITIONS_NOT_DISJOINT gives the path, the case_ids and the partitions. PARTITION_NOT_DECLARED gives the partition and the path. An absent or non-JSON workload.json, answer schema or scenarios file gives the key, an empty path and the file. A Cases file refusal gives the file, plus the line when one line is at fault (not for an unreadable file, nor for CASE_ID_NOT_UNIQUE, which gives the case_id instead). The codes CONFIGURATION_NOT_DECLARED, CONFIGURATION_CYCLE, PARTITION_NOT_DECLARED and PARTITIONS_NOT_DISJOINT relate values to each other or to the declaration, which JSON Schema cannot express, so they stay in Track code (ADR 0002, clarification of 2026-11-04). CASE_ID_NOT_UNIQUE is raised by the Cases reader in Track code.

The two Configurations

  • plain carries the system text in full. It tells the model to answer with the available tools and not to guess, to answer in the requested schema table-answer/1, and to distinguish an answer, an ambiguity and unavailable information. It states the common format rules: one JSON object and nothing else, no Markdown fence, no extra prose, the six fields, the three statuses and their reason_codes (criterion_undefined for needs_clarification, field_not_available for not_answerable), with columns and rows empty and unit null for the last two.
  • verify is plain (extends) followed by a declared added_instruction and nothing else: check the scope, the joins, the units and the result before concluding. Its resolved text is exactly plain's text plus the instruction. No Reference value is in either text.

The task

runner.build_task(workload, configuration, cases=None, partition="dev") returns the Inspect task. runner.run_episodes(cases, workload=..., configuration=..., model=..., repeats=..., log_dir=...) runs it. The task replaces the spike's single prompt and its chinook_spike task.

  • Each Case is one sample. Its input is two messages: the Configuration's system text, and a user message rendered from the question, the requested columns and the unit only (prompt.render_user_message). A Case with no unit goes through the same rendering (Unit: none), so the system text is identical for every Case of a Configuration. The Case identifier, the group, the family, the Reference rows, the oracle SQL, the expected status and the tolerance never reach the model.
  • The model gets exactly two tools, describe_schema and query_readonly, both bound to the database path of the declaration. describe_schema takes no argument and returns the eleven tables with their columns, types and foreign-key relations, read from SQLite itself (PRAGMA); it holds no row, Reference or oracle SQL.
  • Both Configurations run with the same tools, limits and generation settings; only the system text differs.
  • The log header keeps the Configuration name and its exact system text (eval.metadata), and the live run record (run.json) repeats them.
  • The References are not an input of the builder: a task built from the declaration runs when the References file is absent.
Open pointThe builder can build a task on any declared group of Cases, public_check included. The builder does not refuse it; the counting and rescoring entry points do (see the scripted_fixture section), because they take no Study. Inside a Study, assess accepts public_check Episodes and records the Exposure (chapter 12).

The read-only SQL tool

The evaluated model reaches Chinook through two tools, describe_schema (see above) and query_readonly(sql) (agentic/sql_tool.py; the connection details are in the spike chapter). No SQL text is inspected: three native SQLite mechanisms decide what runs. The limit on attached databases is 0, execute takes a single statement, and an authorizer holds the allow-list.

  • Allowed: SELECT, reads of the tables the Chinook file itself declares, and the functions of the table below. AG-03 did not list like and glob; the owner added them by an explicit revision of AG-03 on 2026-11-07 (#51). SQLite reports the operators LIKE (also NOT LIKE and LIKE … ESCAPE) and GLOB to the authorizer under these two names; both only compare text, and the per-query caps still bound any pattern. LIKE keeps SQLite's default matching (case-insensitive for ASCII; GLOB is case-sensitive) because PRAGMA case_sensitive_like stays refused.
  • Tables read from the file: the table names are never written in the code, so a table the file does not declare is not readable, and the system tables such as sqlite_master stay refused.
  • Denied: writes and DDL, ATTACH, DETACH, PRAGMA, transactions, VACUUM (also INTO), extension loading, recursive queries and any other function. A statement that starts with WITH or SELECT but needs a denied action is refused.

A refused or failed statement sets error to a code, a type and a message; the code is what to rely on.

Allowed functionKind
countAggregate.
sumAggregate.
avgAggregate.
minAggregate.
maxAggregate.
roundScalar.
lowerScalar.
upperScalar.
coalesceScalar.
ifnullScalar.
strftimeScalar.
likeOperator LIKE, reported under this name (#51).
globOperator GLOB, reported under this name (#51).
CodeMeaning
DENIED_ACTIONThe authorizer refused an action outside the allow-list.
SQL_ERRORSQLite failed the statement for another reason (syntax, unknown table or column).
MULTIPLE_STATEMENTSThe text holds more than one statement.

Four caps per query

Every call is also bounded by four caps (QueryCaps in agentic/sql_tool.py, AG-04). A cap that is reached is never turned into a complete result: it shows as truncated: true, or as an error that names the limit and comes with no rows.

CapDefaultWhen reached
Watched time1 s of wall clockError code TIME_LIMIT, no rows.
Instruction ceiling100,000,000 SQLite VM instructionsError code INSTRUCTION_LIMIT, no rows.
Rows200 returned, 201 fetchedThe first 200 rows in the query's order, truncated: true.
Serialized size64 KiB (the JSON of columns and rows)The longest prefix of rows that fits, truncated: true, and a row_count that matches the rows returned.

SQLite's progress handler, called every 1000 instructions, watches the first two caps. The fetch stops at 201 rows through fetchmany, so the extra row only tells that more exist. AG-04 gives no number for the instruction ceiling, so 100,000,000 is a declared default: the 1 s time stays the primary cap, and the ceiling is a machine-independent backstop set near it (about 0.7 s at the roughly 138 million instructions per second measured on the development machine). It is about 3,000 times the heaviest of the 16 oracle statements (about 32,000), so it does not refuse a read the time cap would allow on this machine. It changes only by a new value in the record. The two time mechanisms are witnessed apart: each test puts the other cap out of reach.

The query runs in a worker thread (anyio.to_thread), not on Inspect's event loop. A slow query therefore does not stop the Episode time limit from taking control: when that limit cancels the call, a stop flag read by the progress handler interrupts the statement. The four caps are recorded in the task metadata and read back with the Episode limits, under query_caps (read_back in agentic/limits.py).

The four Criteria

CriterionScored true whenScored false when
exact_tableStatus, reason_code, columns in order, rows in the requested order, types and unit all equal the Reference. The comparison is made on what is read from the answer.Any of them differs, or what is read lacks one of the six fields (every component is then false, with the Reason invalid_response_format). An extra field alone does not make it false.
format_validThe raw answer is one JSON object, with no Markdown fence and no prose, valid against the Workload's table-answer/1 schema.Otherwise, with the Reason invalid_response_format, whatever the other Criteria read: text that is not exactly one JSON object (wrapped answers included), a missing or extra field, or needs_clarification with non-empty rows.
evidence_link_validEvery ID the answer cites is the application tool_call_id of a successful tool call of this Episode, made before the final answer, whose native ID matches a tool-call event of the log. An answered answer must cite at least one.A cited ID is found nowhere in the trace (another Case or another Repeat included), designates a call that failed, comes after the final answer, or has a native ID matching no tool-call event; or an answered answer cites nothing; or nothing is read from the answer, or the object read is not valid against the schema (invalid_response_format, trace or not). What is read from a wrapped answer is set out in its table. Reasons: evidence_unknown_id, evidence_call_failed, evidence_after_final_answer, evidence_no_native_event, evidence_missing.
contract_successFor answered: exact_table and format_valid and evidence_link_valid all true. For needs_clarification and not_answerable: format_valid true and the exact status and reason_code, with no tool call required.As soon as one of those is false. A malformed answer is never a success, even when the Reference is missing.

The comparison rules are the spike's: no string is converted to a number, a boolean is not an integer, NULL stays NULL, non-finite numbers are refused, and no number is picked out of prose. The numeric tolerance is read from the Reference (numeric_absolute_tolerance), not from the code: on E08 an answer within 0.005 of 283910.04 is correct and one beyond it is not, and every other number, whose Reference tolerance is 0, is compared exactly.

Three situations, three outcomes

SituationCriterion stateReasonCounts against the evaluated system
Reference missingexact_table unscoredreference_missingNo
Reference invalid (for example without rows)exact_table unscoredreference_invalidNo
Crash of the Scorerevery Criterion erroredscoring_failedNo
Malformed answerformat_valid and contract_success scored false; exact_table and evidence_link_valid follow what is readinvalid_response_formatYes

format_valid does not need the Reference, so a missing Reference leaves it scored. A crash is caught where the core applies the Scorer to one Episode (apply_scorer): that Episode's Criteria become errored, the exception's type goes into the structured field error_type (the message is never kept or read), the other Episodes are still scored, and the Episode's execution status stays produced. The log projection of the spike keeps telling an Inspect scorer crash from an execution failure through structured fields of the log, never through error text.

Wrapped answers

The prompt asks for a single JSON object and nothing else, and a model sometimes puts a sentence before it or a Markdown fence around it. By owner decision of 2026-11-07 (#58, option E) the Scorer keeps the format as its own Criterion and stops letting the wrapping hide the content. The answer is scanned left to right; at each { the standard JSON decoder tries to read one value (NaN and the infinities are refused, and so is a number that decodes to a non-finite float), a read that gives an object is a candidate and the scan resumes after it, and a failed read (a stray brace in prose, a truncated object, a non-finite number) skips only its own {, so an object that decodes further on, even inside the text of a failed read, is still a candidate. find_embedded_object in core/answer_scoring.py returns the decoded candidate with its text only when there is exactly one, wherever it sits; the table below says what each outcome means. parse_answer is unchanged and still decides format_valid. The raw answer is never altered, and the record says what was scored in its top-level field answer_read_from. The Scorer's version was 2 when that decision was taken (#58), since the same raw answer then scored differently; its name stays chinook_contract_v1.

A number that decodes to a non-finite float, such as 1e999 or -1e999, is refused like NaN: the JSON value holding it is unreadable, so a bare answer holding one reads nothing (invalid_response_format), and in a wrapped answer it is a failed read that skips only its own {. Numbers that stay finite are read as they were (1e308, 5e-324, and an integer of any length up to the limit below, even one too large for a double), and the tokens NaN, Infinity and -Infinity stay refused. An integer of more than 4,300 digits, which Python refuses to read, leaves the answer unreadable in the same way, unless an object can still be read inside it. Since #61 the Scorer's version is 3, because the verdicts of those answers changed; the answer schema table-answer/1 is unchanged, as the rule is the Scorer's.

The answerScored false, with invalid_response_formatScored onanswer_read_from
Exactly one JSON object and nothing else, valid against the schemaNone for this reasonexact_table and evidence_link_valid are scored on the answer itselfraw_answer
Exactly one JSON object and nothing else, not valid against the schema (an extra field, for example)format_valid, evidence_link_valid, contract_successexact_table as for that object alone (false, with invalid_response_format, when a field is missing)raw_answer
Not exactly one JSON object, but containing exactly one, valid against the schema (prose before or after it, a Markdown fence around it, an array holding it)format_valid, contract_successexact_table and evidence_link_valid have the state, score and Reason they would have if the answer were that object aloneembedded_object
The same, with an object that is not valid against the schema (an extra field, for example)format_valid, evidence_link_valid, contract_successexact_table as for that object alone (false, with invalid_response_format, when a field is missing)embedded_object
A wrapped answer whose wrapper is not JSON, such as {"answer": <object>, "note": NaN}, the same with 1e999, or a truncated wrapper around a complete object, the object being valid against the schemaformat_valid, contract_successThe object that decodes, as for any wrapped answerembedded_object
The same, with an object that is not valid against the schemaformat_valid, evidence_link_valid, contract_successexact_table as for that object alone (false, with invalid_response_format, when a field is missing)embedded_object
No JSON object (text, a number, an array without an object), or more than one, or a bare answer holding a number that decodes to a non-finite float or an integer of more than 4,300 digitsAll four Criteria, except that exact_table is unscored with reference_missing or reference_invalid when the Reference is absent or unusable (an Instrument failure, never held against the system)Nothing is readnull

Made valid JSON, a non-JSON wrapper is itself the one object, and is judged as such: {"answer": <right table>, "note": NaN} is read on the right table, while {"answer": <right table>, "note": 1} is read on the whole wrapper, which lacks the six fields.

The evidence link and contract success

A successful tool call is one with no Inspect-level tool error and no structured error in its result body (the shape {code, type, message} that the read-only SQL tool returns, for example when its authorizer refuses a statement). The Scorer reads the Episode's projected steps, plain records, and never an Inspect object; the projection itself still reads the log only through Inspect's public log API. The link is a check that a cited ID points to a real, earlier, successful call. It is not a proof that every claim in the answer follows from the trace.

An exact table with no valid evidence is exact but not a success of the tool-based path: exact_table true, evidence_link_valid false, contract success false. Contract success is scored false as soon as one dependency is false, whatever the state of the others. Only when none is false and one is unscored or errored does it take that state and Reason: a well-formed answer with valid evidence and a missing Reference gives unscored with reference_missing. Without a trace, evidence_link_valid is unscored (trace_missing), and so is the contract success of an answered answer; for an answer whose evidence cannot be read (nothing is read from it, or the object read is not valid against the schema), evidence_link_valid is scored false with invalid_response_format, trace or not.

The scripted_fixture Mode

fixture.run_fixture(workload_dir=..., out_dir=..., configuration=...) runs the Chinook task under a Configuration the Workload declares (the Track names none as a default), and runs the Chinook task with Inspect's own mock model, which plays scripted responses. The tools are the real local tools on the Chinook file; the task (runner.run_episodes), the projection and the Scorer are the code a live execution uses. Only the model differs. It needs no network and no provider key.

The scripts are data in examples/chinook/fixtures/scenarios.json, not Python. Each entry is one model turn: a tool call (with repeat on the last turn, it repeats), an answer built from the last tool result and citing its own tool_call_id, or a raw answer kept verbatim. The scenario Cases (SCN-01 to SCN-07) are technical: none is one of the 20 business Cases, and the execution records business_case_outputs: 0.

ScenarioExecution statusCriteria
good_resultproducedAll four scored true.
wrong_resultproducedexact_table and contract_success scored false; the format and the evidence link are valid.
invalid_formatproducedformat_valid, exact_table, evidence_link_valid and contract_success scored false with invalid_response_format (prose is not an answer); the raw answer is kept.
recovered_tool_errorproducedScored as usual. The failed call (SQL_ERROR) stays in the projected trace and is counted apart, by code, under tool_errors.
budget_exhaustedfailed, Reason turn_limitEvery Criterion scored false with turn_limit: the stop is the agent's own failure.
missing_referenceproducedexact_table and contract_success unscored with reference_missing; the other two scored true.
absent_from_log (the seventh check)missingNever run. Expected but not attempted, every Criterion unscored with observation_missing.

counts.tally gives, per Criterion, N_expected, N_attempted, N_produced, N_scored, N_correct, N_incorrect, N_unscored and N_errored, with N_scored = N_correct + N_incorrect. In the scripted execution the denominators differ (7 expected, 6 attempted, 5 produced) and each scenario moves a different combination of counts. Tool errors are counted apart. The shares and the Assessment contract belong to #9.

An Episode stopped by a limit. Stopped by one of our limits before a usable final answer, an Episode is failed, never produced with no answer, and its Reason names the stop. It stays in N_expected and N_attempted, out of N_produced, and has a state for every Criterion (owner decision D1 on #8, rescore.criteria_for_limit_stop), according to who is responsible for the stop:

StopReasonEvery Criterion
Turn limit; output-token budget (Inspect's TokenLimit(type="output"))turn_limit, token_limitScored false (the agent's own failure, contract success false). token_limit means that budget, whose value is the row of the table of current limits.
Episode cost limit (Inspect's cost_limit, when one is set)cost_limitScored false, as token_limit (D-146 on chadlis/hypotrail#75, by analogy with owner decision D1). Inspect checks the cost before every model call and after it, so an Episode can overshoot the limit by the one call in flight.
Episode time limittime_limitunscored with time_limit: wall time mixes the agent and the provider's latency.
Final answer cut by the output cap (stop_reason max_tokens on the final model call)output_capunscored with output_cap: the cap is our setting. The cut text stays in the projected steps.

The token budget meters output tokens only. By owner decision on #50 (2026-11-07) the Episode token budget is set with Inspect's own TokenLimit(type="output") (reasoning tokens count as output), and the table below gives the limits in force. It replaces AG-11's 12,000 tokens in total for this Track: the input history and the cached tokens resent at every call no longer weigh on the budget, so a stop measures what the agent wrote. Inspect keeps the count and the metering type apart in the log header (eval.config.token_limit and token_limit_type, where None means all tokens), and a stop raises the same token limit whatever the type, so the Episode's stopped_by_limit reads {"type": "token", "limit": ...} with the table's limit. plain and verify get the same budget. An Episode stopped by it stays in N_expected and N_attempted and out of N_produced, every Criterion counted incorrect; a time-limit stop still leaves every Criterion unscored. The budget's value is a judgement, to be checked on an Attempt.

LimitValueMeteringStateWitnessWitness file
Model turns per Episode6shown enforcedtest_the_turn_limit_stops_the_episode_at_the_configured_counttests/test_limits.py
Tool calls per Episode8not enforceabletest_inspect_has_no_tool_call_limittests/test_limits.py
Seconds per Episode60configured, not witnessedtest_the_time_limit_stops_a_slow_episodetests/test_limits.py
Output tokens per Episode (the token budget)4000outputshown enforcedtest_the_output_token_budget_stops_the_episodetests/test_limits.py
Output tokens per model call1024configured, not witnessedtest_the_output_token_cap_reaches_every_model_calltests/test_limits.py

Cost per Episode in USD (Inspect's per-sample cost_limit; off unless run agent --mode llm_live is given --episode-cost-limit-usd and --price-list, chapter 13)noneshown enforcedtest_the_cost_limit_stops_the_episodetests/test_limits.py
Parallel tool calls (Inspect's parallel_tool_calls, sent to Anthropic as disable_parallel_tool_use: true): one tool call per turn is requested from the provideroffconfigured, not witnessedtest_one_tool_call_per_turn_reaches_every_model_calltests/test_limits.py

In the record, the budget's key names its metering and token_budget_metering stands beside it:

Token limit in the logKey of the budgettoken_budget_meteringRefusalWhere it occurs
metering type outputoutput_token_budgetoutputThe budget in force, requested and read back.
metering type all, or a limit with no metering type (None: all tokens)total_token_budgetallA log made under the spike's all-token budget, read back offline. No field of the current record calls the output budget a total.
no limitnullNo budget key stands in the record; token_budget_metering is the record's JSON null, which makes the absence visible.
another metering type (an Inspect formula)TOKEN_METERING_UNSUPPORTEDRefused when the log is read back or summarised.

A final answer that is in the state and was cut by max_tokens is output_cap, whatever limit Inspect recorded (D1: the token budget applies before a final answer). A recorded limit decides only when no final answer exists. Both unscored cases are Instrument failures as the glossary words them; a new Assessment can revise them, since each Criterion keeps its Reason.

Public_check Items are refused. rescore.rescore_episodes, rescore.rescore_log and counts.tally take the Workload and refuse a public_check Item before any computation, with PUBLIC_CHECK_RESCORING_REFUSED or PUBLIC_CHECK_COUNTS_REFUSED. The Partition of an Item is the one the Workload declares for its Case, not a label the caller passes; a Case the declaration does not list (a scenario) has none and is not refused. They take no Study, so they have no journal to write to and keep the refusal; inside a Study, assess does this scoring and counting, and lifts the refusal with the Exposure journal (chapter 12). Scoring one answer against one Reference (score_episode) stays allowed. The scenarios are not public_check Items.

The library function writes under out_dir; run agent --mode scripted_fixture records the same scenarios as a scenario Attempt of a Study (chapter 13). What the function writes names the Mode and carries the banner "test simulation, no LLM evaluated" (one text, for the library and the Study): run.json, projection.json, verdicts.json and the Inspect log header. The recorded model is mockllm/model. run.json also holds the Inspect version the log records (not the installed one), the effective limits read back from the log (the per-query caps included), the tool-call limit named as not enforceable (Inspect has none), the note on how many provider calls the turn limit allows, and the SHA-256 of the Chinook file before and after the library fixture's execution, which must be equal. The banner as a report renders it is #10's.

Choices and limits worth knowing

Malformed means not exact, unless it only wears a wrapper

What the other two Criteria read is set out in the table of wrapped answers; a failure to read is the system's failure, not the instrument's.

Failed and missing Episodes

Every expected Episode has a state for every Criterion, with a Reason for each non-score (rescore.rescore_episodes). An Episode failed by an execution error has every Criterion unscored with execution_failed; an Episode absent from the log is missing, every Criterion unscored with observation_missing. Neither is scored false, and neither has an empty list. An Episode stopped by one of our limits is failed too (see the scripted_fixture section), with Criteria that depend on who stopped it.

The schema is an input

The Scorer is given the Workload's table-answer/1 schema; it does not read it from disk, so rescoring opens no Workload file.

Not built

An Inspect @scorer adapter (ADR 0001's parity test applies if one is built). The report of a Chinook Study exists since #86: see chapter 14. An Inspect log is imported into a Study since #69: see chapter 09. Answers imported as JSONL without a trace, the Assessment contract with its counters and shares, and the closed Scorer registry that holds this Scorer exist since #68: see chapter 08.

The live command

spike live runs one declared Configuration of the Chinook task against a real Anthropic model. It is opt-in, paid and bounded by the limits of the table above, among them the output-token budget metered by Inspect's TokenLimit(type="output"), and it is never part of the tests or CI. It writes only under --out and records nothing in a Study; run agent --mode llm_live (chapter 13) is the command that records a native Attempt of the llm_live Mode in a Study, with its own bounds (a Partition's Cases times three Repeats at most), while spike live keeps its two Episodes on dev. It was rehearsed offline against a local fake endpoint, and the live Episodes kept so far (eight, two in each of four --out directories, made on 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-07) are recorded below. The rule is that the References review of D07, D08, E11 and E12 comes before any live Episode. The owner did that review on 2026-11-06, before the live Episodes, and accepted the four References as written (comment on chadlis/hypotrail#8, owner decisions of 2026-11-06, point 2). The live Episodes below, and the 72-Episode campaign, happen outside the ticket that built the command (#49).

export HYPOTRAIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...   # the only variable read for the key
uv run python -m hypotrail spike live --config plain  --model anthropic/<model> --out DIR_PLAIN
uv run python -m hypotrail spike live --config verify --model anthropic/<model> --out DIR_VERIFY

spike live takes --examples-dir to name the Workloads from an install that is not a checkout (chapter 04). Each output directory must be empty. With no --case, the spike live execution takes the two dev join Cases, D03 and D05, one Repeat each, so two Episodes; the record names them. The command refuses, before the key is read and before Inspect is imported, a Configuration the Workload does not declare, a model that is not an explicit anthropic/<model> (there is no default), a Case outside dev, and more than two Episodes. With no key it exits 5 and sends nothing. The output is scanned for the key before the execution reports.

Reasoning is disabled, identically for plain and verify, and the output cap per model call is kept (owner decision 1 on #8). A reasoning level would draw its thinking from the same per-call cap as the answer, so an answer could be cut by our own setting; disabled leaves the whole cap to the answer. A test captures the request bodies of both Configurations at the fake endpoint and checks that each carries the same thinking field and the same max_tokens, the cap of the table above. Which thinking value a model gets is Inspect's to decide: {"type": "disabled"}, or {"type": "between_tools"} for Sonnet 5.5, which rejects disabled. Inspect's source describes between_tools as no up-front thinking and the model's lowest setting; it does not say whether thinking may still happen between tool calls, so that is not claimed here. Before any provider call, the execution asks Inspect's own request building (completion_config, no network) what the request would carry; if it would not state the reasoning setting (for example Opus 5.5 cannot disable thinking, and models before 4.7 get no thinking field), the execution is refused with REASONING_NOT_EXPLICIT and nothing is sent. The record keeps the field as it was sent, with this note.

The projection records why each model call stopped. A live Episode whose final call stopped on the output cap is failed with the Reason output_cap, never produced; one stopped by the turn, token or time limit carries turn_limit, token_limit or time_limit. How they are scored is described above; live Episodes are still not scored against References here.

run.json holds: the Mode llm_live and the provider; the requested and served model; the Configuration name and its exact system text; the reasoning setting (requested and as sent) and the output cap (requested and as sent); the engine version, Python and hardware (where each field comes from); the requested limits (requested_limits) and the effective limits (effective_limits), with the token budget under the key of the table above and token_budget_metering beside it. The effective limits are read back from the log and include the per-query caps. request_source says where the requested limits come from (see its table); a request read from a kept log goes through the same header fields as the effective limits, so they agree wherever the log records the limit, with tool_calls null because no log holds it, and output_cap's requested value follows the same source; the statements that Inspect has no tool-call limit and how many provider calls the turn limit allows; the Cases of the execution; the SHA-256 of the Chinook file before and after the execution; and, per Episode, the number of model calls, the latency, the cost when the log gives one, and five token figures under Inspect's own field names: input_tokens, output_tokens, input_tokens_cache_write, input_tokens_cache_read and total_tokens; token_budget (the budget Inspect recorded on the sample, under the same key and with the same token_budget_metering as above, and metered_usage, so a budget stop shows what it counted next to the Episode's output_tokens); and uses_tool_result with uses_tool_result_reason. The token figures are Inspect's model_usage for the sample, read through the public log API and summed over the models of the sample as ModelUsage adds them; the sample's model_usage is also kept per model, keyed by model name, as the log gives it. They include the provider's cache reads and writes: input_tokens alone is only the input charged at full rate, and total_tokens counts the cached input tokens too, so it is the figure an all-token limit is checked against. The record copies what the log gives. A field Inspect leaves None (the cache fields, when a provider reports none) is null, and an Episode absent from the log has every figure null; but Inspect itself writes 0 for input_tokens, output_tokens and total_tokens when a provider omits them, so a 0 there may not be the provider's count. These are token counts, not a cost. uses_tool_result is true when every row of a readable final answer is a row the tool returned, and false when a row is not; its Reason is then null. A readable answer is exactly one JSON object, as parse_answer decides. In every other case the value is null, never false, and uses_tool_result_reason names the cause. The cause is one of:

ReasonWhen
no_final_answerThe Episode has no final answer.
no_tool_resultThere is a final answer but no tool result to compare it with.
answer_unreadableThe final answer is not exactly one JSON object, for example prose before the JSON object, a JSON value that is not an object, or an object holding a number that decodes to a non-finite float such as 1e999.

The table below says where requested_limits comes from, for each value of request_source.

request_sourceThe requested_limits are
this_attemptthe request of the execution that wrote the record: a spike live execution, or a run agent --mode llm_live Attempt (chapter 13); the value is a format value, and a spike live execution is not an Attempt, since it records nothing in a Study
kept_logthe request a kept log holds, when live.summarize reads it again

Where each field of run.json comes from, in a record the execution writes right after it (spike live) and in a re-summary of a kept log (live.summarize): a field the log holds is the log's in both, read through Inspect's read_eval_log, so a re-summary run on another machine, with another installed Inspect and other arguments, gives the same facts of the execution as the log. A re-summary reads the log only: it calls no model and no tool, takes no model, Cases or Repeats from its caller (who may still pass them; they are ignored), and its output is still scanned for the key. What no log holds, because the pinned Inspect's EvalSpec has no interpreter or platform field, describes the execution only in a record the execution itself writes; in a re-summary it is null at its place, and the summarising machine's values, with the installed Inspect version, sit under the key summarised_by, which names them as the re-summary's. The kept run.json files are dated observations and are not rewritten; a re-summary of their logs gives the logs' values (a test pins them for one kept log).

inspect_logthe codethe codethe path of the log under the output directory

episodesthe Inspect logthe Inspect logeach expected Episode projected from the log; one the log lacks is missing
tool_loop_observedthe Inspect logthe Inspect logderived from the projected Episodes

In a re-summary, summarised_by also holds the statement about Inspect's provider code (reasoning_note) and the database hashes the caller gives (database_sha256_given_by_caller), which no log holds either. The Episodes of a log whose header names no plan (no sample IDs or no epochs) are the ones it holds, and its cases is null. The model licence line of the specification's §3 does not apply to a remote provider: the record has no field for it.

projection.json gives each model turn a stop_reason (stop, tool_calls, max_tokens, ...), so an answer cut by the output cap is told apart from a complete one. That is a record only: live Episodes are not scored against References here. The record also reports whether the tool loop was observed: a produced Episode with at least one successful query_readonly call between model turns, then a final answer. describe_schema calls and further queries may come anywhere in it.

The live Episodes

Two Episodes per Configuration were kept on anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5, on Cases D03 and D05, one Repeat each, with reasoning sent as {"type": "between_tools"} and the 1,024-token output cap: plain (projection) and verify (projection), each with its Inspect log under inspect/. In both, tool_loop_observed is true and the Chinook file has the same SHA-256 before and after. In both Configurations D05 (a join of Artist and Album) took three model turns (model_calls: 3): turn 1 called describe_schema, turn 2 called query_readonly, turn 3 gave the final answer; it is produced. In both, D03 is failed with the Reason token_limit (limit 12000, type token; both directories were made under the earlier 12,000 all-token budget, which #50 replaced by the output-token budget, so "limit 12000" stays true of them only) after four model calls: describe_schema, a query_readonly refused with DENIED_ACTION ("not authorized to use function: LIKE"; this refusal predates #51, which now allows LIKE, and the trace is a dated observation that is not regenerated), then a query_readonly that succeeded and returned 25 rows; there is no final answer. These Episodes are not scored against References here (#49). The kept run.json files are dated observations and were not regenerated: they still say "uses_tool_result": false for D05, written before the field could be null. D05's final answer has prose before the JSON object, so summarising the kept log offline now gives null with the Reason answer_unreadable.

These two traces were written before the record kept the cache figures: their run.json still has only input_tokens and output_tokens for each Episode, and they are dated observations that are not regenerated. Their kept run.json files record the 12,000 all-token budget under the earlier key total_tokens, in both requested_limits and effective_limits; a re-summary of their logs gives it under the key of the all-token row of the table of budget keys. Summarising their Inspect logs offline now also gives the five figures. D03 (the Episode stopped at the earlier 12,000 all-token limit) has input_tokens 10, output_tokens 557, input_tokens_cache_write 5,234, input_tokens_cache_read 9,935 and total_tokens 15,736 (10 + 557 + 5,234 + 9,935); D05 has 8, 406, 3,869, 6,204 and 10,487. A test pins these from the kept plain log.

Four further Episodes were kept on 2026-11-07, two per Configuration, on anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5, on Cases D03 and D05, one Repeat each, under the limits their run.json records (the table of current limits is not a record of them): plain (projection, Inspect log) and verify (projection, Inspect log). All four Episodes are produced, each in three model calls: describe_schema, one query_readonly, then the final answer. The D03 query of both Configurations uses LIKE and ran without a refusal, as the authorizer now allows it (#51); the earlier Episodes above were refused on it. run.json records these output and total tokens per Episode, the total counting the cached tokens: plain D03 361 and 10,274, plain D05 400 and 10,501, verify D03 384 and 10,481, verify D05 393 and 10,631. In all four Episodes uses_tool_result is null with the Reason answer_unreadable, because each answer starts with prose before the JSON object, and the verify answer to D03 also wraps the object in a ```json fence (filed as #58). These are dated observations of these four Episodes; the run record does not score them against References (#49), and they say nothing about main once the code changes. Scored offline by the Scorer of version 2 with their References and projected steps, each of the four gives exact_table and evidence_link_valid true and format_valid and contract_success false (wrapped answers; a test pins it).

Where the decisions are

ADRWhat it explains here

0001Why the declaration code imports no Inspect: only the runner, the tools and the projection do.
0003Why this chapter is updated in the pull request that changes the behaviour.
04

Core

Study and Attempts

A Study is a folder that holds one Protocol and the Attempts run under it. init creates the Study of the bike Workload; run ml runs one Configuration on one Partition and records it as an Attempt: a manifest, an Observation set and the predictions, every artifact referenced by its digest and published atomically before the terminal manifest. A repeated --request-id returns the recorded Attempt or is refused. A Study admits one writer at a time (init, run ml, import ml, recover, assess and compare take its lock); status shows its local state and recover records an Attempt left behind by a dead writer as interrupted. This chapter describes what exists; importing outputs produced elsewhere is chapter 06; scoring an Attempt is chapter 05; comparing two Assessments is chapter 07.

Commands

The four commands are in src/hypotrail/cli.py. With --json, stdout holds exactly one JSON object ({"ok": true, ...} or {"ok": false, "error": {"code", "message", "stage", "details"}}) and the human diagnostic goes to stderr; no error carries a secret (an unexpected exception is reported by its type only, and so is an OS error of run agent; the other commands report an OS error by its type and message). Without --json, stdout holds one line.

  • init verifies the Workload's data against its lock, then creates the Study: the StudySpec (study.json), a copy of the dataset, and the population (memberships) and the References set as Study artifacts, each with an ID. The Workload must be of the temporal regression Track or of the tool-using agent Track; a Study of the latter (Chinook) holds the database, the Case memberships, the References set and the answer schema, and its primary metric is the Criterion the Workload declares (chapter 08). A Study folder that already holds a study.json is refused. init --study DIR --new-revision adds a revision to an existing Study when its Protocol changes, for example a corrected References set (chapter 10); it takes no --workload. A changed Case text changes the Protocol too: a Study created before the change takes it with init --study DIR --new-revision.
  • run ml runs one declared Configuration (seasonal_7, ridge_v1) on one declared Partition and records one Attempt. --request-id is required. Its output is {"ok": true, "replayed": false, "attempt": MANIFEST, "observation_set_id": ID}; replayed is true when the request was already recorded and nothing was computed. observation_set_id is the ID of the Observation set the Attempt recorded, the one assess --observations takes, and is null when its manifest names none (a failed or interrupted Attempt returned for a request sent again); without --json, the line ends with ; Observation set ID or ; no Observation set. It needs the ml extra only to run ridge_v1.
  • status only reads. It lists every Attempt of the Study with its technical_status and the artifacts found in its folder, the partial ones of an Attempt that never reached its terminal manifest included, and the Study's Write sequence (see The Write sequence below). It takes no lock and writes nothing.
  • recover is the explicit command that writes. It records as interrupted, with finished_at and a Reason, an Attempt that is running while no writer is alive, and keeps its artifacts. It resubmits nothing.

The Workload stays where it is declared (examples/<name> of a checkout, or the folder given by --examples-dir: see below): a Configuration can change after the Study exists, and an Attempt records the Configuration it ran. The Study freezes the Protocol, not the Configurations.

CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
init--study DIR--workload NAME (to create a Study; not with --new-revision), --new-revision (to add a revision), --examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N (with --new-revision only), --json
run ml--study DIR, --config NAME, --partition NAME, --request-id ID--retry-of ATTEMPT_ID, --examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json
run agent--study DIR, --mode scripted_fixture|llm_live, --config NAME, --request-id ID--partition NAME, --repeats N, --model MODEL, --cases CASE_ID, and --episode-cost-limit-usd USD with --price-list FILE (llm_live only; chapter 13), --retry-of ATTEMPT_ID, --examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json
import ml--study DIR, --source CSV, --mapping MAPPING, --population FILE, --request-id ID--examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json
import agent--study DIR, --source JSONL, --population FILE, --request-id ID--examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json
