status--study DIR--since N, --json
recover--study DIR, --attempt ATTEMPT_ID--read-sequence N, --json

Where the Workloads are found

One resolver, resolve_examples_dir in src/hypotrail/core/data.py, serves every command that reads a Workload (D-016 on chadlis/hypotrail#75). No Workload file is packaged in the wheel, which holds only src/hypotrail, and no environment variable names the directory. The supported install is a checkout with uv sync: its examples/ is found without a flag. Any other install, a wheel in another environment for example, passes --examples-dir DIR naming a checkout's examples/ or a copy of it.

OrderInputUsed when
1--examples-dir DIRWhenever it is given, whatever else exists.
2the checkout's examples/, next to src/Only if that directory exists and holds at least one Workload lock (data-sources.lock.json).

With neither, the command is refused before it writes anything, with the coded error below, exit 2, and the message says to give --examples-dir. The command is never pointed at a directory that does not exist. spike live takes --examples-dir like the others (its Workload is Chinook). A command that reads the Workloads only for some of its work, assess and compare (for public_check Items, chapter 12), resolves them at that point, so on dev Items it needs no Workload.

The Study on disk

Everything in a Study is a file under the folder given by --study. A path a record holds is relative to that folder.

write_journal/SEQ.jsonevery command that writes, twice (SEQ is the entry's position, six digits, from 000001)One entry of the write journal, appended and never rewritten. Its count is the Study's Write sequence. A Study written before the journal holds none and shows 0. See The Write sequence.

attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observation_set.jsonrun mlThe Observation set record. Exactly one per Attempt.

The records

Every record is canonical JSON (core/canonical.py): UTF-8, keys sorted, separators , and :, no whitespace, strict types. NaN and Infinity are refused, never written (NON_CANONICAL_VALUE), and an integer and a float of the same value stay distinct. A configuration declared with its keys in another order has the same digest; the source files are hashed on their bytes, separately. Each record is validated against the contract schema (src/hypotrail/core/contracts.schema.json, by jsonschema) before it is written and after it is read; a fault is CONTRACT_VIOLATION.

The schema is derived from the kit's contracts.schema.json and owned here; the kit's copy is not changed. It keeps every field of the kit's envelopes, names the Criterion states scored / unscored / errored and their counters unscored / errored, and defines the fields and the two further record kinds (freeze, exposure) that the next tickets of the slice name. Only the records below are written today.

Attempt manifest. attempt_id is a UUID, never a hash of the inputs: two requests of identical inputs under two request_ids are two Attempts with the same effective_config_digest. effective_config holds the Command, the Workload, the Mode, the Partition, the Configuration (its id and its declared content), the Protocol (id and digest), the input digests (dataset, population, References), the effective parameters (null while running), and the applicable bounds. code_environment holds the Python version, the dependency lock (uv.lock, by digest) and the code revision: the commit, whether the code is dirty and, when it is, a fingerprint of the local code. "The code" is src/, pyproject.toml and uv.lock. No provider secret is written: the process environment is never read into a record. A manifest that is running has no finished_at; a completed one has it, the counts of its Items by execution status (item_counts) and its output_refs.

Observation set. One per Attempt. Its Items are exactly the Items of the Partition (read from the Study's memberships), each produced, failed or missing. population_ref is the memberships artifact, raw_refs the library's own files, normalized_ref observations.csv.

RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
study.json (StudySpec)kind, schema_version, study_id, revision, question, domain, objective, protocol_id, protocol_digest, dataset_refs, primary_metric (id, direction, unit), limitations, workload, artifacts (artifact_id, ref; version optional)revision_number, previous_revision (chapter 10), check_informed (a revision created after the Study's first Exposure, chapter 12)
Attempt manifestkind, schema_version, attempt_id, request_id, operation, mode, technical_status, started_at, finished_at, effective_config, effective_config_digest, input_refs, code_environment, outcome_unknown, retry_ofitem_counts and output_refs (required once completed), reason, check_informed, case_digests (an llm_live Attempt: the digest of each evaluated Case, its whole canonical object, by Case ID; outside effective_config, so not part of the request's identity)
Observation setkind, schema_version, observation_set_id, attempt_id, domain, origin, mode, population_ref, raw_refs, normalized_ref, provenance, unknown_fieldsnone
ArtifactRefpath, sha256, size_bytes, media_type, rolenone
LevelValues
Attempt (technical_status)running, completed, failed, interrupted. run ml writes running, then completed, or failed when an error stops it inside the live process; recover writes interrupted. Every non-completed terminal state has a Reason. A terminal manifest has finished_at; a running one does not.
Item (execution status)produced, failed, missing. Separate from how an Item is scored. A native Attempt of a declared Configuration produces every Item.
RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
ItemCounts (item_counts of the manifest)produced, failed, missing: the numbers of Items in each execution status, each a non-negative integernone

Artifacts and integrity

An ArtifactRef is {path, sha256, size_bytes, media_type, role}: the path is relative to the Study and the digest is of the bytes. A path that leaves the Study (.., an absolute path) or goes through a symlink pointing outside it is refused (ARTIFACT_PATH_OUTSIDE_STUDY), for writing and for reading; a symlink that stays inside is not a traversal.

Publication is atomic (core/artifacts.py): the bytes go to a temporary file in the destination's folder, inside the Study, are flushed and fsynced, then renamed onto the final path; nothing appears under the final path before the rename. A published artifact is immutable: other bytes under the same path are refused (ARTIFACT_IMMUTABLE). When a referenced artifact's bytes no longer match its digest, or the file is gone, reading it back is an integrity refusal (ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY, ARTIFACT_MISSING; exit 3), raised before any computation: run ml reads back the dataset, the memberships and the References of the Study before it computes, and an Attempt it replays.

The canonical JSON and the atomic write were copied from hypotrail/hypotrail at commit 4ef4925 (framework/mlkit/study/understanding/canonical.py, framework/mlkit/study/store.py) and are owned here; nothing is imported from that project.

The Protocol

The StudySpec's protocol_digest is the canonical digest of what binds every Attempt: the Partitions and their ranges, the population and the References by their bytes, the data file by its bytes, the columns, the rules on when information is available (period_k, the forbidden and the allowed target features) and the primary metric. It changes when a Partition range or the References change. It does not change when a Configuration changes, nor with the key order of the declaration. run ml recomputes it from the Workload for a new request and refuses a Workload whose Protocol is no longer the Study's (PROTOCOL_CHANGED, exit 3); a Protocol that changed on purpose is a new revision of the Study (chapter 10).

The question, the objective and the limitations of the StudySpec are declared by the Workload (question, objective, limitations in workload.json, read by init only). The primary metric is mae, lower_is_better, in the unit the Workload declares. study_id is a UUID; revision is the digest of the StudySpec without its study_id and itself. An artifact ID is its kind and the first 16 hex digits of its digest (references-<digest>), so a corrected References set has another ID.

Request identity

--request-id is required for run, for import (chapter 06) and for decide (chapter 17), and must not be empty. A request is identified by the elements below, read from the Attempt manifest itself. The same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Attempt, whatever its status, after reading its artifacts back against their digests: no fit and no prediction happens, and nothing is written. With any element different it is REQUEST_CONFLICT (exit 6), naming the elements that differ, and nothing is written. A wanted rerun uses a new request_id and --retry-of ATTEMPT_ID; the earlier Attempt and its artifacts keep their digests.

A Study has one request_id namespace, for its Attempts, its Decisions and its Declared deviations. A request_id that names a record of another kind is REQUEST_CONFLICT: a Decision or a Declared deviation for run and import, an Attempt or a Declared deviation for decide, an Attempt or a Decision for deviation. In each, differs is ["command"] and details carry attempt_id, decision_id and declared_deviation_id, those that do not apply set to null. Two Decisions under one request_id that differ name, in differs, the fields of the Decision record that differ (chapter 17).

Changing ridge.alpha from 1.0 to 10.0 under the name ridge_v1, or changing the dataset, or running another commit, is therefore a conflict under the same request_id; under a new one it is a new Attempt (a changed dataset is then refused as a Protocol change). A different dirty state or fingerprint of the local code with the same commit is a conflict too.

ElementIn the identityTaken from
commandyeseffective_config.command (run ml)
workloadyeseffective_config.workload
configurationyesThe id and the declared content, in canonical form: the key order of the file does not matter.
partitionyeseffective_config.partition
casesyeseffective_config.cases: the Cases of the Partition a run agent Attempt runs, as a list; absent from a native ML request and from an import.
modeyeseffective_config.mode (native_ml)
modelyeseffective_config.model: absent from a native ML request; an import holds the model its mapping declares or unknown.

the Python versionnoRecorded in code_environment, not part of the request.
retry_ofnoSays why a request exists, not what it asks.

One writer, interruption and retry

The lock. Every command that writes to a Study (report only when its page records an Exposure) holds an exclusive, non-blocking lock on writer.lock (core/lock.py) for the whole command. The steps that all writers share, in the order each writer keeps (the argument check, the lock, the write journal, the replay of a request identity, the stale-sequence check), are written once, in core/study_write.py. A second writer does not wait: it is refused at once with STUDY_LOCKED, exit 6, and writes nothing. The operating system drops the lock when its holder ends, killed or not, so a dead writer never blocks the Study. status takes no lock; it probes whether one is held. The lock needs fcntl (Linux, macOS); elsewhere the writers are refused with LOCKING_UNAVAILABLE (exit 5). The lock mechanism was adapted from framework/mlkit/study/lock.py of hypotrail/hypotrail at commit 4ef4925 and is owned here.

What an interruption leaves. The manifest is written running before anything else, each artifact is published before the terminal manifest, and the terminal manifest replaces the running one atomically, last. A process killed in between leaves the artifacts it had published, no artifact under a final path it had not reached, and a manifest that is still running, without finished_at. No complete state is invented. An error that is raised inside a live process instead leaves the Attempt failed, with a Reason and finished_at, its artifacts kept, and the command exits 4.

Looking and recovering. status shows such an Attempt as it is: running, with recoverable: true when no writer is alive. recover --attempt ID records it as interrupted. It refuses an Attempt that is already terminal (ATTEMPT_TERMINAL, exit 3) and, while a writer is alive, the whole command (STUDY_LOCKED). An Attempt whose Mode calls a provider (llm_live) is recorded with outcome_unknown: true: a lock that is gone is never read as the cancellation of a remote call.

Retry. A retry is never implicit and nothing is resubmitted. The same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Attempt, whether it is running, interrupted or failed, with neither a fit nor a prediction. To run it again, use a new --request-id and --retry-of ATTEMPT_ID: it is a new Attempt, and the earlier one and its artifacts keep their digests.

Immutability. Once an Attempt is no longer running, a write that would change its manifest or its outputs, or add a file to its folder, is refused with ATTEMPT_TERMINAL.

Field of an Attempt in statusMeaning
attempt_idThe Attempt.
request_idThe request that created it.
operationFrom the manifest.
modeFrom the manifest.
technical_statusrunning, completed, failed or interrupted, as recorded. Never inferred.
started_atFrom the manifest.
finished_atNull while running.
outcome_unknownFrom the manifest.
retry_ofThe earlier Attempt this one is made after, on purpose, or null.
reasonWhy a failed or interrupted Attempt is not completed, or null.
item_countsThe Items by execution status of a completed Attempt, or null.
writer_aliveTrue for a running Attempt while a writer holds the Study.
recoverableTrue for a running Attempt while no writer holds the Study: recover can record it.

The Write sequence

A Study keeps a Write sequence: a value that every write to the Study advances and that nothing else changes. It is the number of entries of an append-only journal, write_journal/SEQ.json (core/write_journal.py, opened and appended to by core/study_write.py); an entry is never rewritten. status --json shows it as the top-level key write_sequence, and no other command's output shows it, but the details of the two refusals of --read-sequence (below). It is not a revision of the Study (chapter 10): a revision changes the Protocol, a write only advances the sequence.

Two entries per writing command. A command that writes appends a begin entry just before its first write and an end entry after its last. A writer killed between its first write and its last has still written: it leaves its begin entry only (a run ml killed after its running manifest leaves the sequence one higher), and the recover that follows adds two. A command that writes and then stops appends its end entry too: a run ml that leaves a failed Attempt, and a compare refused for its coverage after it appended its Exposure entry (chapter 12).

What a command that writes nothing leaves. status, a replay (the same request_id with the same request), a refusal before the first write and a report whose page shows dev Items only leave the sequence as it was, and append no entry.

What an entry says. request_id is null for a command without one (init, assess, compare, recover, report). The outcome of an end entry is completed (the command wrote what it set out to write), failed or refused. For run and import, any error after the running manifest is failed, whatever its exit code, since it leaves a failed Attempt. For a command with no Attempt, an error after its first write is failed when it exits 4 and refused when it is a coded error with another exit. outcome may gain values: a reader does not treat that list as closed.

Reading the sequence before a write: --read-sequence N. A command that writes to a Study takes, as an option, the Write sequence its author read (the write_sequence of status --json); the command table below says which do, and whether it is required. A command for which the table makes the option optional, given none, behaves as it did before the option existed; it appends its journal entries all the same. Given and equal to the Study's, the command does what it does without the option. The table of refusals below gives what any other value gets. report compares the value only when its page records an Exposure; a page of dev Items only is written as without the option.

The value is compared under the writer lock, so a write made by another process between the author's read and the command is caught. The refusals come in this order: the arguments (the read sequence value is the library's first argument check); the Study; the lock; the request identity (a replay of the same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Attempt, or the recorded Decision for decide, and writes nothing, whatever value was read; another request is a conflict); the comparison of the value; then the command's other refusals. The table of checks before the lock below lists the checks that come before the lock beyond this general order, those that read more than the arguments. Such a check comes before the comparison of the value, as before this version; compare --factor and report --out are among them. An older value given with one of them failing gets that refusal, not the one for the value. In run ml, import ml and import agent the refusals that guard the reads the identity needs keep their place before the identity; in run agent, whose identity reads its input bytes directly, only the data check and the artifact checks come after the comparison (the table of refusals below gives their codes, and chapter 13 the order of that command); the others keep their place, before the lock or before the identity. An older value with corrupt data is therefore refused for the value in run agent and for its data in run ml; neither writes anything.

A Study written before this version holds no journal and shows write_sequence 0 until its first write. Its files are never rewritten.

Reading status --json. The table below lists the top-level keys of the object. A later version may add keys to it, or to an entry of a list it holds, so a client should ignore a key it does not know rather than refuse the object. status --since N gives what was written since a Write sequence: a client that read the Study at Write sequence N asks what was written after it. The value is judged as the value of --read-sequence is, before the Study is read; one above the Study's is refused as a write's is (the table of refusals below), and 0 is answered. The answer is the whole object of status --json with one key more, the last row of the table below, whose entries have the shape of the detail that names them in the refusal of a write made on an older Write sequence, a killed writer's begin entry included. The entries and the write_sequence of one answer come from one listing of the journal, so a writer that appends while it reads cannot make them disagree. It stays a read: it takes no lock, writes nothing and records no Exposure. Without --json, the lines of status are followed by the count of those entries and, for each, its position, its phase and its command:

uv run python -m hypotrail status --study runs/bike --since 10
Study 5d85c1c9-817c-4038-8fc8-7016825a971b (bike_day); a writer is not active
  0 Exposures recorded in this Study; any earlier exposure to public_check is unknown
  38dbe611-f5c4-4a46-8108-5f7749560cb6 completed request ml-dev-ridge_v1, 5 artifacts
  c5edaa37-ba8e-4936-8999-1ce78f1b1146 completed request ml-dev-seasonal_7, 4 artifacts
2 write-journal entries after 10, up to 12:
  11 begin compare
  12 end compare (completed)
Top-level key of status --jsonMeaning
studyThe Study's identity and its current revision (chapter 10).
writer_aliveTrue while a writer holds the Study's lock.
revisionsEvery revision of the Study (chapter 10).
attemptsEvery Attempt, with the fields of the table above.

check_informedEvery record that carries the mark (chapter 12).
write_sequenceThe number of entries of the write journal: 0 for a Study written before it, 2 for a new Study, 2 more for every command that writes, and 1 more for a writer killed between its first and its last write.
decisionsThe Study's Decisions, in the order they were recorded, each with whether it still applies (chapter 17).
declared_deviationsThe Study's Declared deviations, withdrawals included, in the order they were recorded, each with whether it is withdrawn (chapter 17).

comparisonsEvery Comparison record the Study holds, each entry equal to the stored record, its roles included when the record holds them, in the order of the first write-journal entry that names each; a Comparison that no entry names comes first, and ties are broken by its id. [] when the Study holds none.
pending_decisionsThe Comparisons that wait for a decision, in the order of comparisons, each with the question and the options the core supplies (chapter 17). Derived when status reads the Study, never stored; [] when none waits.
record_filesThe file of the Study that holds each record of decisions, declared_deviations, assessments and comparisons, in that order and in each listing's order: one entry per record, with exactly kind (decision, declared_deviation, assessment or comparison), id (the record's id) and path (relative to the Study's folder, written with /). Derived when status reads the Study, never stored; [] when the Study holds none of these records.
writes_sincePresent only with --since N: the write-journal entries after position N, in journal order, each as stored (begin or end). [] when N is the Write sequence.

Exit codes and refusals

ExitMeaningName in the code
0The operation ran and its artifacts are valid, even if the model is poor.EXIT_OK
2Configuration, schema or input invalid.EXIT_INVALID_INPUT
3Integrity or comparability refused.EXIT_INTEGRITY_REFUSED
4Infrastructure failure (an OS error, an unexpected exception).EXIT_INFRASTRUCTURE
5A required capability is unavailable (a live model that is not configured).EXIT_CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE
6Lock held, a request_id conflict, or the Study changed since the Write sequence the command was given.EXIT_LOCK_OR_CONFLICT

The item counts stay in the manifest and are not summarised by the exit code. Each refusal below has the stage and the details keys shown; the data refusals of init and run ml that come from the Workload (DATA_INTEGRITY and the others of data verify) and from the declaration (chapter 02) keep their own codes.

How to run it

# from the repository root; the ml extra is needed for ridge_v1
uv run python -m hypotrail init --workload bike_day --study runs/bike
uv run python -m hypotrail run ml --study runs/bike --config seasonal_7 --partition dev --request-id ml-dev-a
uv run python -m hypotrail run ml --study runs/bike --config ridge_v1 --partition dev --request-id ml-dev-b --json
# the same request_id and request again: the recorded Attempt, nothing recomputed
uv run python -m hypotrail run ml --study runs/bike --config ridge_v1 --partition dev --request-id ml-dev-b --json
# after a writer was killed: look, record the Attempt as interrupted, then repeat it on purpose
uv run python -m hypotrail status --study runs/bike --json
uv run python -m hypotrail recover --study runs/bike --attempt ATTEMPT_ID
uv run python -m hypotrail run ml --study runs/bike --config ridge_v1 --partition dev --request-id ml-dev-c --retry-of ATTEMPT_ID

The tests are tests/test_canonical_json.py, test_artifacts.py, test_contracts.py, test_study_init.py, test_run_ml.py and test_study_lock.py (the lock, the killed writer, status, recover, the retry); like the rest of the suite they run offline and call no model. run ml may be run on any declared Partition, public_check included: there is no freeze record or Exposure journal yet.

Limits

No resume engine, POSIX locking

An interrupted or failed Attempt is never continued: a retry is a new Attempt (a generic resume engine is out of scope). The lock needs fcntl; on Windows the writers are refused. A status probe holds a shared lock for an instant, so a writer that starts at that very moment is refused (it writes nothing) and can simply be run again. recover leaves the unpublished scratch files of a dead writer in .scratch/.

No scoring, no Comparison

run ml does not score: assess does, in a separate step (chapter 05), and compare compares two Assessments in another (chapter 07). An import is a separate command (chapter 06).

No freeze at run time

A run ml on public_check is recorded like any other and is not an Exposure: it scores nothing, so it writes no freeze record and no journal entry. The Exposure is the Assessment or the Comparison (chapter 12). After the Study's first Exposure, a run ml of a Configuration the freeze record does not hold is not refused: its Attempt is marked check_informed.

Two Tracks, one native each

run ml serves the temporal regression Track only. A Chinook Study (init) takes answers produced elsewhere with import agent (chapter 08) and records its own native Attempts with run agent (chapter 13), which follows the order, the request identity, the lock and the manifest described here.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the Study, Attempt and Observation set records are core contracts shared by both Tracks, and the core imports neither Inspect nor scikit-learn.
0002Why the question, the objective and the limitations of a Study are declared by the Workload.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.
0004Why a write to a Study takes the Write sequence its author read, why a write on an older Write sequence is refused with STUDY_CHANGED and nothing is written, and why the Write sequence is a count of writes rather than a digest of status --json or the Protocol revision.

Failures no command refuses

These two codes are not refusals of an input. A command ends with exit 4 when an operating-system error or any other exception reaches it, and reports the exception by its type.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILUREAn operating-system error ends a command (a full disk, a file that cannot be read). run agent reports it by its type alone, any other command by its type and message.4infrastructuretype
UNEXPECTED_ERRORAny other exception ends a command. It is reported by its type only, because the text of a foreign exception can carry anything, a provider key included.4infrastructuretype
05

Core

Assessments and Scorers

assess applies one registered Scorer version to one kept Observation set and one References set of a Study, and writes a new Assessment. It fits nothing, computes no Configuration's prediction and calls no model or tool: it reads files the Study already keeps, and computes from the Study's copy of the dataset the forecast of each lag feature the Configuration declares, used alone (the single-feature check, below). Every expected Item gets a Criterion state, with a Reason for every non-score, and rescoring never changes an earlier Assessment: it writes another one. This chapter describes what exists (the temporal regression Track, on dev); the agent Scorer chinook_contract_v1 is in the registry too, and chapter 08 describes what it writes; the report is later work (see Limits).

The command

assess is in src/hypotrail/cli.py; the work is in src/hypotrail/ml/assess.py and the registry in src/hypotrail/core/scorers.py. It follows the rules of chapter 04: with --json, stdout holds one JSON object, {"ok": true, "assessment": RECORD} or {"ok": false, "error": {"code", "message", "stage", "details"}}, and the diagnostic goes to stderr; without it, stdout holds one line. The three ids are the ones the Study keeps: the observation_set_id of an Observation set record (run ml prints it in attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observation_set.json), a Scorer id from the registry, and the artifact_id of the References set in study.json (references- and 16 hex digits).

CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
assess--study DIR, --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID, --scorer SCORER_ID, --references REFERENCES_ID--examples-dir (read only for public_check Items, chapter 12), --read-sequence N, --json
# the bike Study of chapter 04, after `run ml` on dev; ids come from the Study's files
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/bike --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer regression_mae_v1 --references references-0123456789abcdef
# the same predictions, scored again: a second Assessment, the first one untouched
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/bike --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer regression_rmse_v1 --references references-0123456789abcdef --json

The Scorer registry

The registry is a static, closed table in the code. A --scorer value is a key of that table and nothing else: it is never imported, evaluated or looked up by name, so a string such as package.module:function loads no Python and is refused as a Scorer that is not registered. A Scorer belongs to one Track (its domain); one applied to an Observation set of another Track is refused. chinook_contract_v1 is the Scorer of the agent Track (version 3); it has four Criteria and no Metric of its own, and its Assessment is described in chapter 08.

Both Scorers have the same Criterion, the absolute error of the day, and differ in the Metric they aggregate from it: mae, the mean of the absolute errors, and rmse, the square root of the mean of their squares. The Metric's unit is the one the StudySpec declares for the primary metric; its direction is lower_is_better. The config_digest is the digest of the Scorer's configuration (the Criterion, the Metric and the direction): another Metric is another digest.

Scorer idVersionTrackCriterionMetric
regression_mae_v11temporal regressionabsolute_errormae
regression_rmse_v11temporal regressionabsolute_errorrmse
chinook_contract_v13tool-using agentexact_table, format_valid, evidence_link_valid, contract_successnone: shares of Episodes

What is checked, in order

  1. The Study exists; the Scorer is in the registry.
  2. The Observation set and the References set exist in the Study (the References set of the current revision: an earlier revision's set is refused with REFERENCES_NOT_FOUND), and the Scorer belongs to the Observation set's Track.
  3. Every artifact read is read back against its digest: the Observation set record, the normalized outputs, the raw predictions it was made from, the population and the References, and, only when the Attempt's Configuration declares a lag feature, the copy of workload.json the Attempt keeps and the Study's copy of the dataset. One that is gone or no longer matches is an integrity refusal (chapter 04, ARTIFACT_MISSING or ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY, exit 3) and nothing is written, on public_check no Exposure either.
  4. The Partition of the Items is dev or public_check; any other Partition (train) is refused. An Assessment on public_check is an Exposure: the Study's freeze record is written if the revision has none and one journal entry is appended before any Item is scored (chapter 12). Running predictions on public_check with run ml is not an Exposure.
  5. The Items of the Observation set must be Items of that Partition, each once.
  6. Each expected Item (for a native Attempt, every Item of the Study's memberships in the Partition; for an Import, every Item of the population file it was given, which may list fewer; whatever the outputs hold) gets its Criterion; the Metric is aggregated from the scored ones. per_item.csv is published, then assessment.json, last.

Criterion states

A Criterion is scored (a number), unscored (the Scorer ran and had no usable reading) or errored (the scoring machinery broke). Neither of the last two is held against the evaluated system, and a null value is never zero: it is left out of the Metric and counted. The execution status of the Item (produced, failed, missing) stays separate, in its own column. An expected Item that the outputs do not hold at all is missing.

ReasonStateWhen
item_failedunscoredThe Item's execution status is failed: there is no output to score.
item_missingunscoredThe Item's execution status is missing, or the outputs hold no row for it.
reference_missingunscoredThe References set holds no row for the Item.
reference_invalidunscoredThe Reference is not a finite number.
prediction_invalidunscoredThe Item is produced but its prediction is not a finite number.
scoring_failederroredBoth numbers are finite but their difference is not (an overflow).

The Assessment record

assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/assessment.json is canonical JSON, validated against the contract schema before it is written and when it is read. assessment_id is a UUID, new each time. counts holds one counter per key of the schema, named for the key (the specification's N_expected is expected). attempted is the Items that were produced or failed (a missing Item was never attempted). For a numeric loss, correct and incorrect are null: an absolute error is not a binary verdict; the other counters are filled. scored + unscored + errored = expected. The Metric has the number of scored Items as its denominator; with none, its value is null with the Reason no_scored_item.

RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
Assessmentkind, schema_version, assessment_id, observation_set_id, references_ref, scorer (id, version, config_digest), counts, per_item_ref, metrics, limitationsstudy_revision (the revision whose Protocol the Assessment applied), recorded_at, rescoring (chapter 10; null when no earlier Assessment differs); check_informed (written when a Configuration the first freeze record does not hold marks it, chapter 12); primary_criterion, unknowns, operational_errors, detailed_metrics_ref (written by an agent Assessment only, chapter 08); single_feature_check (computed, each with feature and metric, and beating, each with feature and value; written for a native regression Attempt, below)
Countsexpected, attempted, produced, scored, correct, incorrect, unscored, errorednone
Metricid, value, unit, direction, denominator, status, reasonnone

The single-feature check

A regression Assessment of a native Attempt also scores each lag feature its Configuration declares, used alone as the forecast: the forecast of an Item is the feature's value at the Item's date, computed from the Study's copy of the dataset with the date and target columns of the workload.json the Attempt keeps, never from the Workload under --examples-dir. Nothing is fitted. Only the declared features that the kept declaration allows and whose kind is a value of the target (lag, mean_previous) are computed: a calendar column never is, and neither is an allowed feature the Configuration does not declare. Each feature is scored by the Assessment's Scorer over the Items the Assessment scored, and beating lists every one whose Metric is strictly lower than the Assessment's, best first. It is a flag, never a refusal: the Metric, the counts and per_item.csv are those the Assessment has without it, and compare names the flag in a warning (chapter 07).

single_feature_check has three states. A Configuration that declares no lag feature, such as seasonal_7, gets it with both lists empty: checked, nothing to compare. An Import declares no Configuration and gets no field: not checked. Neither does an Assessment one of whose features needs a date the series does not hold for some scored Item (LAG_SOURCE_MISSING): no feature is compared on fewer Items than the Assessment scored, so the field is left out, the command still exits 0, and one line on stderr names the cause. The dataset copy is read back against its digest before an Exposure is recorded and parsed only after it, and the series it gives keeps only the dates before the last scored Item's, so an Assessment of dev reads no value of public_check.

PathWritten byContent
assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/per_item.csvassess, firstOne row per expected Item: row_id, execution_status, criterion, state, value (empty unless scored), reason (empty when scored).
assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/assessment.jsonassess, lastThe Assessment record. A folder without it is not an Assessment.

Refusals

Each refusal has the exit code, stage and details keys below. The integrity refusals (ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY, ARTIFACT_MISSING, CONTRACT_VIOLATION, exit 3), STUDY_NOT_FOUND and INVALID_ARGUMENTS are those of chapter 04. UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY is the code the specification gives to a combination the milestone does not support, with no fallback to another Track.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
SCORER_NOT_REGISTEREDA Scorer id that is not a key of the registry; nothing is imported from it2assessscorer, registered
UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITYA Scorer of another Track than the Observation set's2assessscorer, scorer_domain, observation_set_domain
OBSERVATION_SET_NOT_FOUNDThe Study keeps no Observation set with this id2assessobservation_set_id
REFERENCES_NOT_FOUNDThe StudySpec holds no References set with this id2assessreferences_id, available
OBSERVATIONS_INVALIDAn Observation set that names no Partition, repeats an Item, holds an Item outside its Partition or has an unknown execution status (problem: unknown_partition, duplicate_item, unexpected_item, bad_status)2assessproblem, row_id
ASSESSMENT_PARTITION_NOT_DEVAn Assessment of Items of a Partition other than dev and public_check (train)2assesspartition

Limits

One writer

assess holds the Study's writer lock for the whole command, as init, run ml, import ml, recover and compare do: while another writer holds it, assess is refused at once with STUDY_LOCKED (exit 6) and writes nothing (chapter 04).

Summarised by the report

Two Assessments are compared by compare (chapter 07), and report sets Assessments and Comparisons of a Study out on one page (chapter 14). The record's rescoring field says which earlier Assessment of the same Observation set the new one differs from and why (chapter 10).

Dev, and public_check with a journal

Items of public_check can be assessed because the Study journals the Exposure (chapter 12); Items of train never can. PROTOCOL_CHANGED (exit 3) refuses an Assessment of public_check Items when the Workload's Protocol is no longer the revision's.

Two Scorers' shapes

The two regression Scorers aggregate one Criterion into a Metric and leave correct and incorrect null. chinook_contract_v1 scores four Criteria per Episode and counts them: its Assessment is the subject of chapter 08.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the registry and the Assessment record are core contracts, and assess imports neither scikit-learn nor Inspect.
0002Why the Partitions have fixed roles: fit on train, measure on dev, keep public_check for the Exposure-tracked check.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.
0004Why assess takes --read-sequence and is refused with STUDY_CHANGED when the Study was written to since the sequence was read.

The tests are tests/test_assess.py, test_assess_criteria.py and test_assess_refusals.py; they run offline and call no model.

06

Core

Importing predictions made elsewhere

import ml adopts a complete CSV of daily predictions, written by a program that never imports Hypotrail, as an Import Attempt of the Study that already holds the native Attempts. The original file, the mapping and a validation report are kept. Everything the import cannot establish about how the file was made is unknown; what the mapping claims is declared, never verified. assess then scores the Import like any Observation set (chapter 05). This chapter describes what exists: the temporal regression Track (the bike Workload), complete files only.

The stand-in exporter

examples/external/bike_trailing_mean.py stands in for someone else's program (ADR 0002 makes examples/external/ the one place for Python that never imports hypotrail). It uses the standard library only. It reads the daily file, predicts every day of a window with the mean of the 28 days before the window, writes a CSV with its own column names (day_key, forecast_rentals, forecast_unrounded) and prints the sha256 of the data file it read. A test runs it in a subprocess where hypotrail cannot be imported.

The command

import ml is in src/hypotrail/cli.py; the work is in src/hypotrail/ml/imports.py. It follows the rules of chapter 04: --request-id is required, with --json stdout holds one object ({"ok": true, "replayed": BOOL, "attempt": MANIFEST, "observation_set_id": ID}, with the Observation set ID of chapter 04, or {"ok": false, "error": {"code", "message", "stage", "details"}}) and the diagnostic goes to stderr, and the command holds the Study's writer lock for its whole execution. It imports neither scikit-learn, NumPy nor Inspect, and reads no network: import ml and assess of the Import run where none of them is installed.

# a program that never imports hypotrail writes its predictions; it prints the digest of the data it read
python examples/external/bike_trailing_mean.py --data examples/bike_day/data/day.csv --first 2012-01-01 --last 2012-06-30 --id-prefix bike-day --out predictions.csv
# mapping.json: {"row_id": "day_key", "y_pred": "forecast_rentals", "dataset_sha256": "<that digest>"}
# population.json: {"item_ids": ["bike-day:2012-01-01", "bike-day:2012-01-02", "..."]}
uv run python -m hypotrail import ml --study runs/bike --source predictions.csv --mapping mapping.json --population population.json --request-id import-a
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/bike --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer regression_mae_v1 --references references-0123456789abcdef
CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
import ml--study DIR, --source CSV, --mapping MAPPING, --population FILE, --request-id ID--examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json

The mapping and the population

The mapping is a JSON object of strings. A key that is not in the table is refused, and so is a missing required key. The values of the three column keys must name columns of the source file; a value that names a callable or a module (os.system, pickle:loads, f(x), a path) is refused, and a column whose name looks like a dotted name cannot be mapped. The claim keys are free text, kept as data and never read as code. The population is {"item_ids": [...]}: the Items the file must hold, all of one Partition of the Study's Protocol. The expected population comes from this file checked against the Protocol, never from the source file or its name; the source cannot add an Item to it.

KeyRequiredMeaning
row_idyesThe column holding the Item ID. Rows are joined to the References by this ID, never by position.
y_predyesThe column holding the prediction: a finite number.
dataset_sha256yesThe sha256 of the data file the predictions were made on (64 lowercase hexadecimal digits). It must equal the Workload lock's digest of that file.
y_pred_rawnoThe column holding the prediction before post-processing. A file without it is accepted.
modelnoWhat the producer says its model is: declared.
generated_atnoWhen the producer says the predictions were made: declared, as written.
historical_protocolnoThe protocol the producer says it followed: declared.
tool_usenoWhat the producer says about its tool use: declared.

What is checked, in order

  1. The Study exists and the writer lock is free; the Workload's data is verified against its lock and the Study's artifacts are read back against their digests (chapter 04).
  2. The three files are read and their sha256 taken. The request is identified (below). The same request_id with the same request ends here with the recorded Import: the source is not validated again, and nothing is written or scored.
  3. The Protocol of the Workload is still the Study's (PROTOCOL_CHANGED, chapter 04).
  4. The mapping, then the source file (data and not a serialized object, valid UTF-8, a readable CSV that holds the mapped columns), then the population are checked. The dataset digest of the mapping is compared with the lock's.
  5. Every row of the source is joined to the population by ID: no empty ID, no repeated ID, no ID outside the population, a finite number in y_pred; every Item of the population must be present. A population smaller than its Partition is allowed: assess counts the Items of the Partition that the file does not hold as missing.
  6. Only then is anything written: the running manifest, the original files, the validation report, the normalized Observations and the Observation set, and the terminal manifest last. A refusal at steps 4 or 5 writes nothing at all. An error inside the process after the manifest is written leaves a failed Attempt with a Reason, as in chapter 04.

Nothing in an import is executed or deserialised as code. A source with the extension of a pickle, joblib or array dump, or whose first byte is the pickle marker, is refused as SOURCE_SERIALIZED before it is read as text.

What the Import keeps

The Import is an Attempt of the same Study as the native ones: its manifest has operation: import and Mode external_unknown, and its Observation set has origin: imported. The original source is copied under its own file name and referenced with its digest (role import_source), and it is the only raw output of the Observation set. The manifest's started_at and finished_at are import times; effective_config.import_time says so, and the time of generation is a separate field of effective_config.producer. The normalized Observations (observations.csv) are written in the same form as a native Attempt's, one line per Item of the population in ID order, every Item produced, so a permuted source gives the same bytes and the same measures. The Observation set's population_ref is the population file, so assess expects exactly those Items.

PathRoleWhat it holds
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/attempt.jsonmanifestThe Attempt manifest (running, then terminal).
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/source/NAMEimport_sourceThe original predictions file, byte for byte.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/mapping.jsonimport_mappingThe mapping, byte for byte.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/population.jsonimport_populationThe population file, byte for byte.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/validation_report.jsonvalidation_reportThe digests of the three inputs, the rows read, the columns used and not used, and the checks that passed.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observations.csvobservationsThe normalized Observations: row_id, status, reason, y_pred_raw, y_pred.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observation_set.jsonobservation_setThe Observation set record; its provenance and unknown_fields are below.

What is known about the producer

Each producer field is {"value": ..., "origin": ...} in effective_config.producer and in the Observation set's provenance. The origin is declared when the mapping says it and unknown when it does not; no field is verified, because a claim in an external file is not checked by importing it. unknown_fields of the Observation set lists the fields whose origin is unknown. Whether the producer saw public_check values is always unknown: no mapping key can declare it. No record states that the producer worked independently. An Import whose fields are all unknown is scored like any other (descriptively); a later Comparison against it is descriptive with a warning.

FieldDeclared byOtherwise
modelmodelunknown
generation_timegenerated_atunknown
historical_protocolhistorical_protocolunknown
tool_usetool_useunknown
saw_public_checknothingunknown, always

The request

The identity of an import holds every element of chapter 04's list, each with the import's value or unknown when the import does not establish it: the command (import ml), the Workload, the Configuration (unknown), the Partition of the population, the Mode (external_unknown), the model (the declared one, or unknown), the repeats (unknown), the digests of the dataset, the source, the mapping and the population, and the code revision (commit, dirty state, fingerprint of the local code). The same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Import: the digests are recomputed to compare the requests, and nothing is validated again, written or scored. With any element different, including a request id that names a native Attempt, it is REQUEST_CONFLICT (exit 6).

Refusals

Each refusal below has the stage and the details keys shown; the refusals of chapter 04 (REQUEST_ID_EMPTY, REQUEST_CONFLICT, STUDY_LOCKED, DATA_INTEGRITY, PROTOCOL_CHANGED, the contract and artifact refusals) apply to import ml as they do to run ml. A refusal publishes no Observation set.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
SOURCE_NOT_FOUNDThe source file cannot be read2importsource
MAPPING_NOT_FOUNDThe mapping file cannot be read2importmapping
POPULATION_NOT_FOUNDThe population file cannot be read2importpopulation
SOURCE_SERIALIZEDA pickle, joblib or array dump2importsource
SOURCE_NOT_UTF8A source that is not valid UTF-82importsource
SOURCE_UNREADABLEA source that is not a CSV with a header2importsource, problem
MAPPING_INVALIDA mapping that is not a JSON object (NaN, a repeated key or an out-of-range number included)2importmapping, problem
MAPPING_KEY_UNKNOWNA key that is not in the mapping table2importkey, allowed
MAPPING_KEY_MISSINGNo row_id, y_pred or dataset_sha2562importkey, required
MAPPING_VALUE_INVALIDA value that is not a non-empty string, or a digest that is not 64 hexadecimal digits2importkey
MAPPING_VALUE_EXECUTABLEA column value that names a callable or a module2importkey
MAPPING_COLUMN_ABSENTA mapped column that the source does not have2importkey, column
POPULATION_INVALIDA population that is not {"item_ids": [...]} of distinct non-empty IDs (NaN, a repeated key or an out-of-range number included)2importpopulation, problem
POPULATION_ITEM_UNKNOWNAn Item that is in no Partition of the Protocol2importrow_id
POPULATION_MIXED_PARTITIONSItems of more than one Partition2importpartitions
DATASET_DIGEST_MISMATCHThe mapping's dataset_sha256 differs from the Workload lock's digest3importdeclared_sha256, locked_sha256
ROW_ID_EMPTYA row with an empty ID2importline
ROW_ID_DUPLICATEAn ID that appears twice2importrow_id
ROW_ID_OUTSIDE_POPULATIONAn ID that is not an Item of the population2importrow_id
PREDICTION_NOT_FINITEA y_pred that is empty, NaN, infinite or not a number2importrow_id, column
POPULATION_ITEM_ABSENTAn Item of the population that the source does not hold2importrow_id, absent

Limits

Complete ML files only

An incomplete file is refused, never imported with the gaps marked missing. An agent import of JSONL answers is chapter 08; an import of an Inspect log is chapter 09; the migration of old Studies is another ticket. A Comparison that involves an Import is descriptive, with a warning on its scope (chapter 07); the report and the Exposure journal come later in issue #9.

Claims are not checked

The digest of the dataset is checked against the lock; the model, the date, the protocol and the tool use are only what the mapping says. A column whose name looks like a dotted name (pred.v1) cannot be mapped, because the same shape names a module.

The tests are tests/test_import_ml.py (every criterion of the ticket) and tests/test_handbook.py (the tables of this chapter against the code). They run offline and call no model.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0004Why import ml takes --read-sequence and is refused with STUDY_CHANGED when the Study was written to since the sequence was read.
07

Core

Comparison

compare takes two Assessments of a Study, one named the Control and one the Candidate, and writes a Comparison record into the Study. It reads what the Study keeps (the Assessments, their per-Item files, the Observation sets and Attempt manifests they point to) and nothing else: it fits nothing, predicts nothing and calls no model. A Comparison is strict when everything that makes two Assessments comparable holds, and descriptive when one side is an Import. Items are aligned by row_id, and a Comparison never rests on a hidden intersection: if the Items differ by one, it is refused. This chapter describes the temporal regression Track, on dev; the Comparison of two agent Assessments, which aligns Episodes by Case and Repeat, is chapter 11, and the report and the Exposure journal are later work (see Limits).

The command

compare is in src/hypotrail/cli.py; the work is in src/hypotrail/core/comparison.py, a core module (ADR 0001): it imports neither a Track, scikit-learn, NumPy nor Inspect, and runs where Inspect is not importable. It follows the rules of chapter 04: with --json, stdout holds one JSON object, {"ok": true, "comparison": RECORD} or {"ok": false, "error": {"code", "message", "stage", "details"}}, and the diagnostics (each warning of the Comparison, the refusal) go to stderr; without it, stdout holds one line, which names beside the status the code of a warning a compatible Comparison can hold (the warning codes below). The two ids are assessment_id values of Assessments the Study keeps (chapter 05). Each call writes a new Comparison with a new comparison_id; another Comparison of the same Assessments never changes an earlier one.

compare writes to the Study, so it holds the Study's writer lock for its whole execution, as init, run ml, import ml, recover and assess do: while another writer holds it, compare is refused at once with STUDY_LOCKED (exit 6) and writes nothing (chapter 04).

CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
compare--study DIR, --control ASSESSMENT_ID, --candidate ASSESSMENT_ID--factor (agent Studies only, chapter 11), --examples-dir (read only for public_check Items, chapter 12), --read-sequence N, --json
# the bike Study of chapter 04: seasonal_7 and ridge_v1 run on dev and assessed with the same Scorer (chapter 05)
uv run python -m hypotrail compare --study runs/bike --control ASSESSMENT_ID_OF_SEASONAL_7 --candidate ASSESSMENT_ID_OF_RIDGE_V1 --json

What is checked, in order

Both Assessments must exist in the Study (ASSESSMENT_NOT_FOUND, exit 2), and every artifact read (the Observation set, its Attempt manifest, the per-Item files) is read back against its digest (chapter 04, exit 3). Then the checks below run in this order, and the first one that fails refuses the Comparison with its own code and exit 3. The refusal's details carry the check, its Reason code (reason), the values of both sides (control, candidate) and the checks evaluated so far (checks). Nothing is written when a check fails.

CheckPasses whenRefusal codeReason
populationThe two Observation sets are of the same Partition and the same expected Items (the digest of their IDs, so an Import's population file and the Study's memberships name the same population when they expect the same Items)COMPARISON_POPULATION_DIFFERSpopulation_differs
referencesThe two Assessments were scored against the same References set (by digest)COMPARISON_REFERENCES_DIFFERreferences_differ
scorerThe same Scorer id, version and configuration digestCOMPARISON_SCORER_DIFFERSscorer_differs
unitThe Metrics are in the same unitCOMPARISON_UNIT_DIFFERSunit_differs
itemsThe two per-Item files hold exactly the same row_ids. Otherwise the refusal gives the count of Items on each side and how many are on that side only; no intersection is computedCOMPARISON_ITEMS_DIFFERitems_differ
modeThe two sides were produced in the same Mode. A side that is an Import (external_unknown) does not refuse: the check is recorded as failed with the Reason provenance_unverified and the Comparison is descriptiveCOMPARISON_MODE_DIFFERSmode_differs
coverageEvery Item is scored on both sides, so the paired differences leave no Item outCOMPARISON_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENTcoverage_insufficient

One guard on the Partition comes with the population check. Once the populations are known to be the same, a Partition other than dev and public_check is refused with COMPARISON_PARTITION_NOT_DEV (exit 2). A Comparison of public_check Items is an Exposure: once the Items and the Mode have been checked, and before the differences are paired, the Study's freeze record is written if the revision has none and one journal entry names the Comparison and both sides (chapter 12). A dev side against a public_check side is two populations and is refused as such.

Strict against descriptive

A Comparison is compatible (strict) when every check passes. A Comparison where a side is an Import is descriptive_only, never strict: what an Import records about its producer (model, generation time, whether it saw public_check) is declared or unknown, never verified (chapter 06), so the Comparison supports no claim that the two sides were produced under the same conditions. Its record carries a warning on that scope (code COMPARISON_DESCRIPTIVE_IMPORT) in warnings and in limitations, and the command prints it on stderr. The population, References, Scorer, unit and Items checks still apply to it. The status incompatible exists in the schema but is never recorded: an incompatible pair is refused, and no record is written for it.

StatusWhen
compatibleEvery check passed and both sides were produced by Hypotrail: a strict Comparison
descriptive_onlyAt least one side is an Import: descriptive, with a warning on its scope
incompatibleNever written: a failed check refuses the Comparison

A Comparison can hold a warning whatever its status. The table below gives each warning code a Comparison can hold: whether a Comparison that holds it is always descriptive_only, and whether a compatible Comparison can hold it. Each warning is in warnings and its message in limitations, and compare prints it on stderr.

Warning codeAlways descriptive_onlyA compatible Comparison can hold itWhen
COMPARISON_DESCRIPTIVE_IMPORTyesnoA side is an Import (this chapter and chapter 11)
COMPARISON_FACTOR_NOT_ESTABLISHEDyesnoA native side of an agent Comparison does not record a factor (chapter 11)
COMPARISON_CASES_NOT_ESTABLISHEDyesnoA native side of an agent Comparison establishes no Case content (chapter 11)
COMPARISON_SINGLE_FEATURE_BEATSnoyesA side of a regression Comparison is beaten by a lag feature of its own Configuration used alone, as its Assessment's single_feature_check records (chapter 05): one warning per such side, naming it and the features with their Metrics; compare and the report name the code beside the status
COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATEDnoyesTwo native Attempts of an agent Comparison ran one after the other, in separate time blocks; compare and the report name the code beside the status (chapter 11)
COMPARISON_DEVIATION_DECLAREDnoyesA Declared deviation in force covers a side of a Comparison of either Track: it cites that side's Attempt, or the References set its Assessment was scored against (chapter 17). One warning for each such Declared deviation, naming the side or sides, its id and its text; compare and the report name the code beside the status

The Comparison record

comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/comparison.json is canonical JSON, validated against the contract schema before it is written. roles names the Control and the Candidate; left_assessment_id is the Control and right_assessment_id the Candidate. delta is the Metric of the Candidate minus the Metric of the Control, taken from the two Assessments: for regression_mae_v1, delta_mae = MAE(Candidate) - MAE(Control), and a positive value means the Candidate is worse. An improvement is never required. paired_deltas_ref is the per-Item file: the absolute error of each side and their difference, per row_id. coverage counts the expected Items, those scored on each side and those paired. population_ref is the population identity: the Partition, the digest of the population artifact and the row_ids. code_environment compares the two Attempts' code environments (Python, dependency lock, code revision): when they differ the record says so, naming what differs, and its limitations state that the two executions are not identical. When a side is an Import, its recorded environment is the importer's, so same is null, and the limitations say that a shared environment is not established. When they are the same it still does not claim that the executions were identical. The record holds no confidence interval: daily observations are dependent.

RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
Comparisonkind, schema_version, comparison_id, left_assessment_id, right_assessment_id, comparability (status, checks, each with check, passed, reason), population_ref, paired_deltas_ref, coverage, limitationsroles, delta, code_environment, warnings (written for every Comparison); declared_factor, descriptive, secondary_deltas, primary_criterion, matrix_ref, rates (written for an agent Comparison, chapter 11); check_informed (not written yet)
RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
Delta (delta, and each entry of secondary_deltas)metric (the Metric or Criterion), value (a number, or null with its reason), unit, definition (the sentence that says what the difference is and which sign is better)reason (why value is null), control and candidate (the two values the difference is taken from, an agent Comparison only)
DescriptiveMeasures (descriptive.control and descriptive.candidate, an agent Comparison)latency_seconds, model_calls, tool_calls, tokens, cost: each a number, or null when it is not known for every Episode that ran, never a partial sum and never 0none
PathWritten byContent
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/population.jsoncompare, firstThe population identity: partition, population_sha256, count, row_ids.
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/paired_deltas.csvcompareOne row per paired Item: row_id, control_abs_error, candidate_abs_error, delta_abs_error (Candidate minus Control).
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/comparison.jsoncompare, lastThe Comparison record. A folder without it is not a Comparison.

Refusals

Each refusal has the exit code, stage and details keys below. The integrity refusals (ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY, ARTIFACT_MISSING, CONTRACT_VIOLATION, exit 3), STUDY_NOT_FOUND, STUDY_LOCKED and INVALID_ARGUMENTS are those of chapter 04. The comparability refusals are exit 3, as the specification gives to a comparability refusal; their details keys are the same for all seven.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
ASSESSMENT_NOT_FOUNDThe Study keeps no Assessment with this id (the id is only compared, never used as a path)2comparerole, assessment_id
OBSERVATION_SET_NOT_FOUNDThe Observation set an Assessment names is not in the Study2comparerole, observation_set_id
COMPARISON_PARTITION_NOT_DEVBoth sides are of the same Partition, and it is not dev2comparepartition
COMPARISON_POPULATION_DIFFERSTwo populations (for example two Partitions)3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_REFERENCES_DIFFERTwo References sets3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_SCORER_DIFFERSTwo Scorers, or two versions or configuration digests of one Scorer3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_UNIT_DIFFERSTwo units3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_ITEMS_DIFFERItems that differ by even one, with the counts on each side3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_MODE_DIFFERSTwo Modes of production, neither of them an Import3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENTAn Item that is not scored on one side or the other: the paired differences would leave it out3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks

Limits

Regression here

This chapter's checks and record are those of the temporal regression Track. Two agent Assessments are aligned by Case and Repeat, with more checks and a matrix, in chapter 11. The bike Comparison records no latency or cost. The report comes later.

Exposure and marks

A Comparison of public_check Items is recorded as an Exposure (chapter 12). The record's check_informed field is written when a Configuration the first freeze record does not hold marks a side; declared_factor is written for an agent Comparison only.

Population identity

The identity of the population is the Partition and the digest of its expected Item IDs, read from the Study's memberships (native Attempt) or from the population file of the Import. The Items are then compared through the per-Item files.

Import

The code environment recorded for an Import is that of the process that imported it, not of its producer, which is unknown. A Comparison with an Import is descriptive for that reason too.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the Comparison is a core module, with no Track and no Inspect behind it.
0002Why the Partitions have fixed roles: a Comparison is on dev, or on public_check through the Exposure journal.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.
0004Why compare takes --read-sequence and is refused with STUDY_CHANGED when the Study was written to since the sequence was read.

The tests are tests/test_compare.py, test_compare_refusals.py and test_compare_scope.py; they run offline and call no model. The library Comparison of chapter 02 (hypotrail/ml/comparison.py, on two predictions files) is kept unchanged and is the oracle the dev test compares against, to 1e-9.

08

Agent

Agent answers: Study, import and Assessment

init --workload chinook creates a Study of the tool-using agent Track. import agent adopts answers produced elsewhere, as JSON Lines, as an Import Attempt of that Study: one line for every expected Case and Repeat, a failed or missing line included. assess --scorer chinook_contract_v1 scores the kept Observation set with the Scorer of chapter 03 and writes an Assessment: four Criteria per Episode, each with a state and a Reason for every non-score, the counters of CMP-03 and the two named rates of CMP-04. Nothing here runs a model or a tool, opens the Chinook database or imports Inspect. This chapter describes what exists for JSONL answers, on dev; an Inspect log, which needs Inspect, is chapter 09.

A Chinook Study

init reads the Workload's track: tool_using_agent goes to src/hypotrail/agentic/study.py, temporal_regression to the code of chapter 04. The data is verified against its lock first (this one check opens the database and counts its tables); nothing is created before it passes. The Study then keeps a copy of the database and three Study artifacts, each with an ID: the memberships (which Partition each Case belongs to), the References set exactly as the Workload declares it, and the answer schema (table-answer/1) the Scorer checks answers against. The References set is therefore frozen in the Study: assess reads no Workload file.

The Study's primary metric is the primary Criterion of its Assessments. The Workload declares it as data (primary_criterion in examples/chinook/workload.json, closed to the four Criteria of the Scorer by workload.schema.json) and init copies it into the StudySpec, so it is frozen with the Protocol. Chinook declares contract_success (owner decision 1A on issue #9). The Workload also declares the question, the objective and the limitations the StudySpec needs; init refuses a declaration without one of them or without the primary Criterion (DECLARATION_INCOMPLETE). The Protocol digest covers the Cases of each Partition, the memberships, the References, the answer schema and the database by their bytes, and the primary Criterion; it holds no Configuration. A command of the other Track on a Study is refused with UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY (chapter 04).

uv run python -m hypotrail init --workload chinook --study runs/chinook
PathRoleWhat it holds
study.jsonstudy_specThe StudySpec: domain agentic, the Protocol digest, the primary metric (contract_success), the limitations.
datasets/Chinook_Sqlite.sqlitedatasetThe database as it was at init. Hashed and read back, never opened as a database after init.
artifacts/memberships.csvmembershipscase_id, partition: the Partition of every Case.
artifacts/chinook-references.jsonlreferencesThe frozen References, one line per Case.
artifacts/answer-schema.jsonanswer_schemaThe table-answer/1 schema.
writer.locknoneThe writer lock file (chapter 04); it stays empty.

The command: import agent

import agent is in src/hypotrail/cli.py; the work is in src/hypotrail/agentic/imports.py. It has the shape of import ml (chapter 06): --request-id is required, with --json stdout holds one object ({"ok": true, "replayed": BOOL, "attempt": MANIFEST, "observation_set_id": ID}, with the Observation set ID of chapter 04, or the error object of chapter 04), and the command holds the Study's writer lock for the whole command. It imports neither Inspect, scikit-learn nor NumPy.

# population.json: {"episodes": [{"case_id": "D01", "repeat": 0}, {"case_id": "D03", "repeat": 0}, ...]}
uv run python -m hypotrail import agent --study runs/chinook --source examples/chinook/fixtures/agent-answers-authored.jsonl --population population.json --request-id import-a
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/chinook --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer chinook_contract_v1 --references references-0123456789abcdef
CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
import agent--study DIR, --source JSONL (or an Inspect log, chapter 09),--population FILE, --request-id ID--examples-dir DIR, --read-sequence N, --json

The lines and the population

The population is {"episodes": [{"case_id", "repeat"}, ...]}: the Episodes the file must hold, every Case taken from one Partition of the Study's Protocol, each Episode once. The expected keys come from this file checked against the Protocol, never from the answers: a line cannot add an Episode to it. The source holds exactly one line for each, whatever its order; a Case or a Repeat the population does not list is refused (an unknown Case, a Case of another Partition, a Case the population does not list and a Repeat out of range each have their problem value). A line is a JSON object with the keys below. A key the table does not list is ignored: the original file is kept as it is.

A produced line carries one answer: raw_answer (the text the system returned) or final_answer (an already-parsed JSON object, as the kit's fixture has it). Both is refused. Each Observation records answer_given_as, and a final_answer is scored as its canonical JSON text. The answer of a failed or a missing line is not scored: it stays in the original file only. A trace, when present, is a list of the projected steps the Scorer reads (plain records, each with a kind, as hypotrail.agentic.projection produces them); it is kept as data and nothing in it is executed.

KeyRequiredMeaning
case_idalwaysThe Case, a non-empty string.
repeatalwaysThe Repeat, a non-negative integer (0-based).
observation_statusalwaysproduced, failed (execution stopped without an output) or missing. A line with no answer to give says so with failed or missing.
raw_answerwhen produced, one of the twoThe text the system returned, scored as it is.
final_answerwhen produced, one of the twoThe parsed answer, a JSON object.
tracenoThe projected steps of the Episode. Without it the evidence link is unscored; no trace is ever generated.
originnoWhat the file says it is (the kit's fixture says authored_fixture): declared.
modenoThe Mode the file says it was produced in (the fixture says scripted_fixture): declared, and never the Import's Mode.
producernoAn object: model, generation_time, historical_protocol, tool_use, each a string or null.

What is checked, in order

  1. The Study exists and the writer lock is free; the Study is of the agent Track (UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY); the bytes of the Workload's other files and licence notices are checked against its lock, the database is neither opened nor hashed (the Study's copy of it, read back against its digest, stands for it), and the Study's artifacts are read back against their digests.
  2. The two files are read and their sha256 taken. The request is identified (below). The same request_id with the same request ends here with the recorded Import: nothing is validated again, written or scored.
  3. The Protocol of the Workload is still the Study's (PROTOCOL_CHANGED): the Cases, the References, the answer schema and the primary Criterion are compared by their bytes, and the database by the digest of the Study's copy.
  4. The population is checked, then every line of the source in file order: JSON, an object, its fields, its status, its answer, its place in the population, its uniqueness; then every Episode of the population must be present.
  5. Only then is anything written: the running manifest, the original files, the validation report, the normalized Observations and the Observation set, and the terminal manifest last. A refusal at step 4 writes nothing at all.

An Import whose file holds failed or missing lines is completed: its manifest holds item_counts by execution status. The Import is an Attempt of the same Study as any other: operation: import, Mode external_unknown, and its Observation set has origin: imported. Nothing in the file is executed or deserialised as code; a source with the extension of a pickle, joblib or array dump, or whose first byte is the pickle marker, is refused as SOURCE_SERIALIZED before it is read as text.

PathRoleWhat it holds
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/attempt.jsonmanifestThe Attempt manifest (running, then terminal), with item_counts.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/source/NAMEimport_sourceThe original answers file, byte for byte, under its own file name.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/population.jsonimport_populationThe population file, byte for byte. It is the Observation set's population_ref.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/validation_report.jsonvalidation_reportThe digests of the two inputs, the lines read, the Episodes expected, the execution-status counts and the checks that passed.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observations.jsonlobservationsThe normalized Observations in canonical JSON, one line per Episode in (Case, Repeat) order: case_id, repeat, status, answer_given_as, raw_answer, final_answer, trace. A permuted source gives the same bytes.
attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/observation_set.jsonobservation_setThe Observation set record: domain agentic, its provenance and unknown_fields.

What is known about the producer

Each field is {"value": ..., "origin": ...} in effective_config.producer and in the Observation set's provenance. A field is declared when every line of the file states the same non-null value, and unknown otherwise (a line that says nothing, or two lines that disagree, make it unknown; the original file keeps what each said). No field is verified: a claim in an external file is not checked by importing it. The model, the date of generation, the historical protocol and the tool use stay unknown unless the file establishes them; the kit's fixture states none of them. Whether the producer saw public_check values is always unknown. The import time (import_time, also the manifest's started_at) is a separate field from generation_time. No record states that the producer worked independently, and the origin the file declares is kept as a declared field, never as the Import's own: the fixture's Observation set says file_origin: authored_fixture (declared), and nothing names it llm_live or a provider's model. unknown_fields of the Observation set lists the fields whose origin is unknown.

FieldDeclared by (in a line)Otherwise
modelproducer.modelunknown
generation_timeproducer.generation_timeunknown
historical_protocolproducer.historical_protocolunknown
tool_useproducer.tool_useunknown
saw_public_checknothingunknown, always
file_originoriginunknown
file_modemodeunknown

The request

The identity of an import holds every element of chapter 04's list: the command (import agent), the Workload, the Configuration (unknown), the Partition of the population, the Mode (external_unknown), the model (the declared one, or unknown), the repeats (the largest Repeat of the population plus one), the digests of the database, the source and the population, and the code revision. The same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Import; with any element different it is REQUEST_CONFLICT (exit 6).

Refusals of import agent

The refusals of chapter 04 (REQUEST_ID_EMPTY, REQUEST_CONFLICT, STUDY_LOCKED, UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY, DATA_INTEGRITY, PROTOCOL_CHANGED, the contract and artifact refusals) apply to import agent. Each refusal below has the stage and the details keys shown, and publishes no Observation set.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
SOURCE_NOT_FOUNDThe source file cannot be read2importsource
POPULATION_NOT_FOUNDThe population file cannot be read2importpopulation
SOURCE_SERIALIZEDA pickle, joblib or array dump2importsource
SOURCE_NOT_UTF8A source that is not valid UTF-82importsource
POPULATION_INVALIDA population that is not {"episodes": [...]} of distinct {"case_id", "repeat"}2importpopulation, problem
POPULATION_CASE_UNKNOWNA Case that is in no Partition of the Protocol2importcase_id
POPULATION_MIXED_PARTITIONSCases of more than one Partition2importpartitions
LINE_NOT_JSONA line that is not JSON (NaN, a repeated key or an out-of-range number included)2importline
LINE_NOT_AN_OBJECTA JSON line that is not an object2importline
LINE_FIELD_INVALIDA case_id, repeat, answer, trace, origin, mode or producer of the wrong shape2importfield, line
OBSERVATION_STATUS_UNKNOWNA status that is absent or not produced, failed or missing2importline, observation_status, allowed
ANSWER_AMBIGUOUSA line with both raw_answer and final_answer2importcase_id, repeat, line
PRODUCED_WITHOUT_ANSWERA produced line with neither answer2importcase_id, repeat, line
EPISODE_OUTSIDE_POPULATIONA key the population does not list: unknown_case, other_partition, not_in_population or repeat_out_of_range2importcase_id, repeat, line, problem
EPISODE_DUPLICATEA (Case, Repeat) that appears on two lines2importcase_id, repeat, line
POPULATION_EPISODE_ABSENTAn Episode of the population that the source does not hold2importcase_id, repeat, absent

The command: assess with chinook_contract_v1

assess keeps the flags of chapter 05. A Scorer of the agent Track is routed to src/hypotrail/agentic/assess.py; the registry (src/hypotrail/core/scorers.py) holds chinook_contract_v1 at version 3 and its config_digest, and the one scoring function stays in src/hypotrail/core/chinook_contract.py (ADR 0001: one function per Scorer, never duplicated). It reads the Observation set, the frozen References and the kept answer schema, and nothing else: with the model, the tools and the database made to fail if used and Inspect not importable, import agent and assess succeed (a test runs both in a subprocess). An Assessment of public_check Episodes is refused until the Exposure journal of issue #9 exists, as rescoring and counting refuse them.

Every expected Episode gets a state for each of the four Criteria. An answer is scored by the Scorer exactly as chapter 03 describes, a final_answer object as its canonical JSON text; every scoring record states answer_read_from (raw_answer, embedded_object or none). An answer without a trace has exact_table and format_valid scored and evidence_link_valid unscored with the Reason trace_missing. contract_success keeps the Scorer's rule: it is scored false as soon as one dependency is false, even without a trace (a wrong table, a malformed or wrapped answer); only an answered Episode with no dependency false has it unscored with trace_missing. The kit's fixture is the example: D01 and D03 are answered, exact and well-formed, so their primary Criterion is unscored; D07 and D08 (not_answerable, needs_clarification) need no tool call and are true. An Episode without an answer is never scored false:

Execution statusState of every CriterionReason
failedunscoredexecution_failed
missingunscoredobservation_missing

An Episode that the Observation set does not hold at all is handled as missing. A crash of the scoring machinery errors the Criteria of that one Episode (errored, Reason scoring_failed, the exception's type only); the others are scored.
