The counters and the two rates

The primary Criterion is the one the Study names (contract_success for Chinook). The envelope, assessment.json, holds primary_criterion, the counters of that Criterion as counts, and its two named rates as metrics. The other three Criteria are secondary: each keeps its counters and its two rates in metrics.json, referenced by detailed_metrics_ref, and none of them is in the envelope's counters. The counters are those of CMP-03 under the contract schema's keys (the ticket's N_x is the key x); scored = correct + incorrect.

KeyTicket nameCounts
expectedN_expectedEvery Episode of the population.
attemptedN_attemptedEvery Episode not missing.
producedN_producedEvery Episode with an answer.
scoredN_scoredEpisodes whose Criterion is scored, true or false.
correctN_correctScored true.
incorrectN_incorrectScored false.
unscoredN_unscoredNo score, with a Reason.
erroredN_erroredThe scoring machinery broke, with a Reason.

Two named rates follow (CMP-04), both higher_is_better. A zero denominator gives a null value with a Reason (status unscored), never 0. The second rate does not call the unknowns incorrect: an Episode that is unscored lowers it only because it is not a confirmed success, and the unknowns are shown beside the rates in the envelope's unknowns (the counts of unscored and errored and the Reasons of the primary Criterion's non-scores). The failed and missing Episodes are counted apart in operational_errors, so an Episode that failed and is unscored appears in both.

RateDefinitionNull Reason
quality_among_scoredcorrect / scored: the quality among the Episodes that could be scoredno_scored_episode
confirmed_share_of_expectedcorrect / expected: the share of the planned Episodes with a confirmed successno_expected_episode
FieldHolds
primary_criterionThe Criterion of the envelope, read from the Study's primary metric.
unknownsunscored, errored and reasons: the Reasons of the primary Criterion's non-scores with their counts.
operational_errorsfailed and missing: Episodes counted by execution status, apart from the Criterion counters.
detailed_metrics_refThe reference of metrics.json: counters and rates of the secondary Criteria.
PathWritten byContent
assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/per_item.csvassess, firstOne row per Episode and Criterion, with the columns below.
assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/metrics.jsonassess, secondThe secondary Criteria: for each, counts and its two rates.
assessments/ASSESSMENT_ID/assessment.jsonassess, lastThe Assessment record. A folder without it is not an Assessment.
ColumnHolds
case_idThe Case.
repeatThe Repeat.
execution_statusproduced, failed or missing.
answer_given_asraw_answer or final_answer; empty without an answer.
answer_read_fromraw_answer or embedded_object; empty when nothing was read.
criterionOne of the four Criteria.
statescored, unscored or errored.
scoretrue or false when scored, otherwise empty.
reasonThe Reason of every non-score and of a false score that has one.

Refusals of assess

The refusals of chapter 05 apply (the Scorer is not registered, the Observation set or the References set is not found, a Scorer of another Track than the Observation set's, an Observation set that is not valid, a kept artifact that changed) with the Chinook values. Two more are specific to this Track.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
ANSWER_SCHEMA_NOT_KEPTA Study that keeps no answer schema to score against2assessstudy

Choices and limits worth knowing

The primary Criterion is data

Which Criterion is primary is declared by the Workload and frozen by the Study, not chosen by the code. An import without a trace therefore leaves the primary unscored for an answered Episode whose dependencies are not false, and the rate that counts confirmed successes stays low until traces exist.

Claims are not checked

The model, the date, the protocol, the tool use and the origin are only what the file says, and only when every line says it. The authored fixture is a hand-written file: it does not satisfy a live recipe.

The trace format is the Scorer's

A trace is a list of the projected steps the Scorer reads, not an Inspect log. An Inspect log is imported by the same command and read through its public API: see chapter 09.

Not built

A command that runs the agent into a Study. Corrected References are chapter 10, the Comparison of two agent Assessments and its matrix chapter 11, and the Exposure journal that lets assess take public_check Episodes chapter 12.

The tests are tests/test_agent_study.py, tests/test_import_agent.py and tests/test_assess_agent.py (every criterion of the ticket), tests/test_study_lock.py (the second writer) and tests/test_handbook.py (the tables of this chapter against the code). They run offline and call no model.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0004Why import agent takes --read-sequence and is refused with STUDY_CHANGED when the Study was written to since the sequence was read.
09

Agent

Importing an Inspect log

import agent also adopts an Inspect log (a .eval file written by the pinned Inspect) as an Import Attempt of a Chinook Study. The log is read once, through Inspect's public log API, and projected into one Episode per expected Case and Repeat with the projection that rescore_log uses. The trace of each Episode is kept in the Study, so assess --scorer chinook_contract_v1 scores evidence_link_valid from it, and gives the same verdicts as rescore_log on the same log. Neither the import nor the Assessment calls a model, runs a tool or opens the Chinook file. This chapter extends chapter 08, which describes the Study, the population, the request and the Assessment; it only says what differs for a log.

The command and how a source is told apart

The command is the one of chapter 08, with the same flags: --source names a log instead of a JSON Lines file. The kind of the source is decided by its extension and by nothing else: .eval is an Inspect log, any other extension is JSON Lines. The work is in src/hypotrail/agentic/imports.py (the order of chapter 08, with the log read in step 4) and src/hypotrail/agentic/inspect_log.py, the one module of the import that imports Inspect; it is loaded only for a log. The population file is the same {"episodes": [...]}, checked against the Protocol, and it alone gives the expected Episodes: the log never supplies or widens it.

uv run python -m hypotrail import agent --study runs/chinook --source docs/handbook/traces/live-l12-plain/inspect/LOG.eval --population population.json --request-id import-log-a
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/chinook --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer chinook_contract_v1 --references REFERENCES_ID
Extension of --sourceKindNeeds
.evalAn Inspect log, read through the public log APIThe supported Inspect, version 0.3.287 as pyproject.toml pins it (inspect-ai, the agent extra); otherwise INSPECT_UNAVAILABLE. A log written by another Inspect version is LOG_INSPECT_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED
any otherJSON Lines, as in chapter 08The standard library only

What an Episode becomes

The log is read with read_eval_log and nothing else: there is no hand-written parser (ADR 0001). Each expected (Case, Repeat) is looked up among the log's samples (Inspect's epoch is 1-based, a Repeat 0-based), and an Episode the log does not hold is missing with the Reason missing_from_log, as the projection decides; this differs from a JSONL import, where an absent Episode is refused. A sample that is not an expected Episode, or two samples for one Episode, are refused. The execution status and the Reason of an Episode are the projection's (produced; failed by an execution error, a limit or the output cap; missing), and assess gives them the Criteria that chapter 03 gives them: a turn or token limit is scored false, a time limit, the output cap or an error is unscored.

The answer is the text of the final message, kept as raw_answer and scored as it is. The trace is the projected steps of the Episode (model turns, tool calls, tool results, the final answer), kept as data in the normalized Observations; nothing in it is executed. An Episode with no step keeps no trace, so its evidence link is unscored with trace_missing, as for an answer without a trace in chapter 08.

KeyHolds
case_idThe Case (the sample id).
repeatThe Repeat (the sample's epoch minus one).
statusproduced, failed or missing, as the projection decides.
reasonWhy the Episode is failed or missing (execution_failed, turn_limit, cost_limit, output_cap, missing_from_log, ...); null when produced. A JSONL line has no such key, and an Observation without it reads as without a Reason.
answer_given_asraw_answer for an Episode with a final answer, otherwise null.
raw_answerThe text of the final answer.
final_answerAlways null: a log gives text, not a parsed object.
usageWhat the log states for the Episode: latency_seconds (the sample's total time) and tokens (its models' total tokens, cached input included), each null when the log does not give it, and the whole key null for an Episode the log lacks. A JSONL import has no such key.
traceThe projected steps of the Episode; null when there is none.

What is recorded, and as what

The original log is copied into the Study under attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/source/ and referenced with its digest, like a JSONL file; the validation report keeps source_format: inspect_log, the number of samples read and the checks that passed. What the log header states is recorded as declared and never as verified, only from the fields below (nothing else of the header, such as the model arguments or a base URL, is copied into a record; the original copy is the file as it was). import_time is the time of the import and is kept apart from generation_time, which is the header's creation time. The Import's Mode stays external_unknown: the Mode a header states is only the declared file_mode. A log whose model is Inspect's mock model is never labelled llm_live: if its header claims that Mode, file_mode is unknown. A log of a real model is not labelled llm_live either: it is a declared model, nothing more.

Whether the producer saw public_check values, its historical protocol, its tool use and the origin of the file stay unknown: a log does not establish them, and no record says the Episodes were produced independently. They are listed in the Observation set's unknown_fields, with the log. fields the header leaves out (an older log has no Configuration). The producer fields are the ones of chapter 08, under effective_config.producer and the Observation set's provenance; the other header fields of the table below are kept under log in the same two places.

Recorded fieldUnderRead from the header
modelproducerThe model Inspect ran (mockllm/model for the mock model).
generation_timeproducerThe creation time of the log.
file_modeproducerThe Mode the task metadata states (scripted_fixture for a scripted execution); none for a live log.
configurationlogThe Configuration name in the task metadata.
system_textlogThe system text of the Configuration, as the task metadata holds it.
limitslogThe limits that took effect, as the log header records them (chapter 03's read-back).
inspect_versionlogThe Inspect version that wrote the log.
log_format_versionlogThe version of the log format.
log_statuslogThe status of the execution that wrote the log (success, error, ...).
FieldWhy it stays unknown
historical_protocolThe Configuration and the limits are recorded as what the header states; the protocol the producer followed is not established.
tool_useThe trace shows the calls, but the log does not state a tool-use claim.
saw_public_checkNo log says what its producer saw.
file_originA log does not say what it is; the JSONL origin claim has no counterpart.

The Inspect requirement and the refusals of a log import

A log import needs Inspect, and only a log import does: with Inspect not importable, the import of a .eval source is refused with INSPECT_UNAVAILABLE and exit 5 (capability unavailable) before the Study's lock is taken and before anything is read or written, while a JSONL import, assess and every other command run as before. The installed Inspect must be the supported version (0.3.287, the one pyproject.toml pins), else the same refusal; a log whose header states another Inspect version, or none, is LOG_INSPECT_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED (exit 2, with the version it states and the supported one). A log the installed Inspect cannot read is LOG_NOT_READABLE, and only the type of Inspect's error is reported. The refusals of chapter 04 and the population refusals of chapter 08 apply to a log import too; the JSONL line refusals do not. Each refusal below publishes no Observation set.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
INSPECT_UNAVAILABLEA log source and Inspect not importable, or installed in another version than the supported one (details then also hold installed and supported)5importpackage
LOG_INSPECT_VERSION_UNSUPPORTEDA log whose header states an Inspect version other than the supported one2importsource, log_inspect_version, supported
LOG_NOT_READABLEA file the public log API cannot read2importsource, error_type
LOG_EPISODE_OUTSIDE_POPULATIONA sample the population does not list: unknown_case, other_partition, not_in_population or repeat_out_of_range2importcase_id, repeat, problem
LOG_EPISODE_DUPLICATETwo samples for one (Case, Repeat); Inspect's own writer drops such a sample, so only a hand-made file has it2importcase_id, repeat

An Assessment of public_check Episodes of a log is an Exposure like any other (chapter 12): it is journaled, and the log's producer's own exposure stays unknown.

Choices and limits worth knowing

One scoring path

An import keeps the answer and the steps, and assess scores them with the one Scorer function of core/chinook_contract.py, as it scores a JSONL answer. tests/test_import_log.py compares, for scripted logs and for each kept live log, every Criterion of every Episode with rescore_log's verdict.

The log is read once, then copied

The digest and the kept copy are taken from the file's bytes, and Inspect reads the file from its path: a file that changes during the import is not detected. The copy keeps whatever the log held, which is why only the listed header fields are recorded elsewhere.

Only .eval

Inspect can also write JSON logs; they are not recognised as a log source here, and are read as JSON Lines.

Not built

An Inspect @scorer adapter, a command that runs the agent into a Study.

The tests are tests/test_import_log.py (every criterion of the ticket), tests/test_study_lock.py (the second writer) and tests/test_handbook.py (the tables of this chapter against the code). They run offline, call no model and use logs written by Inspect's mock model and the kept live logs.

10

Core

Revisions and corrected References

A Study holds one frozen Protocol per revision, and a Protocol change is a new revision inside the same Study. A corrected References set changes the Protocol, so it is added with init --study DIR --new-revision. So is a changed Case text, which is part of the Protocol too: a Study created before the change refuses a new request with PROTOCOL_CHANGED until init --study DIR --new-revision adds the revision that holds the new text. For a corrected References set, the corrected set is published beside the old one, the StudySpec of the earlier revision is kept as it was, and nothing an earlier revision references changes by a byte. An Assessment of a corrected set scores the same Observation set again, and says which earlier Assessment it differs from and why, so a corrected Reference is never read as a change of the evaluated system.

The command

init --new-revision is in src/hypotrail/cli.py; the work is in src/hypotrail/core/revisions.py and, for each Track, in the new_revision function beside init_study (src/hypotrail/ml/study.py, src/hypotrail/agentic/study.py). The Study names its Workload, so the command takes no --workload (giving one is INVALID_ARGUMENTS, as is init with neither). It reads the Workload again, from --examples-dir or the checkout's examples/ (chapter 04), verifies its data against the lock before anything is written, and derives the Protocol it declares now: the population, the Partitions, the References, the rules on when information is available. With --json, stdout holds {"ok": true, "study": SPEC, "references_id": ID} with the new revision's StudySpec and the ID of its References set, the one assess --references takes, or the error object of chapter 04; without it, one line, which ends with references_id ID.

The command holds the Study's writer lock for its whole execution, as every command that writes to a Study does: a second writer is refused at once with STUDY_LOCKED (exit 6) and writes nothing.

# a corrected References file in the Workload; the Study keeps the old set and adds the new one
uv run python -m hypotrail init --study runs/chinook --new-revision
# the new References ID is in `artifacts` of study.json and in `status --json`
uv run python -m hypotrail status --study runs/chinook --json

What a revision keeps and what it adds

Every artifact the Protocol freezes (the dataset, the population, the References, the answer schema) is compared by its bytes with the one the current revision holds. One whose bytes did not change is carried over as it is: same path, same digest, same ID, same version. One whose bytes changed is published beside the earlier one, under a path that names its first sixteen digest digits, with the next version number and an ID that names its digest; the earlier file is never replaced. A References set is versioned as a whole: version 1 is the set init froze, version 2 the first corrected set.

study.json always holds the current revision, so every other command keeps reading one file. When a revision is added, the StudySpec of the one before is copied byte for byte to revisions/N/study.json, the new StudySpec is published as revisions/N+1/study.json, and study.json is replaced last, atomically. A writer killed between two of these steps leaves the earlier revision current; running the command again completes it. study_id stays the same for the life of the Study; revision is the digest of the StudySpec without its revision and study_id, so it differs from one revision to the next, and previous_revision chains each revision to the one before.

A Workload whose Protocol is the one the current revision already freezes is refused with PROTOCOL_UNCHANGED, and nothing is written. A Configuration is not part of the Protocol: changing one needs no revision (chapter 04).

PathWritten byContent
revisions/N/study.jsoninit --new-revisionThe StudySpec of revision N, canonical JSON. Revision N is copied there byte for byte when revision N+1 is added, and revision N+1 is published there; a file is never rewritten.
artifacts/chinook-references-SHA16.jsonlinit --new-revisionA corrected References set of a Chinook Study, beside the earlier one. SHA16 is the first sixteen digits of its digest. A bike Study names it artifacts/references-SHA16.csv; a changed population, answer schema or dataset is named the same way.
study.jsoninit --new-revision, lastReplaced atomically by the new revision's StudySpec. The only file a revision replaces.

The fields

A revision adds three optional fields to the StudySpec of chapter 04. A StudySpec written by init holds revision_number 1 and a version of 1 on each artifact; one written before revisions existed holds neither and is read as revision 1.

FieldWhereMeaning
revision_numberStudySpec1 for the first revision, then one more each time init --new-revision adds one.
previous_revisionStudySpecThe revision digest of the revision before; absent from revision 1.
check_informedStudySpecTrue when the revision was created after the Study's first Exposure; absent otherwise (chapter 12).
versioneach entry of artifactsHow many different contents the Study has held under this role: 1 at init, one more each time a revision publishes changed bytes for it.

status shows every revision under revisions, read only, and the current one under study:

Field of a revision in statusMeaning
revision_numberThe revision's number.
revisionThe digest that names the revision's StudySpec.
previous_revisionThe revision before, or null for the first.
protocol_digestThe Protocol this revision froze.
artifactsIts Protocol artifacts: artifact_id, role, version, sha256.

Rescoring: the new Assessment names the earlier one

After init --new-revision, assess takes the new References ID (the one in the current StudySpec; the earlier revision's set is no longer the Protocol and is refused with REFERENCES_NOT_FOUND) and an Observation set recorded under any revision. It writes a new Assessment, as ever: a new assessment_id, the same observation_set_id, the new references_ref, and every earlier file, Assessments included, untouched. It runs no model and no tool and opens neither the database nor the Workload: the frozen References, the answer schema and the kept answers are enough. The Assessment records the revision whose Protocol it applied (study_revision) and the time (recorded_at).

Its rescoring field is null when no earlier Assessment of the same Observation set and the same Scorer differs from it. Otherwise it names the latest one that does, in the order of the later Assessment (earlier_assessment_id), the cause and the Items whose verdict changed, each with both verdicts. The cause is the first of the differences below, in this order; any other that also differs is listed in other_changes, so nothing that changed is hidden. A numeric tolerance is held by the Reference (numeric_absolute_tolerance), so a changed tolerance is a changed References set and its cause is references_version.

The later Assessment has one order, used by status (whether a Decision still applies), by assess (the earlier Assessment its rescoring names) and by report (the order in which the page lists Assessments): the position in the write journal first, then (recorded_at, id). An Assessment's position is that of the first end entry of the write journal that names it among the records it wrote, from 1; an Assessment that no entry names is at position 0, before every journaled one, as is one written before the write journal existed.

The Items are listed one per Item and Criterion, sorted. An Episode's Item ID is CASE_ID#REPEAT (D03#0); a bike Item's is its row_id. A verdict is the Criterion's state, its score (a boolean for an agent Criterion, the absolute error for a bike Item) and its reason. Because a numeric loss has no correct or incorrect, a corrected bike Reference lists the Item whose absolute error moved, and the Metric moves with it; an agent Criterion can flip from true to false, or become unscored when the Reference no longer settles a dependency. Either way the delta is the Reference's, not the system's. The human line adds rescoring of Assessment ID: cause C, N Criteria changed.

CauseComparedThe Scorer or the Reference that changed
references_versionreferences_ref.sha256A corrected References set, tolerance included, added by a new revision.
scorer_versionscorer.versionThe Scorer's version changed since the earlier Assessment (the registry is static: this follows a code change).
scorer_config_digestscorer.config_digestThe Scorer's configuration changed under the same version.
Field of rescoringRequiredMeaning
earlier_assessment_idyesThe latest earlier Assessment of the same Observation set and Scorer id that differs by one of the causes, in the order of the later Assessment; it may come from an earlier revision.
causeyesThe first difference, in the order of the table above.
changed_itemsyesThe Items whose verdict changed; an empty list says that no verdict did.
other_changesnoThe other causes that also differ, when more than one does.
Field of a changed ItemMeaning
item_idCASE_ID#REPEAT for an Episode, the row_id for a bike Item.
criterionThe Criterion whose verdict changed.
earlierThe verdict in the earlier Assessment.
nowThe verdict in this one.
Field of a verdictMeaning
statescored, unscored or errored.
scoreA boolean or an absolute error; null unless scored.
reasonThe Reason of a non-score, or null.
Field added to every AssessmentMeaning
study_revisionnumber, revision and protocol_digest of the revision whose Protocol the Assessment applied; absent from an Assessment written before revisions existed.
recorded_atWhen it was written. How Assessments are ordered is stated in the order of the later Assessment.
rescoringThe object above, or null.

Refusals

The refusals of chapter 04 apply as they are: STUDY_NOT_FOUND for a folder that is no Study (no lock file is created in it), DATA_INTEGRITY when the Workload's data no longer matches its lock (checked before anything is written), STUDY_LOCKED and the integrity refusals. A revision adds one code of its own, and gives INVALID_ARGUMENTS two more causes.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
PROTOCOL_UNCHANGEDThe Protocol the Workload declares is the one the current revision freezes2studyprotocol_digest, revision_number
INVALID_ARGUMENTS--new-revision together with --workload (the Study names its Workload); init with neither (details: command only)2configcommand (hypotrail <command>), workload

Limits

Freeze and check_informed

A revision takes its own freeze record, revisions/N/freeze.json, at its first use of public_check, not when it is created. A revision created after the Study's first Exposure is marked check_informed by check_informed_mark in core/revisions.py; one created before is not. After an Exposure, an assess or compare on public_check Items that finds a Protocol other than the current revision's is refused with PROTOCOL_CHANGED (exit 3) and writes nothing (chapter 12).

The population is not compared

An Assessment applies the Protocol of its own revision to an Observation set recorded under an earlier one. assess reads the Items from the Observation set's own population and does not compare it with the new revision's population: a revision that changed the population is the author's to read.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0002Why a corrected References set is read again from the Workload that declares it, and why a Configuration, which the Workload also declares, is not part of the Protocol.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.

The tests are tests/test_study_revision.py, tests/test_rescoring.py and, for the writer lock, tests/test_study_lock.py; they run offline and call no model.

11

Agent

Comparing two agent Assessments

compare on two Assessments of a Chinook Study works as it does on the bike (chapter 07), with what an agent adds: the Items are Episodes, aligned by Case and Repeat; the two sides must share their planned Repeats, tools, model and budgets, except for the factor the request declares as intentional; each side keeps its Case × Repeat matrix; and, when every Episode is scored on both sides, each Criterion is aggregated per Case first and every Case weighs the same. The main delta is that of the primary Criterion, contract_success. With one Episode missing, the Comparison shows the coverage and gives no main delta. A Chinook side is an Import (chapter 08, chapter 09) or a native llm_live Attempt recorded by run agent (chapter 13). A Comparison with an Import on either side is descriptive; the strict path is two native llm_live Attempts, witnessed offline by two Attempts recorded with Inspect's mock model in place of the provider, which only a test can do. An Attempt of the scripted_fixture Mode is never assessed, so it is never compared.

The command

The command is the one of chapter 07, with one more flag. The work is in src/hypotrail/core/agent_comparison.py, a core module (ADR 0001): it imports no Track, no scikit-learn, no NumPy and no Inspect, and hypotrail.core.comparison loads it for an Assessment of an agentic Observation set. --factor names the factor that is intentionally different between the two sides; it defaults to configuration, the factor of plain against verify (the verify Configuration adds one explicit instruction to plain and changes nothing else). On a Study of another Track it is refused with UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY (exit 2). The command holds the Study's writer lock for the whole command and follows the --json rules of chapter 04.

# the Chinook Study of chapter 08: two imports, each assessed with chinook_contract_v1
uv run python -m hypotrail compare --study runs/chinook --control ASSESSMENT_ID_OF_PLAIN --candidate ASSESSMENT_ID_OF_VERIFY --json
# the two sides differ by their model on purpose
uv run python -m hypotrail compare --study runs/chinook --control ASSESSMENT_ID_A --candidate ASSESSMENT_ID_B --factor model

What is checked, in order

The checks run in this order, and the first one that fails refuses the Comparison with its own code and exit 3; nothing is written. A Partition other than dev and public_check is refused right after the population (COMPARISON_PARTITION_NOT_DEV, exit 2), as in chapter 07; a public_check Comparison is journaled once every check has passed (chapter 12). The population of an agent Comparison is the Partition and the Cases; the Repeats have their own check, so that a difference in Repeats can be declared. The References, Scorer and unit checks are those of chapter 07, with the same codes. The refusal's details carry the check, its Reason, the values of both sides and the checks evaluated so far.

For tools, model and budgets, each side establishes what it can: a native Attempt's manifest records them; an Import declares its model and tools in the lines of its file, and its budgets in the header of an Inspect log, or leaves them unknown (chapter 06). A check compares what both sides establish. When a side does not establish it, the check is recorded as failed with the Reason not_established, nothing is refused, and the Comparison is descriptive. An Import is never strict.

CheckPasses whenRefusal codeReasonCan be declared
populationThe same Partition and the same Cases on both sidesCOMPARISON_POPULATION_DIFFERSpopulation_differsno
casesThe same content for each Case: the digest each native Attempt records for each Case it evaluates, the whole Case object, is the same on both sides. When a native side records no such digest at all (an Attempt recorded before them), the digests of the two Cases files are compared instead, and anything but the same file is not_established; a side whose digests leave out a Case of the population is not_established, and an Import establishes no Case contentCOMPARISON_CASES_DIFFERcases_differno
referencesThe same References set (by digest)COMPARISON_REFERENCES_DIFFERreferences_differno
scorerThe same Scorer id, version and configuration digestCOMPARISON_SCORER_DIFFERSscorer_differsno
unitThe Metrics are in the same unitCOMPARISON_UNIT_DIFFERSunit_differsno
repeatsEach Case has the same number of planned Repeats on both sides. When the request declares repeats, the Episodes of one side only are kept and shown in the coverageCOMPARISON_REPEATS_DIFFERrepeats_differyes
toolsThe same tools (compared as a set of names) where both sides establish themCOMPARISON_TOOLS_DIFFERtools_differyes
modelThe same model where both sides establish itCOMPARISON_MODEL_DIFFERSmodel_differsyes
budgetsThe same limits (turns, time, token budget) where both sides establish themCOMPARISON_BUDGETS_DIFFERbudgets_differyes
modeNo scripted_fixture side against an llm_live side: there is no ranking between a simulation and a live model. An Import counts as the Mode its file or log declares; one that declares none is not refused. A side that is an Import records the check as failed with the Reason provenance_unverified and the Comparison is descriptiveCOMPARISON_MODE_DIFFERSmode_differsno

The Reasons a recorded check can carry besides the failures above are these. A declared factor is recorded as a passed check, so the record names what was allowed to differ, and declared_factor names it.

ReasonOn a check that isMeaning
declared_factorpassedThe sides differ and the request declared that factor as the intentional one.
not_establishedfailed, not refusedA side does not establish the factor: it was not checked, and the Comparison is descriptive.
provenance_unverifiedfailed, not refusedA side is an Import, whose producer is declared or unknown, never verified.

Strict against descriptive

A Comparison is compatible (strict) only when every check passed and both sides were produced by Hypotrail. It is descriptive_only when a side is an Import, whatever the origin of the fields the Import declares, when a native side does not record a factor, or when a native side establishes no Case content (its digests leave out a Case of the population, or it records none and the two sides do not record the same Cases file). The first case carries the warning COMPARISON_DESCRIPTIVE_IMPORT of chapter 07; the second, COMPARISON_FACTOR_NOT_ESTABLISHED, naming the factors; the third, COMPARISON_CASES_NOT_ESTABLISHED, since cases is not a factor. All are in warnings and limitations, and the command prints them on stderr. As in chapter 07, the record compares the code environments of the two Attempts: when they differ it says so and never calls the executions identical, and when they are the same it still does not claim they were identical.

Two native Attempts of which one finished before the other started, by the started_at and finished_at of their manifests and nothing else, ran in separate time blocks: run agent runs one Configuration per invocation (chapter 13), so the two sides were not alternated, and the delta may include a time effect. Such a Comparison holds COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATED in warnings and limitations, whatever the declared factor, and its message names the side that ran first. The warning leaves the status as the checks and Imports compute it: a pair whose checks all pass stays compatible. compare prints the warning on stderr and names its code beside the status on its stdout line, and the report names it beside the status in the row Comparability (chapter 14). The Limits below say which pairs get no derived warning.

Episodes, the matrix and the Case weight

Episodes are aligned by (case_id, repeat), never by position. The Comparison takes the union of the two sides' Episodes and drops none: an Episode on one side only (possible only when repeats is declared) and an Episode not scored for the primary Criterion on a side both show in coverage, as counts and as rates of the expected Episodes. matrix.json keeps each side's Cases × Repeats matrix: for each Case and Repeat, the execution status and, for each of the four Criteria, the state, the score and the Reason.

With full coverage, every Episode scored for the primary Criterion on both sides, the Criterion is aggregated per Case first: a Case's share is the share of its Repeats scored true. The value of a side is the mean of the shares of its Cases, so every Case has the same weight and the three Repeats of a Case are never counted as three Cases (they are not independent). The delta is the value of the Candidate minus the value of the Control; a positive value means the Candidate is better, since the Criterion is higher-is-better. paired_deltas.csv holds one row per Case. The main delta, delta, is that of the primary Criterion (contract_success, declared by the Workload and frozen by the Study); secondary_deltas holds the other three Criteria, each with its own coverage rule.

Without full coverage, delta.value is null and delta.reason is coverage_incomplete; paired_deltas_ref is null, so no per-Case table hides the gap. No binomial or other interval is computed over Episodes, and the limitations say so. A correct answer without a trace leaves contract_success unscored (trace_missing, chapter 05), so an import without traces gives no main delta unless every answer is wrong or needs no tool call.

The rates, and what is only described

rates repeats, for each side, the two named rates of its Assessment with the unknowns beside them: quality_among_scored (correct among scored) and confirmed_share_of_expected (correct among expected), the Episodes unscored or errored and their Reasons, and the failed and missing Episodes. A rate whose denominator is zero is null with a Reason, never 0. descriptive reports, for each side, latency, the numbers of model and tool calls, tokens and cost. These are descriptive only: no Criterion and no delta rests on them, and no cost is compared. Calls are counted from the kept traces and are null unless every Episode that ran has one; latency and tokens are the sums of what an Inspect log import kept for each Episode (chapter 09), and are null unless every Episode that ran has them, so a JSONL import or a partial log gives null; cost is always null, because no Observation set keeps it. None of them is ever filled with 0.

Key of coverageMeaning
unitWhat is counted: Episodes.
criterionThe primary Criterion the coverage is about.
expectedThe Episodes of both sides, aligned by Case and Repeat.
control_scoredEpisodes scored for the primary Criterion on the Control.
candidate_scoredEpisodes scored for the primary Criterion on the Candidate.
pairedEpisodes scored on both sides.
control_ratecontrol_scored over expected.
candidate_ratecandidate_scored over expected.
only_controlEpisodes present on the Control only.
only_candidateEpisodes present on the Candidate only.
completeTrue when every Episode is scored on both sides; only then is there a main delta.

What is written

PathWritten byContent
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/population.jsoncompare, firstThe Partition, the Cases and the union of the Episodes: partition, count, cases, episodes.
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/matrix.jsoncompareThe Criteria and, for the Control and the Candidate, each Case with each of its Episodes and the state, score and Reason of every Criterion.
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/paired_deltas.csvcompare, with full coverage onlyOne row per Case: case_id, repeats, control_share, candidate_share, delta_share.
comparisons/COMPARISON_ID/comparison.jsoncompare, lastThe Comparison record (chapter 07), with matrix_ref, primary_criterion, delta, secondary_deltas, rates, declared_factor and descriptive.

Refusals

The refusals of chapter 07 apply, and the four below are new; they are exit 3 with the stage compare and the details of chapter 07. COMPARISON_MODE_DIFFERS also refuses a scripted_fixture side against an llm_live side here.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
COMPARISON_REPEATS_DIFFERDifferent planned Repeats, and repeats not declared3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_TOOLS_DIFFERDifferent tools, and tools not declared3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks
COMPARISON_MODEL_DIFFERSDifferent models, and model not declared3comparecheck, reason, control, candidate, checks

Limits

Native Attempts

The keys a native Attempt records for this chapter (configuration, model, tools, limits, repeats) are written by run agent. The strict path, the repeats and model refusals are witnessed on two recorded llm_live Attempts; the tools and budgets refusals, which no flag of run agent can produce, are witnessed on native manifests built by hand. The model compared is the requested one; the model the log reports is kept beside it as served_model.

Exposure

A Comparison of public_check Episodes is an Exposure and has its journal entry (chapter 12).

Primary Criterion

The primary Criterion is the one of the Control's Assessment. Two Assessments scored under two primary Criteria are not told apart by a check; the Protocol of a Study freezes one.

Latency and cost

Latency and tokens come from an Inspect log, imported or recorded by run agent, and are null for a JSONL import and for an Episode the log gives none for; cost is never compared.

Compatible with a warning

A compatible Comparison can hold a warning, as two native Attempts run one after the other hold COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATED and a side that a Declared deviation covers holds COMPARISON_DEVIATION_DECLARED (chapter 17), so compatible does not mean that warnings is empty (the warning codes are in chapter 07).

Overlapping Attempts

Two native Attempts whose intervals overlap, neither finished before the other started, get no derived COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATED: the warning is derived only for intervals that are disjoint.

An Import side

A Comparison with an Import side gets no COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATED: an Import's manifest records its import time, not when its outputs were produced, so its order is not established, and the Comparison is already descriptive.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the agent Comparison is a core module that reads Assessments and never Inspect.
0002Why the primary Criterion is declared by the Workload and the main delta is on it.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.

The tests are tests/test_compare_agent.py, tests/test_run_agent_compare.py (two native Attempts recorded with Inspect's mock model) and, for the tables of this chapter, tests/test_handbook.py; they run offline and call no model. The Inspect logs they import are written by Inspect's mock model.

12

Core

Freeze, Exposure and check_informed

A Study may use its public_check Partition, and it records every use. Before the first use in a Study revision, the revision holds a freeze record: the digests of its Protocol, of every Configuration the Workload declares, of the environment and of the code. Every Assessment or Comparison that touches public_check Items, native or imported, for either Track, is an Exposure and is appended to the Study's journal. What the first Exposure may have informed is marked check_informed. A Study with no journal entry says that it recorded no Exposure and that any earlier exposure is unknown; nothing here claims that public_check was never seen.

What the commands do

assess, compare and report (chapter 14) record Exposures; a report that shows public_check Items records one even with no Assessment. assess and compare do the work, in src/hypotrail/ml/assess.py, src/hypotrail/agentic/assess.py, src/hypotrail/core/comparison.py and src/hypotrail/core/agent_comparison.py; the freeze record, the journal and the marks are in src/hypotrail/core/exposure.py, a core module (ADR 0001). The three commands take an optional --examples-dir, read only when the Items are public_check ones, to digest the Workload as it is declared now (default: the checkout's examples/, as chapter 04 says).

  1. The usual checks run first (chapters 05, 07, 08 and 11). A refusal there writes nothing.
  2. For public_check Items, the Workload's Protocol is digested again and compared with the one the current revision froze. A difference is refused with PROTOCOL_CHANGED (exit 3) and nothing is written: a Protocol change is made with init --study DIR --new-revision (chapter 10).
  3. If the revision has no freeze record, it is written now, before anything is computed on public_check Items. It is never rewritten, and a revision gets its own at its own first use.
  4. One journal entry is appended, pointing to that freeze record, and only then are the Items scored or paired. The entry is published atomically under the writer lock and never rewritten. An Assessment lists its Attempt and its own ID; a Comparison lists the Attempts and the Assessments of both sides and its own ID.
  5. The Workload's current digests are read through a function the entry point passes in (the Track's freeze_basis), so the core imports no Track. A Configuration's digest itself is computed by one function of the core, exposure.configuration_digest, which freeze_basis of each Track calls and which check_informed calls on an Attempt's manifest, so the two cannot drift. A library call that records an Exposure without passing that function (a Comparison of public_check Items through core.comparison.compare with no live_basis) is refused with WORKLOAD_BASIS_REQUIRED (exit 2) and nothing is written. The basis function is called with the StudySpec the command read under the writer lock, so the digests and the revision they are compared with come from the same read. For a Chinook Study the database part of the Protocol digest is the digest of the Study's own copy: neither import agent nor assess opens or hashes the live file, so a replaced database goes unnoticed by them; init, init --new-revision and data verify are what catch it. The freeze record says so in dataset_source.

An entry written for a Comparison that is then refused at its coverage check stays: the Items were read, and the journal is not rewritten. Its Comparison ID names no Comparison. run ml on public_check is not an Exposure: it produces predictions and scores nothing.

# an Attempt on public_check, then its Assessment: the first Exposure of the Study
uv run python -m hypotrail run ml --study runs/bike --config seasonal_7 --partition public_check --request-id ml-public-a
uv run python -m hypotrail assess --study runs/bike --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID --scorer regression_mae_v1 --references REFERENCES_ID
# the freeze record, the journal and every check_informed mark
uv run python -m hypotrail status --study runs/bike --json

Where public_check is allowed

Inside a Study, assess and compare accept public_check Items for both Tracks: the 184 Bike Items for seasonal_7 and ridge_v1, and the 12 Chinook public_check Cases of an imported file, three Repeats each. A Partition other than dev and public_check (train) is refused as before. An entry point that has no Study keeps its refusal, because it has no journal to write to.

Entry pointIn a StudyOn public_checkRefusal code without a Study
assess (bike and Chinook)yesallowed; one journal entrynone
compare (bike and Chinook)yesallowed; one journal entrynone
reportyesallowed; one journal entry when the page shows public_check Itemsnone
run mlyesallowed, not an Exposure: no score is computednone
hypotrail.ml.metrics.compute_metricsnorefusedPUBLIC_CHECK_METRICS_REFUSED
hypotrail.ml.comparison.compare (two predictions files)norefusedPUBLIC_CHECK_COMPARISON_REFUSED
rescore.rescore_episodes and rescore.rescore_lognorefusedPUBLIC_CHECK_RESCORING_REFUSED
counts.tallynorefusedPUBLIC_CHECK_COUNTS_REFUSED

Inside a Study, the rescoring and the counts of the agent Track are those of assess: it scores each Episode and counts the Criteria. The library functions of chapter 03 take no Study, so they keep their refusal.

The freeze record

One file per revision, revisions/N/freeze.json, beside that revision's StudySpec. It holds the digest of the Protocol, of each Configuration the Workload declares (seasonal_7 and ridge_v1 for the Bike, plain and verify for Chinook), of the environment (Python and the dependency lock) and of the code (revision and dirty state). A Configuration's digest is the one of its declared content, as an Attempt records it in effective_config.configuration.declared; a Chinook Configuration's also covers the system text it resolves to, so a change to plain changes verify too.

FieldMeaning

code.revisionThe commit, or null with no commit
code.dirtyTrue when src/, pyproject.toml or uv.lock differ from the commit, or when that cannot be known

The journal

One file per entry, exposures/SEQ-ENTRY_ID.json, SEQ being its six-digit position. The journal belongs to the Study, not to a revision: its first entry is the Study's first Exposure, whatever the revision.

FieldMeaning
kindexposure
schema_versionexposure/1

attempt_idsThe Attempts whose Observation sets were scored or compared

PathWritten byContent

exposures/SEQ-ENTRY_ID.jsonevery assess, compare or report on public_check ItemsOne journal entry. Never rewritten.

check_informed

Two things are marked. A revision created after the Study's first Exposure is marked, and every later one too; each revision takes its own freeze record at its own first use of public_check; earlier revisions and their records stay readable and unchanged. A Configuration whose digest is not in the freeze record written before the Study's first Exposure, whatever the revision, marks every Attempt, Assessment and Comparison that uses it. A Configuration changed or added after that point is a new revision of the Configuration, identified by its new digest: it is not refused and needs no new Study revision, which is for Protocol changes only. A Configuration the freeze holds unchanged is not marked.

An Import has no Configuration digest, so it is never marked through a Configuration, and its producer's exposure to public_check stays unknown (saw_public_check, chapters 06 and 08). The marks are shown by status and, in a report, by #10.

RecordKind in statusMarked when
Study revision (study.json)revisionIt is created after the Study's first Exposure
Attempt manifestattemptIts Configuration's digest is not in the first freeze record, or it is marked already
AssessmentassessmentIts Attempt is marked, or its Attempt's Configuration is one the first freeze record does not hold
ComparisoncomparisonA side's Assessment or Attempt is marked, or a side's Configuration is one the first freeze record does not hold

What status shows

status reads these records and writes nothing. With no journal entry, it reports zero Exposures recorded in this Study and says that any earlier exposure is unknown.

Key of status --jsonContent
freeze_recordsThe freeze records, one per revision that has used public_check
exposuresThe journal
check_informedEvery marked record, as kind and id (a revision also has revision_number)
exposures.recorded_in_this_studyThe number of entries
exposures.earlier_exposureunknown, always: no local lock proves that the user or an Import's producer never saw public_check
exposures.statementThat sentence in words
exposures.entriesThe journal entries, in order

Refusals

The refusals of chapters 05, 07, 08 and 11 apply as they are, except that ASSESSMENT_PUBLIC_CHECK_REFUSED and, inside a Study, PUBLIC_CHECK_COMPARISON_REFUSED are gone. A Comparison of a dev side and a public_check side is refused as two populations (COMPARISON_POPULATION_DIFFERS). STUDY_LOCKED (exit 6) and the integrity refusals are those of chapter 04. One code is new. With --json, the error has code, message, stage and details, and stdout holds one object.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
PROTOCOL_CHANGEDAn assess or compare on public_check Items finds that the Workload's Protocol is not the current revision's. run and import refuse the same fault at the stage request (chapter 04)3exposureexpected_protocol_digest, actual_protocol_digest

Limits

Not a proof of non-exposure

No lock can prove that the user never read the public CSV elsewhere, and a new Study is not evidence that public_check was unseen. The journal records what this Study did.

A report for both Tracks

The report of a Bike or a Chinook Study has an Exposure section and shows the check_informed marks (chapter 14); the live Campaign on public_check was run on 2026-11-21, and its Study, with its Exposure journal and its report, is kept in docs/handbook/traces/campaign-public-check (chapter 16). No path here calls a model.

A revision is not marked into its Assessments

An Assessment made under a check_informed revision is not itself marked unless a Configuration marks it. The revision's own mark is in status.

Exposure before the score

The entry is written before the Items are scored or paired, so a crash between the entry and the Assessment leaves an entry whose Assessment ID names no Assessment. The journal errs on the side of recording.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the freeze record, the journal and the Assessment and Comparison that write them are core, and import neither scikit-learn nor Inspect.
0002Why the Partitions have fixed roles, and why a Configuration, which the Workload declares, is digested apart from the Protocol.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.

The tests are tests/test_exposure.py and the tables above are compared with the code in tests/test_handbook.py; they run offline and call no model.

Recording an Exposure without a Workload

A code of the Exposure entry points that the table of refusals above does not list.

CodeWhenExitStagedetails
WORKLOAD_BASIS_REQUIREDA command that records an Exposure was given no way to read the Workload, whose digests the freeze record needs. Nothing is written.2exposurenone
13

Agent

Running the agent into a Study

run agent records a native Attempt of the tool-using agent Track in a Chinook Study, from the Inspect log of the Attempt, the way run ml records a Bike Attempt (chapter 04): the same request identity and replay, the same running-then-terminal manifest, the same atomic artifacts, the same writer lock, status and recover. The order of its checks is the one of the order paragraph of chapter 04, where run agent differs from run ml. It has two Modes. scripted_fixture runs the Workload's seven technical scenarios with Inspect's mock model and the real tools, and records the scenario Attempt: a test of the instrument, with a labelled self-check, that is neither assessed nor compared. llm_live runs one Configuration on one Partition (dev or public_check), optionally on some of its Cases, for one to three Repeats, against an explicit Anthropic model; it is opt-in, bounded and paid, and it is never part of the tests.

The command

--mode, not --profile, and --config, not --variant (chadlis/hypotrail#1). --config is required in both Modes: an Attempt runs exactly one Configuration, and the scenario scripts ignore its system text but the Attempt records it. A Study of the temporal regression Track is refused (UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY, exit 2). A flag that belongs to the other Mode is refused too, with the same code: there is no fallback.

FlagRequiredModeMeaning
--study DIRyesbothThe Study to record the Attempt in.
--modeyesbothscripted_fixture or llm_live.
--config NAMEyesbothThe declared Configuration (plain or verify).
--request-id IDyesbothNames the request (below).
--partition NAMEwith llm_livellm_livedev or public_check.
--repeats Nwith llm_livellm_liveAn integer from 1 to 3.
--model MODELwith llm_livellm_liveAn explicit anthropic/<model>; there is no default.
--cases CASE_IDnollm_liveA Case of the Partition, one per flag (repeatable); without it, every Case of the Partition.
--episode-cost-limit-usd USDwith --price-listllm_liveThe cost limit of each Episode in USD, a number above 0, enforced by Inspect's per-sample cost_limit. An Episode stopped by it is failed with the Reason cost_limit, every Criterion scored false (chapter 03). Inspect checks it before every model call and after it, so an Episode can overshoot it by the one call in flight. Without it there is no cost limit.
--price-list FILEwith --episode-cost-limit-usdllm_liveThe prices of the model, read from the provider's price page on the day: {"source": {"url": ..., "read_on": "YYYY-MM-DD"}, "models": {MODEL: {"input": ..., "output": ..., "input_cache_write": ..., "input_cache_read": ...}}}, in USD per million tokens. Both keys are checked, the requested model must be priced, and only models is given to Inspect, as its model_cost_config. run.json records the content, its sha256, the URL and the date.
--retry-of ATTEMPT_IDnobothThe earlier Attempt this one repeats on purpose; the earlier one is left unchanged.
--read-sequence NnobothThe write sequence the author read (status --json): when the Study was written to since, the command is refused (STUDY_CHANGED) and writes nothing (chapter 04). Not given, nothing is checked.
--examples-dir DIRnobothWhere the Workloads are (default: the checkout's examples/; any other install gives the flag, chapter 04).
--jsonnobothStdout holds exactly one JSON object, {"ok": true, "replayed": ..., "attempt": MANIFEST, "observation_set_id": ..., "warnings": [...]} or {"ok": false, "error": {...}}; the human diagnostics go to stderr.
# the scenarios: Inspect's mock model, the real tools, no key, no network
uv run python -m hypotrail run agent --study runs/chinook --mode scripted_fixture --config plain --request-id fixture-a
# opt-in and paid: one Configuration on dev, two Cases, one Repeat
export HYPOTRAIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...
uv run python -m hypotrail run agent --study runs/chinook --mode llm_live --config plain --partition dev --cases D03 --cases D05 --repeats 1 --model anthropic/<model> --request-id live-a

The order

Nothing is run before the checks that precede it, and the first refusal ends the command. The order is the one of the decision on chadlis/hypotrail#74:

  1. The argument checks (exit 2). First, on the argument alone, before the Study or the Workload is read: --read-sequence when given must be a non-negative integer (INVALID_ARGUMENTS). Then the flag checks, against the Workload's declaration only: the flags of the other Mode, the Configuration, the Partition (dev or public_check), the Repeats (1 to 3), the model (anthropic/<model>), the cost limit and the price list (each requires the other; the price list must price the model), and the Cases (each a Case of the Partition, none repeated). They come before the key is read and before Inspect is imported. Together with the Repeats bound they make the bound of the Attempt: at most the Partition's Cases times three Repeats, that is 24 Episodes on dev. spike live keeps its own bound of two Episodes on dev.
  2. The writer lock (STUDY_LOCKED, exit 6): a second writer is refused at once. Then --retry-of must name an Attempt of the Study (RETRY_OF_UNKNOWN, exit 2).
  3. The request is identified (below). The same request_id with the same request returns the recorded Attempt here: no key, no Inspect, no provider call, no scan. With another request it is REQUEST_CONFLICT. The identity is taken before the data is verified, so a database changed on disk is a changed request (the file's digest differs), and a References set changed by a new Study revision is too.
  4. When --read-sequence is given, it is compared with the Study's write sequence, under the lock and straight after the identity (chapter 04): older, STUDY_CHANGED (exit 6); above, WRITE_SEQUENCE_AHEAD (exit 2). Nothing is written. A replay never reaches this step.
  5. The Workload's data is verified against its lock (DATA_INTEGRITY, exit 3), and every Study artifact read is read back against its digest (ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY, exit 3).
  6. For a new request, the Workload's Protocol must still be the Study's (PROTOCOL_CHANGED, exit 3).
  7. Inspect must be importable (INSPECT_UNAVAILABLE, exit 5), in either Mode; then, for llm_live, HYPOTRAIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY must be set and no variable that would override it (LIVE_UNAVAILABLE, exit 5). Nothing is sent without the key. scripted_fixture never reads the key.
  8. The manifest is written running, the Episodes are run, the artifacts are published atomically and the terminal manifest is written last.

An Attempt stopped between its running manifest and its terminal one keeps what it published and is never shown as completed. status shows it as recoverable and recover records it interrupted, with outcome_unknown: true for llm_live, since a vanished lock does not prove that the provider call was cancelled. Nothing is resubmitted. A recorded Attempt may hold failed Episodes (an error of the provider, a limit): it is completed, exit 0, and the failed Episode has a Reason and no invented answer.

Exit 0 is a recorded Attempt, whatever its Episodes' outcomes; 2 is an input the command refuses, 3 an integrity or Protocol refusal, 4 an infrastructure failure, 5 a capability that is not available, 6 a lock held or a request_id that names another request. Every error has code, message, stage and details, and none holds a secret.

The request identity

The elements are those of chapter 04, and the agent Track fills them all: the command (run agent), the Workload, the Configuration, the Partition, the Cases, the Mode, the model, the Repeats, the digests of the input files, and the code revision; an llm_live request with a cost limit adds the cost limit by its value (cost_limit_usd) and the price list by the sha256 of its bytes (price_list_sha256), which a request without them, and one recorded before they existed, reads as null. The Configuration is held as it is recorded, its id, its declared content and its resolved system text, so a changed plain changes the request of verify, which extends it, and a change to another Configuration of the Workload leaves a replay a replay. The Cases are the Partition's Cases in the file's order: --cases omitted and every Case listed (in any order) are one request. The model is the one requested. For scripted_fixture the Partition and the Cases are null, the Repeats are 1 and the model is mockllm/model. The input digests are in the table below; a request that changes only one element is refused with that element's name.

What an Attempt records

The manifest is an attempt/1 record with the operation execute, the Mode, an attempt_id that is a UUID, and the code_environment of run ml. An llm_live manifest also keeps, outside effective_config, the digest of each Case it evaluates (case_digests, chapter 04), which compare reads. Its effective_config holds the keys below. model is the requested model and served_model the one the provider reports in the Episodes' outputs (output.model, the source of served_models in run.json; it is not the name the Inspect eval was given, which Inspect fills from the requested model). Both are always recorded side by side, as given: model with its provider prefix (anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5), served_model as the outputs report it, without one (claude-sonnet-5-5), and a provider prefix alone is not a difference. The output without --json shows both names on its first line, as recorded (model anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5, served_model claude-sonnet-5-5), also for an Attempt recorded earlier, and computes no flag from them. Several reported models are joined by a comma and a space, and none reported is null. limits are the effective limits read back from the log (the turn, time and output-token limits, the per-query caps, a concurrency of 1, the cache off, no retry, the cost limit, null without one, and parallel_tool_calls false: one tool call per turn is requested from the provider), and limit_notes states beside them what Inspect cannot enforce: the number of tool calls. An llm_live Attempt made with --episode-cost-limit-usd and --price-list also holds, with its running manifest, the two elements of the request identity they add, cost_limit_usd and price_list_sha256; an Attempt made without them holds neither key, so the table below lists the keys every Attempt holds. effective_parameters holds the reasoning setting and the output cap as the log recorded them in the request, and null for what it does not show. check_informed marks an Attempt whose Configuration the freeze record does not hold, once the Study has had its first Exposure; an llm_live Attempt on public_check scores nothing and is not itself an Exposure.

Key of effective_configWrittenHolds
commandwith the running manifestrun agent
workloadwith the running manifestThe Workload's name.
modewith the running manifestscripted_fixture or llm_live.
partitionwith the running manifestdev or public_check; null for the scenarios.
caseswith the running manifestThe Cases of the Partition the Attempt runs; null for the scenarios.
repeatswith the running manifest1 to 3; 1 for the scenarios.
modelwith the running manifestThe requested model.
configurationwith the running manifest{id, declared, system_text}: its digest, without the id, is the one the freeze record holds.
protocolwith the running manifestThe Protocol's id and digest.
input_digestswith the running manifestSee the next table.
served_modelwith the terminal manifestThe model the Episodes' outputs report (output.model), as served_models of run.json.

limitswith the terminal manifestThe effective limits, read back from the log.
limit_noteswith the terminal manifesttool_calls (Inspect has no such limit) and provider_calls.
