The files of an Attempt

Inspect owns the agent loop and its log. The log is written outside the Study, into a folder that is removed at the end; it is scanned for the key, then read through Inspect's public log API (never parsed by hand, ADR 0001) and kept in the Attempt as a raw artifact. The Observation set (origin generated, the Attempt's Mode) is projected from it: one Observation per expected Episode, produced, failed with its Reason, or missing (missing_from_log), with the final answer and the trace. Each Repeat is its own Inspect epoch. Tokens and cost are null where the log gives none, never 0. The population is a {"episodes": [...]} document of the Attempt (the selected Cases times the Repeats, all of one Partition), read by assess and compare as they read an Import's. No Reference is passed to the model, and the Chinook file is hashed before and after the Attempt: a difference is DATABASE_CHANGED and the Attempt is failed.

File under attempts/ATTEMPT_ID/RoleModeListed in
attempt.jsonthe manifestbothnone
workload.jsonworkload_declarationbothinput_refs
population.jsonrun_populationbothoutput_refs
inspect/<log>.evalinspect_logbothoutput_refs
run.jsonrun_recordbothoutput_refs
self_check.jsonself_checkscripted_fixtureoutput_refs
observations.jsonlobservationsbothoutput_refs
observation_set.jsonobservation_setbothoutput_refs

run.json is the run record of chapter 03, with every field the log holds taken from the log. For an llm_live Attempt it is the record #62 defines (request_source is this_attempt, the database hashes are the Attempt's own); for a scenario Attempt it is the library's record, with the banner.

The key is never kept

The key is read from HYPOTRAIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and nowhere else, and is given to the provider explicitly. For an llm_live Attempt, every file the Attempt writes, the running and the terminal manifests included, is scanned for the key before it is written. A hit before the running manifest (a request_id that is the key, say) writes nothing and leaves no Attempt, exit 4, and the error has no warnings, since no scan of the Study is made. A later hit leaves the file unwritten, ends the Attempt failed with the Reason SECRET_IN_OUTPUT and exits 4; no file of the Study holds the key. After the Attempt the whole Study folder is scanned (the compressed logs are searched through Inspect's log API): a hit in a file that is no part of this Attempt is a warning on stderr and in the warnings list of the --json object, naming the file, and changes no recorded Attempt; the exit stays 0. The scan is made whether or not the Attempt failed: after a failed Attempt the same warnings are on stderr and in error.details.warnings of the --json object, and the exit is the error's. A replay runs no scan.

The scenario Attempt

scripted_fixture runs every scenario of the Workload once (SCN-01 to SCN-07, SCN-07 never run, so missing), through the real tools and the same task, projection and Scorer code as a live Attempt, with the model mockllm/model. Its Observation set holds the seven scenario Episodes and no Episode of a Workload Case; its population is an artifact of the Attempt, not the Study's memberships, which hold no scenario. self_check.json keeps the status of each scenario and the counters of each Criterion against the scenario file's own references (7 expected, 6 attempted, 5 produced, each scenario moving a different combination, as chapter 03 sets out). It is a labelled self-check, not an Assessment: it carries the Mode and the banner "test simulation, no LLM evaluated", which is also in the Inspect log header, it is never of the kind assessment, and no Assessment ID names it. assess on its Observation set is refused with UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY (exit 2) as the first check once the Observation set is found, before any check of its content, so compare can never see a scenario Attempt. The report that renders the banner is not built yet.

Two native Attempts, compared

The tests record two llm_live Attempts with the same code path and Inspect's mock model in place of the provider (plain and verify, the same dev Cases, Repeats, tools and limits), assess them with chinook_contract_v1 and compare them: the Comparison is compatible, strict, with configuration as the declared factor and no warning, and the Assessment's verdicts are those rescore_log gives on the same kept log. assess and compare run with the model, the tools and the Chinook file patched to fail. Two such Attempts that differ in their Repeats or in their requested model are refused with the code of that check unless --factor declares it. The patch is the tests' own: no flag of the command reaches it, and no such log is kept as a trace or presented as an LLM's result.

The paid Attempt

One paid Attempt was made for this slice, on 2026-11-20, after the exact command had passed offline, into a fresh Study made by init --workload chinook: uv run python -m hypotrail run agent --study STUDY --mode llm_live --config plain --partition dev --cases D03 --cases D05 --repeats 1 --model anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5 --request-id live-check-1 --json. The command exited 0 and recorded an Attempt of status completed with two Episodes, D03 and D05, both produced, no failed or missing Episode and no warning. The requested model is anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5 and the served_model the outputs report is claude-sonnet-5-5. run.json records, per Episode, 373 output tokens and 10,299 total tokens for D03, and 385 output tokens and 10,472 total tokens for D05, in three model calls each; the total counts the cached input tokens. The cost is not observable: cost is null in both Episodes and total_cost is null in their model_usage, so no price is stated here. In both Episodes uses_tool_result is null with the Reason answer_unreadable, and the Chinook file has the same SHA-256 before and after. The Episodes are not scored against References here.

The folder of the Attempt is kept as it was recorded under docs/handbook/traces/run-agent-live-plain/: the manifest (attempt.json), the Observation set (observation_set.json), its observations (observations.jsonl), the population, the Workload declaration, run.json and the Inspect log under inspect/, with the command's own --json output, command-output.json (ok true, replayed false, warnings empty), which backs the exit and the absence of warnings stated above. No file of it holds the key: the files and the decoded log were searched for its value before they were committed. It is a dated observation of one Attempt and says nothing about main once the code changes; the traces of spike live are those of chapter 03. The 72-Episode Campaign on public_check, two Attempts (plain and verify) with their Assessments, their Comparison and the report of the Study, is kept whole in docs/handbook/traces/campaign-public-check/; chapter 16 gives its account.

Limits

Tool calls are not capped

Inspect has no limit on the number of tool calls; the manifest says so in limit_notes. The turn limit bounds them indirectly.

The Campaign ran once

The 72-Episode Campaign on public_check is two invocations of this command, run on 2026-11-21 and kept as they were recorded; its order of Configurations is not alternated (chapter 16).

One provider

Only anthropic/<model> is accepted, with the key sent to that provider alone. The model licence line of the specification does not apply to a remote provider, and no field records one.

A scan is not a proof

The scan finds the key as written, in a file or decoded from a log; it does not find the key in an altered form.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0004Why run agent takes --read-sequence and is refused with STUDY_CHANGED when the Study was written to since the sequence was read.
14

Report

The Study report

report renders one HTML page for a Study of either Track, the temporal regression Track (Bike) or the tool-using agent Track (Chinook), from the Study's records alone: the Attempts, Observation sets, Assessments, Comparisons, the freeze record and the Exposure journal. The page holds its text, styles and tables inline, loads nothing, has no script and computes nothing when it is opened. A page that shows public_check Items is an Exposure and is recorded as one.

The command

python -m hypotrail report --study DIR --out FILE, with --json, --examples-dir DIR and --read-sequence N (the flags are in the commands table of chapter 04). The code is src/hypotrail/report/: gather.py reads and verifies the records (gather_agent.py adds what a Chinook Study holds), render.py writes the page and its sections (render_agent.py writes the sections that differ for a Chinook Study, markup.py holds the escaping building blocks), command.py is the command. It imports neither Inspect nor scikit-learn nor NumPy (ADR 0001), and it calls no fit, no prediction, no model and no tool, so it works with the network cut. An Inspect log is never opened: the page links to it.

  1. The Study is read, whichever Track it is of. --out must not be inside the Study, nor a directory, and its folder must exist.
  2. Every artifact the page shows is read back against its digest: a changed byte is ARTIFACT_INTEGRITY (exit 3) and no page is written.
  3. If the page shows public_check Items, the Study's writer lock is taken (a second writer is STUDY_LOCKED, exit 6), --read-sequence, when given, is compared with the Study's write sequence (STUDY_CHANGED, exit 6, or WRITE_SEQUENCE_AHEAD, exit 2; nothing is written, no Exposure and no page), the Workload is found through the one resolver of chapter 04 and its Protocol is checked against the revision's (PROTOCOL_CHANGED, exit 3), the freeze record is written first if the revision has none, then one journal entry names the Attempts, Assessments and Comparisons the page shows of those Items (chapter 12). A Study whose Items are all dev records nothing, takes no lock, reads no Workload and does not compare --read-sequence.
  4. The page is written to a temporary file beside --out, fsynced and renamed onto it, and the folder of --out is fsynced after the rename: an existing file is replaced.

The page is rebuilt from the Study each time it is asked for; it is never stored as a record. Two reports of the same public_check Study record two Exposures.

# a page for a Study; --out is outside the Study
uv run python -m hypotrail report --study runs/bike --out runs/bike-report.html
uv run python -m hypotrail report --study runs/chinook --out runs/chinook-report.html

The sections

The nine sections of the specification, in its order, then the Exposure section. Each has a stable id. The page's own headings, labels and sentences are in English; a string that comes from outside the code (a question, an ID, a declared field) is shown as given, HTML-escaped, in text and in attributes, and a web address in it is text, never a link.

OrderIdHeadingWhat it shows
1question-modeQuestion and ModeThe question and objective of the Study, its Track, its Primary Metric, the Modes of its Attempts.
2data-licencesData and licencesThe dataset the Study keeps, with its digest, and the licence notice file the Workload names. The notice text stays with the Workload: the page reads the Study alone.
3protocol-partitionsProtocol and PartitionsThe Protocol and revision, and each Partition's Items and date range (Bike), or its Cases (Chinook), read from the Study's population.
4configurationsConfigurations comparedEach Attempt's Configuration and its declared content (Chinook: its id, declared content and exact system text, and an llm_live Attempt's recorded facts); an Import declares none.
5coverage-statusesCoverage and statusesEach Attempt with its Mode, technical status, request_id, outcome_unknown and its produced, failed and missing Items; each Assessment's counts and, per Criterion, its states and Reasons; for a scenario Attempt, its self-check. A null is shown as unknown, never as 0.
6metrics-differencesMetrics and differencesEach Assessment's Metric, its revision, the earlier Assessment it differs from and the recorded cause; a table by month (Bike) or the counters and the two named rates per Criterion (Chinook); each Comparison.
7individual-errorsIndividual errorsBike: the ten Items with the largest absolute error of each Assessment, with date, true and predicted values, and where each is found. Chinook: the Case by Repeat matrix of each Assessment, and each Attempt's answers shown as text.
8limitsLimitsThe limitations the StudySpec, the Assessments and the Comparisons record.
9artifact-originOrigin of the artifactsEach artifact with its relative link, SHA-256, size and role; an Import's producer fields with their origin, declared or unknown.
10exposureExposureThe count of Exposures this Study recorded, the journal, the statement that any earlier exposure is unknown, and every record marked check_informed.

The tenth section is not one of the nine of the specification: the ticket asks for it, and it sits after them so that the nine keep their order.

Wherever a section lists the Assessments of the Study, it lists them in the order of the later Assessment (chapter 10).

The tool-using agent Track's page

A Chinook Study has the same page, sections and rules. Where Bike shows a table by month and the ten largest errors, Chinook shows, for each Assessment, a matrix with one row per Case and one column per Repeat. Each cell names its Case and Repeat and holds the lines of the table below. An Instrument failure (a Criterion unscored or errored, with its Reason) is written apart from a format failure of the system (a Criterion scored false, with its Reason): the first is never held against the evaluated system. A Wrapped answer is named as such and carries the Reason invalid_response_format. The trace line is a relative link to the Attempt's Inspect log with its SHA-256 (the log itself is never opened); for an Import of a JSON Lines file it links the import source, which holds the steps; an Episode with no trace says trace absent.

OrderLineWhat it says
1Case and RepeatThe Case ID and the Repeat the cell stands for.
2Executionproduced, or failed or missing with its Reason (unknown when none is recorded).
3Answer read fromFor a produced answer: raw_answer, embedded_object (Wrapped answer) or nothing read.
4exact_tabletrue or false with its Reason, or unscored or errored with its Reason and the words Instrument failure.
5format_validAs above.
6evidence_link_validAs above (the tool evidence).
7contract_successAs above (the primary Criterion).
8TraceThe Study-relative path and SHA-256 of the Inspect log, linked; Trace in the import source for a JSON Lines Import; trace absent when the Episode has none.

For each Assessment the page also gives, per Criterion, the counters of the contract and the two named rates (CMP-03, CMP-04), read from the Assessment record and its detailed metrics file. A rate whose denominator is zero is shown null with its Reason, never 0, and the unknowns (unscored, errored, and their Reasons counted from the per-Item file) stand beside the rates.

OrderColumn
1Criterion
2N_expected
3N_attempted
4N_produced
5N_scored
6N_correct
7N_incorrect
8N_unscored
9N_errored
10Quality among the scored (N_correct / N_scored)
11Share of the planned Episodes with a confirmed success (N_correct / N_expected)
12Unknowns

An agent Comparison is shown as recorded: its Control and Candidate Assessments, its comparability (with the code of an order warning, and of a Declared-deviation warning, COMPARISON_DEVIATION_DECLARED, beside the status, chapter 11) and the checks behind it, its coverage and coverage rates, its deltas and the definition of the delta (for this Criterion a positive delta means the Candidate is better), and, aggregated by Case first, the shares of each Case from the paired-deltas file. When the record withholds the main delta (CMP-05) the page shows only withheld, with the coverage rates beside it and no bounds; no interval is computed over Episodes. Its descriptive measures show a null as unknown.

A Study that holds a scenario Attempt (Mode scripted_fixture) shows the banner at the top of the page, before the first section, whatever else the Study holds: "test simulation, no LLM evaluated" is the text of the banner, and it names the scenario Attempts it covers by their IDs. Every element the page writes for a scenario Attempt carries a data-attempt-id attribute equal to that ID, and its text names no LLM. Its statuses and counts are shown under the heading Self-check of the scripted scenarios, not an Assessment: a scenario Attempt is neither assessed nor compared. Its final answers are shown as text like every answer. The page states no milestone status: the live acceptance status is shown by the grid and the README, not by a report of one Study.

An llm_live Attempt is shown with these fields beside its Configuration, each as the Attempt recorded it; a value it does not hold is shown unknown, never 0 (the served model is never replaced by the requested one). The tokens, cost and latency of each Episode follow.

OrderFieldWhere it comes from
1Model (requested)The model the Attempt asked for.
2Served model (reported)The served_model the provider reported, beside the requested one.
3Effective limitsThe limits read back from the log.
4Limit note, <name>One row for each note of limit_notes, such as the number of tool calls Inspect does not enforce.
5Effective parametersThe parameters the log shows were sent.
6Chinook file SHA-256 beforeThe run record's hash of the database before the Episodes.
7Chinook file SHA-256 afterThe same, after.

What the numbers are

Every value is written into the HTML when the page is rendered. The values the records hold (Metrics, counters, deltas, coverage) are read as recorded. Two tables hold values the records do not: the table by month (the Items of the month and the mean of the Assessment's per-Item absolute errors, with the number scored) and the Reasons per Criterion (the per-Item states, counted). Both are computed from the recorded per-Item file when the page is rendered, never when it is opened. The ten largest errors are ranked by absolute error, the larger first and equal errors by Item ID; the difference column is predicted minus true.

An Assessment's card shows, beside its Metric, its single-feature check as recorded (chapter 05): the lag features that beat it, each with its Metric; checked: no declared lag feature beats this Assessment when the field lists none; not checked when the record has no such field. A Comparison is shown with its Control and Candidate Assessments, its comparability status (with the code of a single-feature warning, and of a Declared-deviation warning, COMPARISON_DEVIATION_DECLARED, beside it, chapter 07) and warnings, its coverage and its deltas as recorded. A positive delta means the Candidate is worse. A Comparison against an Import is shown as descriptive, with its warning. An Attempt that is running is shown as running, whether or not its writer is alive, and an interrupted one as interrupted.

What the page does not say

The page never attributes readiness, safety or independence to an output, a Configuration or a Study. The one sentence that names those words denies them; it is listed here so that the test over the page's own text can allow it by its exact text.

Sentence
This report does not state that any output, Configuration or Study is production-ready, safe or independent.

Refusals

CaseCodeExitStageDetails
No Study at --studySTUDY_NOT_FOUND2studystudy
--out inside the Study (inside_study), a directory (is_directory) or in a folder that does not exist (no_folder)REPORT_OUTPUT_INVALID2reportout, reason
An artifact whose bytes no longer match its referenceARTIFACT_INTEGRITY3artifactpath, expected_sha256, actual_sha256, expected_size_bytes, actual_size_bytes
A second writer, while an Exposure is recordedSTUDY_LOCKED6lockstudy
The Workload's Protocol is no longer the revision's, when an Exposure is recordedPROTOCOL_CHANGED3exposureexpected_protocol_digest, actual_protocol_digest

A page that shows public_check Items also needs the Workload: without --examples-dir, outside a checkout, it is refused before anything is written, as every command that reads a Workload is (chapter 04).

Limits

Logs are linked, not read

The page links to an Inspect log with its SHA-256 and shows no event of it (Inspect owns its viewer). A Study that holds a log still renders where Inspect is not installed.

A report is an Exposure

Showing public_check outputs is an Exposure even with no Assessment. The entry is written before the page, so a failure to write the page leaves the entry.

No licence text

The Study keeps the dataset, not the licence notice or its attribution addresses: the page names the notice file the Workload declared and shows no notice text.

No in-page links

The only links are relative links to Study artifacts, from the folder of --out, each with its SHA-256. Moving the page without the Study breaks them.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0004Why report compares --read-sequence only when its page shows public_check Items and so records an Exposure: a page of dev Items only writes nothing.
15

Data

Acquiring the data: data fetch

data fetch acquires a Workload's public sources from the URLs of its data-sources.lock.json. Each file is downloaded to a temporary file, bounded while it arrives, checked against the lock's SHA-256 and published by an atomic rename. It never chooses a mirror or another URL, never replaces a file that is there, and the files it publishes pass data verify like the committed bundle.

The command

python -m hypotrail data fetch --lock LOCK --dest DIR, with --json. With --json, stdout holds exactly one JSON object and the human diagnostic goes to stderr; every error has code, message, stage and details. The code is src/hypotrail/core/fetch.py (standard library only).

FlagRequiredMeaning
--lock LOCKyesThe data-sources.lock.json to acquire. Its sources and their URLs are the only input; no URL is ever taken from anywhere else.
--dest DIRyesThe Workload directory the files are published in: each source lands at its lock's local_path under it, and each licence notice at its notice path.
--jsonnoPrint one JSON object on stdout.
# acquire a checkout's data in place: only a missing file is downloaded
uv run python -m hypotrail data fetch --lock examples/bike_day/data-sources.lock.json --dest examples/bike_day
uv run python -m hypotrail data verify
# a clean install into a directory of its own, then verified there
uv run python -m hypotrail data fetch --lock examples/chinook/data-sources.lock.json --dest my-examples/chinook
uv run python -m hypotrail data verify --examples-dir my-examples --workload chinook

What it does, in order

  1. The lock is read. Not valid JSON, an unknown schema_version (only data-sources/1 is read), or a key the fetch needs that is missing is a refusal with exit 2, before any download. The keys a source needs are dataset_id, download_url (an http or https address), local_path, sha256 and size_bytes; an archive source adds archive_sha256 and member; a source with a notice_url adds notice and notice_sha256.
  2. Every path that is written stays inside --dest. A local_path, a notice or a member that is absolute or holds .., and a destination whose path to the published file passes through a link that leads outside --dest, are refused with exit 2 before any download and with nothing written.
  3. The files already there are compared with the lock, before any connection. A file that matches (SHA-256 and, for a data file, size) is left as it is, is not downloaded again and is reported present. A file that differs is refused with exit 3 and is left unchanged byte for byte: a fetch never replaces a file. To acquire a file again, remove it first. A missing file is downloaded.
  4. Each missing file is downloaded from its lock's download_url and from no other address, to a temporary file in the directory it will be published in. The redirects that URL answers with are followed, at most 5 hops and https only; a redirect to an address that is not https (a downgrade) or a sixth hop is a failed transfer, with no fallback. The command holds no list of allowed hosts: the lock's SHA-256 is the only gate on what arrives, and the timeout and the bounds below apply to the file that finally arrives. The download stops as soon as it passes its bound. Then the SHA-256 (and the size, for a data file) is checked against the lock, and only then is the file published by an atomic rename; the temporary file never outlives the command.
  5. The Bike source is an archive. The archive is checked against archive_sha256 before it is opened; only the lock's member (day.csv) is read from it, and only as a stream into a second temporary file: no archive path is ever joined to the destination. An entry that is a link, or not a regular file, is refused. The extracted file is checked against the lock's sha256 and size_bytes before it is published.
  6. The Chinook licence notice is downloaded from the lock's notice_url and checked against notice_sha256, under the rules above. The Bike lock has no notice_url: its notice is not fetched and is reported absent (the committed notice stays where it is).

The files are published one at a time, each only after its own check. If a later file is refused, the files already published stay, and each of them matches the lock.

Bounds

A data file downloaded directly is bounded by its lock's size_bytes, and the member extracted from an archive by the same size_bytes, whatever the archive says of it. Any download whose lock gives no size (an archive, a notice) is bounded by one generic cap: 67108864 bytes (64 MiB). The cap names no Workload and is the same for every lock. A body that announces a length past its bound is refused before it is read, and one that does not is stopped as soon as the bytes read pass the bound.

The result

With --json: {"ok": true, "sources": [...]}, one entry per source in the lock's order, each with dataset_id, file (the local_path), status (fetched or present), sha256, size_bytes and notice (file and status: fetched, present or absent). data verify then checks the published files with the same checks as the committed bundle: the bytes, the licence notice and the content. A byte altered after a fetch is refused by data verify with exit 3.

There is no automatic mirror. When a transfer fails, the message names the explicit alternative: the already verified bundle, checked with data verify on the committed data of a checkout. A failure reports the final host (the one the request had reached) and never a URL, a path or a query string, so a signed address is never printed.

The command takes the framing of a response from the framing http.client computed for it, and parses no Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding header itself; the framing http.client computed is read before the first read. It reads a body as chunked, or as framed by a length (a Content-Length that is an integer and not negative; a signed +12 is read as 12), or as unframed, with no length, so that the close ends it. It ignores a Content-Length on a chunked body, one that is not a number, and a negative one: such a response is chunked or unframed. A connection cut in the middle of a transfer then ends in one of these ways. A body framed by a length that closed before it arrived, and a chunked body that closed before its last chunk (the client reports an incomplete read), are DOWNLOAD_FAILED with reason interrupted, exit 4. So is an unframed body that delivered fewer bytes than the lock's size_bytes: only fewer bytes than size_bytes triggers it. In all three nothing is published, the temporary file is removed, and the message names the already verified bundle. An unframed body of an equal size (the lock's) with a wrong hash, and a framed body that arrived whole but differs, are DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY, exit 3. The limit: where the lock gives no size (an archive, a notice), an unframed short body cannot be told from a differing one by HTTP; it stays DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY, exit 3, and its message says so and names the already verified bundle (data verify on the committed data) as the explicit alternative. Only that case carries the note that a server may have cut the body short; a framed body that differs does not.

Refusals

CaseCodeExitStageDetails
No file at --lockFILE_NOT_FOUND2configfile
The lock is not valid JSONFILE_NOT_JSON2configfile, line
A schema_version other than data-sources/1LOCK_SCHEMA_UNKNOWN2configfile, schema_version, supported
A key the fetch needs is missing or unusable (key says which, for example sources[0].sha256); a download_url that is not an http or https addressLOCK_INVALID2configfile, key
A local_path, notice or member that is absolute (absolute) or holds .. (parent_reference); a destination path through a link that leads outside --dest (link_outside)PATH_OUTSIDE_DEST2configkey, path, reason
--dest exists and is not a directoryDEST_NOT_A_DIRECTORY2configdest
A file already at the destination does not match the lock; it is left unchanged and nothing is downloadedDEST_FILE_DIFFERS3integrityfile, expected_sha256, actual_sha256
A download (what: file, archive, member or notice) whose SHA-256 or size differs from the lock; size_bytes is the lock's (null where the lock gives none) and actual_size_bytes the bytes that arrived, so a size-only difference states both sizesDOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY3integrityfile, what, expected_sha256, actual_sha256, size_bytes, actual_size_bytes

The lock's member is not in the archive (missing), is a link (link), is not a regular file (not_a_file), or the archive cannot be read (unreadable)ARCHIVE_MEMBER_REFUSED3integrityfile, member, reason
The host is unreachable (unreachable), answers with an HTTP error status (http_status, with status), the connection is cut mid-transfer (interrupted), a redirect leads to an address that is not https (redirect_downgrade), or more than 5 hops are asked for (too_many_redirects); nothing is published and no other source is triedDOWNLOAD_FAILED4downloadfile, host, reason, status

An operating-system error while writing (a full disk, for example) is reported by the command line as INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE with exit 4, as for every command.

Where the tests stand

No test reaches the network. A stand-in server on the loopback interface serves every download, a replaced opener serves the one that must be stopped early, and the network guard of tests/conftest.py stays on. The committed locks name the UCI and GitHub hosts; no test opens them, and the lock's landing_url is never opened by the code. The stand-in speaks http, so the https-only rule is witnessed without TLS: a redirect to the stand-in is refused as a downgrade, a test that follows hops widens the allowed schemes for its own duration, and the redirect handler is also tested directly with https and http targets. The first fetch against a real host is therefore a step an installer does, not one the suite has done.

16

Acceptance

Acceptance grid T01-T24

The first milestone is accepted test by test. SPEC §10 lists 24 acceptance tests, T01 to T24. This chapter has one row per test with what it does, the result it expects, and either the proof that it holds or an explicit not run with its reason. Three statuses are kept apart, each with its own value and its own evidence, and none derived from another: OFFLINE_ACCEPTED, LIVE_ACCEPTED (conditional: References E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending) and USABILITY_REVIEW_PENDING (open).

The three statuses

OFFLINE_ACCEPTED rests on T01 to T19 and T21 to T24 and is computed from the rows of the grid below: it is reached only if each of those rows has a passing proof, and otherwise it is pending and names the rows that hold it back. A row marked not run holds it back. LIVE_ACCEPTED rests on T20 and on the model report of the spike, together with the owner's review of the References, and it is conditional: References E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending. The grid's test computes its value: conditional when T20's row has a passing proof, the spike's report is cited as its evidence and the owner's review of the References is pending, and pending otherwise. USABILITY_REVIEW_PENDING stays open until another person has read the README path.

StatusValueEvidence
OFFLINE_ACCEPTEDreachedRows T01-T19 and T21-T24 of the grid below, each by its own proof; every pytest proof is a node of the suite, so a failing proof makes the suite red.
LIVE_ACCEPTEDconditionalReferences E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending. Row T20 has a passing proof, the Campaign of chadlis/hypotrail#98 kept whole in docs/handbook/traces/campaign-public-check, and the spike's report (chapter 01) is cited with it. The order of the Configurations was not alternated (AG-12 of the L1 specification): that limit is recorded beside the pending owner review (D-028 on chadlis/hypotrail#75). Only the owner lifts the condition.
USABILITY_REVIEW_PENDINGopenIt needs a reading by another person, which has not happened. The T24 walk was done by an agent, not by the owner and not by another reader.

The value LIVE_NOT_VALIDATED of SPEC §0 is for the case where no usable real model exists. That does not hold here: a real model was used (the spike, one paid run agent Attempt and the Campaign, kept in docs/handbook/traces/), so LIVE_ACCEPTED is conditional (References E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending), not LIVE_NOT_VALIDATED.

The grid

Each row's English text renders the row of SPEC §10; whether it renders it faithfully is checked by the review of record, not by a test. A pytest proof names test nodes, and each node declares the row (T05 in its name or its docstring): a collected test that is about another row fails the grid. Whether the content of a test matches what §10 asks of the row is not checked by that declaration; it stays with the review of record. A pytest proof passes if its nodes pass in the same suite as the test that reads this table. The T24 row passes when the walk record exists, passes the test that checks it against the README, and holds what T24 expects: the walker identified, a failure and a limit found, the difficulties recorded. A recorded difficulty does not fail it; a difficulty that kept the walker from finding the failure or the limit does. The T20 row passes when it is a command proof with exit 0, every file it names exists in the repository, the kept outputs hold 72 Episodes (36 per Attempt) with each recorded exit of the Campaign's commands at 0, and it states what the kept files record: the two command lines and their output, the statuses, the tools actually called and the effective limits. T20 is not proven by an Attempt whose Episodes all failed (SPEC §0: a weak result of the model does not block the acceptance of the measurement, but the absence of a real model-tool loop cannot be disguised as a success).

Reading the gridThe T24 walk record is walk-t24.html, beside this handbook. Nodes are given as path::function; a parametrised test stands for all its cases. The rows are checked by tests/test_handbook.py: exactly T01-T24, every named node collected by the suite and declaring its row, and the status table equal to what the rows compute.

T20 and the conditional status

T20 asks for two live join traces and the 72-Episode Campaign. It is proven by the Campaign of chadlis/hypotrail#98, run on 2026-11-21 and kept whole in docs/handbook/traces/campaign-public-check (see below). The row checks the kept files, not the account written here. The two live join traces are one Episode per Configuration on E02, a join Case (D-027 on chadlis/hypotrail#75). A fresh slice-gate read both through Inspect's log API (SPEC AG-10) and recorded in chadlis/hypotrail#93, comment 7057388641, that each holds the sequence model, tool executed, model, final answer.

LIVE_ACCEPTED is conditional: References E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending. It rests on T20 and on the spike's report, which is chapter 01, with its live traces kept in docs/handbook/traces/live-spike, live-l12-plain, live-l12-verify, live-l12b-plain and live-l12b-verify. One paid run agent Attempt (2026-11-20, plain, Cases D03 and D05) is kept whole in docs/handbook/traces/run-agent-live-plain (chapter 13). The earlier live Episodes did not score the 12 public_check Cases against References; the Campaign did. The condition is the owner's review of the References of E01-E10, which is pending, and the deviation from AG-12 of the L1 specification on the order of the Configurations (below), which D-028 on chadlis/hypotrail#75 names beside it. Only the owner lifts the condition; no one else changes the value.

The live Campaign

The Campaign is 12 Cases x 3 Repeats x 2 Configurations = 72 Episodes on public_check. run agent runs one Configuration per invocation, so it was two invocations, one with --config plain and one with --config verify, with the same model and limits for both, the response cache off and retries at zero (the limits of the Track, chapter 03). Each Attempt was then assessed, the two Assessments were compared with plain as the Control and verify as the Candidate, and the Study was reported. Running on public_check is an Exposure (chapter 12). The table gives the command lines as they ran, with the placeholders they were filled from (the output of the step before and status --json, chapter 04); T20's row holds the filled lines with their recorded output.

StepCommand
1. plain invocationuv run python -m hypotrail run agent --study DIR --mode llm_live --config plain --partition public_check --repeats 3 --model anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5 --request-id REQUEST_ID_PLAIN
2. verify invocationuv run python -m hypotrail run agent --study DIR --mode llm_live --config verify --partition public_check --repeats 3 --model anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5 --request-id REQUEST_ID_VERIFY
3. assess plainuv run python -m hypotrail assess --study DIR --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID_PLAIN --scorer chinook_contract_v1 --references REFERENCES_ID
4. assess verifyuv run python -m hypotrail assess --study DIR --observations OBSERVATION_SET_ID_VERIFY --scorer chinook_contract_v1 --references REFERENCES_ID
5. compareuv run python -m hypotrail compare --study DIR --control ASSESSMENT_ID_PLAIN --candidate ASSESSMENT_ID_VERIFY
6. reportuv run python -m hypotrail report --study DIR --out FILE

Every line parses with the CLI's parser once its placeholders are filled, so it carries the flags the parser requires: --study and --request-id for run agent (with --mode and --config), --study, --observations, --scorer and --references for assess, --study, --control and --candidate for compare, and --study and --out for report. verify is plain plus one instruction (chapter 03), which is why plain is the Control. The two invocations used two --request-ids.

What happened. The Campaign ran on 2026-11-21 from a checkout at commit f696f58, into a Study created by init --workload chinook, and every command exited 0. commands/ of the kept folder holds the shell's captures, unedited: stdout, stderr and exit status of each command, and start and end times for the two paid invocations only (plain.start, plain.end, verify.start, verify.end), none for the other five commands. commands/ holds no argv. command-output.json is an index assembled after the invocations from the files in commands/ by the agent that drove the slice (.claude/skills/drive/), and it carries the argv of each command; the commands printed human output, not --json. The full argv strings are recorded only in command-output.json; the state comment of #93, last edited at 04:37:01Z, records the --config and --request-id of each paid invocation, and each run.json records the Configuration, the model and the effective limits. The two paid invocations ran from 04:27:42Z (plain.start) to 04:35:29Z (verify.end) on 2026-11-21; the index was committed at 04:36:35Z (067e917) and the captures at 05:35:16Z (c5c1b08), about one hour later. The captures are tied to the invocations only by commit c5c1b08, which added them, and by #93's state comment, written at the time; no digest was posted at run time. Both Attempts are completed with 36 Episodes produced, 0 failed and 0 missing, served by claude-sonnet-5-5. The served model of each Episode is recorded in the kept Inspect log, in the output.model of its Inspect sample at its epoch, read through Inspect's log API; served_models in run.json is the per-Attempt summary. assess gave contract_success 27 of 36 for plain and 21 of 36 for verify; compare found the two Assessments compatible, with a delta of -0.166667 (Candidate minus Control) over 36 of 36 paired Episodes. These are measurements of the system under test; a weak result does not block T20. The Study's copy of the Chinook database is not kept: it is the Workload's own file, by hash.

The rehearsal that ran before the Campaign, at commit f696f58 (chadlis/hypotrail#97), ran every hypotrail argument of the paid lines under the test interpreter, with the provider patched. The uv run launcher was not rehearsed. The paid invocations themselves proved the launcher: both exited 0, as commands/plain.exit and commands/verify.exit in the kept folder record.

The conditions it ran under:

  • the conditions 1 to 5 of D-025 on chadlis/hypotrail#75. (1) A fresh proxy re-reviewed E07 read-only before any paid invocation, and its verdict is "holds" (chadlis/hypotrail#95, comment 7057075785); a verdict of "still ambiguous" would have fallen back to waiting for the owner. (2) The wording added to E07's Case text states the format only, never the values (chadlis/hypotrail#94). (3) LIVE_ACCEPTED is written only as conditional: References E01–E10 reviewed by proxy, owner review pending. (4) A note on an integer-year answer to E07 is written only if one occurs; none occurred (below). (5) Outputs are kept whole, with no extra paid invocation except after a technical failure;
  • Inspect logs and Observation sets kept whole;
  • no further paid invocation was made: the Campaign is its two invocations, with no retry, and one more is allowed only after a technical failure (D-027 on chadlis/hypotrail#75);
  • within the USD 30 budget and five paid live invocations per slice;
  • the two invocations back to back, with the same pinned model and the same limits, both start and end times recorded, and the same served model reported by both Attempts (D-028 on chadlis/hypotrail#75, chadlis/hypotrail#96). Had the reported versions differed, the Comparison would have been flagged and T20 not proven.

E07 was ambiguous: its Reference stores year as text, while its Case text stated no type (the References review by proxy of 2026-11-20, chadlis/hypotrail#10). It was resolved by D-025 on chadlis/hypotrail#75, option B, in chadlis/hypotrail#94: the Case text of E07 now ends with "La colonne year est renvoyée sous forme de texte de quatre caractères.", so the column is returned as four-character text; the Reference and the Scorer are unchanged, and the change is a new Protocol revision. The re-review by a fresh proxy before the Campaign found that it holds (chadlis/hypotrail#95, comment 7057075785). In the kept Observation sets, every E07 answer gave the years as text and matched the Reference: all six (3 Repeats x 2 Configurations) score true on contract_success. So no E07 answer of the Campaign has an integer year, and the note on such an answer that D-025 on chadlis/hypotrail#75 condition 4 asks for, written only if one occurs (D-025 on chadlis/hypotrail#75 addendum 1, D-026 on chadlis/hypotrail#75), is not written. A test of the grid reads the kept answers and fails if one has an integer year while the chapter has no note.

A later correction of a Reference is rescored from the kept outputs under a new Study revision, without a new model call (chapter 10).

LimitAG-12 of the L1 specification (SPEC-LOT-1.md, in the specification kit linked from chadlis/hypotrail#1) asks to alternate the order of plain and verify by the parity of case_index + repeat_index, which needs both Configurations interleaved. run agent runs one Configuration per invocation (one Attempt, one Inspect log), so the Campaign is two invocations, plain then verify: order not alternated (AG-12 of the L1 specification), by D-028 on chadlis/hypotrail#75 (chadlis/hypotrail#96). Inspect has no ordered interleave (its max_tasks is concurrency), and 72 single-Episode invocations would be far over five paid live invocations. So the Configuration is confounded with the time block: a drift of the provider between the two blocks, each of about four minutes, would load on the difference between the Configurations. A second Campaign with alternation would need a change to run agent and a new budget from the owner. This limit replaces the open point this chapter held before the Campaign. The kept Comparison, 4dd96c89, predates chadlis/hypotrail#150, which made compare derive this limit, and so holds no warning; a Comparison of the same two Attempts written now would hold the order warning COMPARISON_ORDER_NOT_ALTERNATED (chapter 11), as the Comparison of the Campaign's rehearsal does.

USABILITY_REVIEW_PENDING

SPEC §10 asks for one more test, a reading by another person, which stays USABILITY_REVIEW_PENDING until it is done. It is required before the path can be declared usable by a third party and does not block the technical acceptance. The path is not declared usable by a third party, here or in the README: the T24 walk was made by an agent, and no one else has read the path yet.

The grid of L2

This chapter is the grid of the first milestone. The grid of L2, the review decision, is docs/product/l2-acceptance.md, and chapter 19 describes it.

17

Decisions

Decisions

decide records one Decision in a Study: the question it answers, the option chosen, who decided (a human or a proxy, with a declared source if any), the ids of the Study's records it concerns, and the Write sequence its author read. The Study is the only decision authority: a Decision is a record of the Study, kept in a file of its own and never rewritten, and nowhere else. decide calls no model; it records what its caller says, after it has checked the ids and the sequence. deviation records a Declared deviation in the same shape (below).

The command

decide holds the Study's writer lock for the whole command, as every command that writes does. A Decision is made on the Write sequence its author read (chapter 04), and a repeated request is a replay (below). It creates or changes no file outside the Study's folder.

The question and the option are free text, and neither may be empty. The author is human or proxy, recorded by its kind only: a human has no name field. The declared source, for either author, is a text, recorded as given, or as null. A cited id given twice is recorded once and the ids are sorted, so the same ids in another order are the same Decision.

A Decision can also name the pending decision it answers (below), by the id of its question and the id of the option chosen, and hold the person's words: one or more texts, each from a file or from an argument, kept whole and in the order given. Nothing is stripped, re-encoded or normalised, and no length is limited; a file is the way for an agent in a terminal, since no shell then reads the words. The words come with the channel by which they reached the caller, as the caller declares it, and with a declared label of who gave them, recorded as given or as null. The words are those of a human. They are not authenticated: the channel is what the caller says, and no check reads the words, or compares them with the option or with anything else. The table below gives each option, what it records and when it is refused.

The words are stored whole and in clear in the Study folder, in the Decision's file, so that committing a Study publishes them.

CommandRequired flagsOptional flags
decide--study DIR, --question TEXT, --option TEXT, --author human|proxy, --artifact ID (repeatable), --request-id ID, --read-sequence N--source TEXT, --question-id ID, --option-id ID, --words-file FILE (repeatable), --words TEXT (repeatable), --channel panel|choice|chat_relayed, --by LABEL, --json
OptionWhat it recordsRefused, with INVALID_ARGUMENTS, when
--question-id IDquestion_id, the id of the pending question; null when not given.It is not keep_after_comparison; it is given without --option-id; --question is not that question's text.
--option-id IDoption_id, the id of the option chosen; null when not given.It is not one of the question's three option ids; it is given without --question-id; --option is not that option's text.
--words-file FILE (repeatable)One text of words.texts: the file's bytes decoded as UTF-8, whole.The file does not exist, cannot be read or is not UTF-8; its text is empty; the author is proxy.
--words TEXT (repeatable)One text of words.texts: the argument, whole. With --words-file, the texts keep the order of the command line.The text is empty; the author is proxy.
--channel panel|choice|chat_relayedwords.channel; chat_relayed when words are given without it. With choice, no text is given: the one text recorded is that of the option --option-id names.It is given without words; it is choice without --option-id, or with a text.
--by LABELwords.by, as given; null when not given.It is given without words.
# read the Write sequence, then decide on it: the ids come from the records the Study holds
uv run python -m hypotrail status --study runs/bike --json
uv run python -m hypotrail decide --study runs/bike --question "Which Configuration do we keep?" --option ridge_v1 --author human --artifact ASSESSMENT_ID --artifact COMPARISON_ID --request-id decide-a --read-sequence N
# answer a pending decision with its ids and the person's words, kept in a file as typed
uv run python -m hypotrail decide --study runs/bike --question "Which Configuration do we keep after this Comparison?" --option "Keep the Candidate" --author human --artifact COMPARISON_ID --question-id keep_after_comparison --option-id keep_candidate --words-file words.txt --by owner --request-id decide-b --read-sequence N

With --json, stdout holds one object, {"ok", "replayed", "decision"}, the same shape as run's with the Decision in place of the Attempt. Without it, the line names the Decision, its author, its option and the count of its cited ids; when words were recorded it adds their count and their channel, for example (words: 2 texts, channel chat_relayed), and never a text.

The ids a Decision may cite

An id is citable when the Study holds a record with that id, of one of these kinds: an Attempt, an Observation set, an Assessment, a Comparison, a Study artifact (references-<digest>, for example), a revision, a freeze record, an Exposure entry, a Decision, whose id is its decision_id, or a Declared deviation, a withdrawal included. decide looks the id up in the Study's records. A revision is cited by its revision id, the one the Study's records give it (chapter 10); its revision_number is not citable, so that one revision never appears under two ids and "the same Decision" stays one thing. A request id, the Study's id, a path and a file's content digest are not citable either: they name no record. An id the Study does not hold is refused, and nothing is written.

status --json lists every Assessment and every Comparison the Study holds, each as its record stores it (chapter 04), so a caller finds their ids there and, for a Comparison, the two Assessments it depends on. It lists no Observation set: a caller finds those ids in the Attempts' records.

The request identity

A Study has one request_id namespace, for its Attempts, its Decisions and its Declared deviations (chapter 04). "The same Decision" is the same question, option, author, source and cited ids, the ids de-duplicated and sorted, then the same question id, option id and words (the same texts in the same order, the same channel and label); the read sequence is not part of it. A decision/1 record, which holds none of the last three, is compared as holding null for each: the same call with none of the new options replays it, and the same call with words is a conflict that names the words. The same request_id with the same Decision is a replay: it is found before the stale-sequence check, so a client whose answer was lost can repeat its decide after its own write moved the sequence on. It returns the recorded Decision with replayed true and writes nothing.

The same request_id with another Decision is a conflict (the table of refusals below). It lists the fields that differ in the order of the sentence above, under the Decision record's own field names.

The checks come in the order of every writer: the arguments (the read sequence value first), the Study, the lock, the request identity, the comparison of the read sequence, then the cited ids. A refusal appends nothing to the write journal.

The Decision record

The record is canonical JSON, of the record kind decision of the contract schema, in one of two versions: decide writes decision/2 (DecisionRecordV2), and a decision/1 record (DecisionRecord) written before it stays valid as it is, is read and replayed, and is never rewritten. A decision/2 record holds the twelve keys of decision/1 and three more, always present, each null when not given (the tables below). A record is validated before it is written and after it is read, against the definition of its own version, with a fault refused (the table of refusals below). Its revision is read under the lock, after the stale-sequence check, so a Decision made after a new revision holds the new one. For a recorded Decision, read_sequence is always write_sequence minus 1.

RecordRequired fieldsOptional fields
decisions/SEQ-DECISION_ID.json (DecisionRecord)kind, schema_version, decision_id, request_id, question, option, author, source (a text or null), cited_ids, revision, read_sequence, write_sequencenone
decisions/SEQ-DECISION_ID.json (DecisionRecordV2)The twelve fields above, schema_version being decision/2, then question_id (an id or null), option_id (an id or null), words (null, or {texts, channel, by, authenticated})none
FieldMeaning
kinddecision
schema_versiondecision/2 for every Decision decide records; decision/1 for one recorded before it.
decision_idA UUID, never derived from the content. A Decision may cite it.
request_idThe --request-id given, at the top level as an Attempt manifest keeps it: it is what a replay and a REQUEST_CONFLICT are read from.
questionThe question the Decision answers, as given.
optionThe option chosen, as given.
authorhuman or proxy. The kind only: no other author field exists.
sourceThe declared source, as given, or null.
cited_idsThe ids of the Study's records the Decision concerns, de-duplicated and sorted; at least one.
revisionThe revision of the Study's StudySpec when the Decision was recorded.
read_sequenceThe Write sequence the author read, given to --read-sequence.
write_sequenceThe position of the begin entry of the command that recorded the Decision in the write journal.
question_idThe --question-id given, the id of the pending question, or null. decision/2 only.
option_idThe --option-id given, the id of one of its options, or null. decision/2 only.
wordsNull, or the person's words: texts, one or more non-empty texts, whole and in the order given; channel, panel, choice or chat_relayed, as the caller declared it; by, the declared label or null; authenticated, always false. Stored, and never read by a check. decision/2 only.

The Decision history

status --json lists the Study's Decisions, in the order they were recorded; a Study with no Decision lists none ([]). Each entry holds every field of the Decision record, as decide recorded it, and the two keys of the table below, which the record does not hold.

The human output of status gains one line when the Study holds Decisions, for example Decisions: 4 recorded, 1 no longer applies; a Study with no Decision prints no such line.

Key of a Decision in status --jsonMeaning
kindThe record's own field, decision.
schema_versionThe record's own field.
decision_idThe record's own field.
request_idThe record's own field.
questionThe record's own field.
optionThe record's own field.
authorThe record's own field.
sourceThe record's own field.
cited_idsThe record's own field.
revisionThe record's own field: the revision when the Decision was recorded, which stays in the record whatever the Study's revision becomes.
read_sequenceThe record's own field.
write_sequenceThe record's own field.
question_idThe record's own field; a decision/1 record lists none.
option_idThe record's own field; a decision/1 record lists none.
wordsThe record's own field, the texts whole; a decision/1 record lists none.
appliesTrue when none of the conditions below holds. Computed when status reads the Study.
reasonsA list of {"reason", "id"}, one for each condition that holds (a Decision with two causes lists both); [] when the Decision applies.
evidence_changeWhat happened to the Decision's evidence since: null when no Assessment it cites or reaches has a later Assessment, otherwise an object with the keys of the table of evidence keys below. Computed when status reads the Study.

When a Decision still applies

A Decision no longer applies when any condition of the table below holds. The rule is computed when status reads the Study, from the ids the Decision cites and the records of the Study. It is never stored: nothing is written when a Decision stops applying, the Decision's own file is byte for byte the same, and status takes no lock, advances no Write sequence and writes nothing. No record marks a Decision outdated, and the rule is no general invalidation engine: beside the ids a Decision cites, it follows one hop, from a cited Comparison to its two Assessments, and goes no further.

A cited record made under an earlier revision does not by itself make a Decision outdated: an Assessment may apply its revision's Protocol also to outputs recorded under an earlier revision, and the id a revision reason gives is the revision id a Decision cites. A rescore under a new Scorer version supersedes the earlier Assessment, since the Scorer counts at any version; an Assessment of the same Observation set by another Scorer does not. When several later Assessments exist, the reason names the latest.

"Later" is the one order of the later Assessment, stated once in chapter 10 and read from the write journal (chapter 04). A later Decision on the same question changes nothing: it does not make an earlier one outdated.

The two Assessments of the hop are the Control and the Candidate the Comparison's record names; a record that names no roles has its left Assessment as the Control and its right one as the Candidate. The hop starts from a cited Comparison only: a cited Attempt or Observation set reaches no Assessment. The reasons of the cited ids come first, in the order of the table; then those of the hop, walking the cited Comparisons in the order of the Decision's cited ids, the Control before the Candidate. An Assessment reached through two cited Comparisons gives one reason, at the first. A Decision that cites a Comparison and one of its Assessments can hold two reasons for one later Assessment, one for the cited Assessment and one for the hop. Whether a Decision applies reads the order of the Assessments only, never when the Decision was recorded: a Decision recorded after a later Assessment exists does not apply from its first read.

ReasonWhenid
cited_id_not_heldA cited id is no longer held by the Study.The cited id.
revision_changedThe current revision differs from the Decision's revision.The current revision's revision value.
assessment_supersededA cited Assessment has a later Assessment of the same Observation set and Scorer id.The later Assessment's id.
compared_assessment_supersededAn Assessment that a cited Comparison names, its Control or its Candidate, has a later Assessment of the same Observation set and Scorer id.The latest later Assessment's id.
compared_assessment_not_heldAn Assessment that a cited Comparison names is not held by the Study.The id of the Assessment not held.

What happened to the evidence

The evidence of a Decision is the Assessments it cites and the Assessments it has reached, an Assessment being reached when a cited Comparison names it, by the hop above. When some of them have later Assessments, status --json says what happened to the evidence since, in the last key of the history table, and leaves whether the Decision applies as the rule above gives it: a repeat still makes a Decision outdated, and the state is what says that nothing changed.

One line is given per Assessment of the evidence that has a later Assessment of the same Observation set and Scorer id, and that Assessment is the line's anchor. The lines come in the order in which the cited ids first reach their anchors: a cited Assessment at its own id, a cited Comparison's Control before its Candidate. A line names the first cited Comparison that names its anchor, with the anchor's role in it, or none when the anchor is only cited directly. A side that the Study does not hold gives no line. The state of a line is read from the later Assessments of its anchor, and the state of the whole is the strongest of its lines, in the order of the table of states.

A later Assessment counts as rescored or changed only when the earlier Assessment its rescoring names is the anchor or one later than it; otherwise it counts as a repeat. A new Assessment names as earlier the latest one that differs from it (chapter 10), which can be older than the anchor: assessed again on a corrected References set, an Observation set names its Assessment on the set before the correction, and the Items it lists as changed did not change since the anchor. The count of changed Items counts each Item once, however many of its Criteria changed. A line gives the Metric values of the anchor and of the latest later Assessment as their records hold them; no difference of the two is computed, and the Comparison is not updated.

StatePhraseWhen, over the later Assessments of the anchor
result_changed"result changed"At least one counts and changed an Item.
evidence_rescored"evidence rescored"At least one counts, and none that counts changed an Item.
repeated"repeated, nothing changed"None counts.
KeyOfMeaning
statethe objectThe strongest state of its lines.
linesthe objectThe lines, at least one.
assessment_ida lineThe anchor.
comparison_ida lineThe first cited Comparison, in the order of the cited ids, that names the anchor; null when the anchor is only cited directly.
rolea linecontrol or candidate, the anchor's role in that Comparison (its left and right Assessments when the record names no roles); null with no Comparison.
latest_assessment_ida lineThe latest later Assessment of the anchor.
statea lineOne of the table of states.
changed_item_counta lineThe number of distinct Items that the later Assessments that count list as changed; 0 when none counts.
metricsa lineOne entry per Metric of the anchor's record, in its order.
idan entry of metricsThe Metric's id.
citedan entry of metricsThe Metric's value in the anchor's record.
latestan entry of metricsThe value of the Metric of that id in the latest later Assessment's record, or null.

The pending decision

A reviewer who opens a Study finds in status --json what waits for a decision. A Comparison is pending when both its Assessments are of the current revision, its two Assessments have no later Assessment, by the rule of the hop, and no Decision that applies cites it. An Assessment is of the current revision when its record holds a study_revision whose revision is the Study's current one; one whose record holds none, or that the Study does not hold, is not. A Comparison written under a new revision from Assessments of an earlier one is therefore not pending, and neither is a Comparison whose side is not held.

Each pending Comparison comes with one question and three options, the same for every Comparison of every Study, of either Track: the core holds them, no Workload declares them and no client holds them. A client answers with decide, citing the Comparison, with the question's text and the chosen option's text; the Comparison then stops being pending while that Decision applies. It does not become pending again when a side gets a later Assessment, since the Comparison then has a later Assessment and the Decision no longer applies; a new compare of the latest Assessments writes another Comparison, which can be pending. Nothing is stored: the list is derived each time status reads the Study, and an answer with an option of its own wording closes the pending decision like any other, while that Decision applies.

KeyOfMeaning
comparison_idan entryThe pending Comparison.
control_assessment_idan entryIts Control, as its record names it (its left Assessment when the record names no roles).
candidate_assessment_idan entryIts Candidate, as its record names it (its right Assessment when the record names no roles).
questionan entryThe question, with the keys below.
optionsan entryThe three options, in the order of the table of the question, each with the keys below.
idthe questionIts identifier.
textthe questionIts text, to pass to --question.
idan optionIts identifier.
textan optionIts text, to pass to --option.
consequencean optionWhat recording it does in the Study.
IdentifierOfTextConsequence
keep_after_comparisonthe questionWhich Configuration do we keep after this Comparison?none
keep_controlan optionKeep the ControlA Decision that cites this Comparison is recorded with the option "Keep the Control". The Comparison stops being pending while that Decision applies. Beyond that Decision and its two write-journal entries, nothing is written, and the Study enforces nothing on later work.
keep_candidatean optionKeep the CandidateA Decision that cites this Comparison is recorded with the option "Keep the Candidate". The Comparison stops being pending while that Decision applies. Beyond that Decision and its two write-journal entries, nothing is written, and the Study enforces nothing on later work: a later compare writes a new Comparison that is pending under the same rule.

The Study on disk

decide writes one file of its own, besides the two entries of the write journal (chapter 04). SEQ is the Decision's position among the Study's Decisions, count plus one, six digits, as in exposures/; it is not the Write sequence, which the record holds in write_sequence.

PathWritten byContent
decisions/SEQ-DECISION_ID.jsondecideOne Decision, published atomically and never rewritten.

Declared deviations

deviation records one Declared deviation in a Study: one known limit on what results of the Study show, declared in the Study because its records cannot establish it (for example an owner review of References still pending): what is declared, its author, a declared source if any, and the ids of the Study's records it cites. It follows the Decision record's shape: one file of its own, never rewritten, made on the Write sequence its author read and under the writer lock. A derived deviation, such as the order of the Episodes that compare reads from the manifests (chapter 11), is a deviation but not a Declared deviation.

A Declared deviation either declares or withdraws. A declaration cites Attempts and References sets only: an Attempt by its id, the References set of any revision by its Study artifact id. A withdrawal cites exactly one earlier declaration and nothing else, with its own author and text. A declaration covers a side of a Comparison when it cites that side's Attempt, or the References set that side's Assessment was scored against; since compare refuses two Assessments scored against different References sets (chapter 07), a References set covers both sides. A withdrawal covers no side.

When compare writes a Comparison, each declaration in force that covers a side gives it one warning (chapter 07), in the order of the declarations' write_sequence, and the status is computed as without it. A withdrawn declaration gives none. The report shows the warnings each Comparison recorded and reads no Declared deviation.

The checks come in the order of every writer: the arguments, the Study, the lock, the request identity, the comparison of the read sequence, then the ids cited, in this order: an id the Study does not hold; a Declared deviation cited with any other id; several Declared deviations; a withdrawal cited; an id of a kind that cannot be cited; a declaration that is withdrawn already. The first that fails is the refusal, and nothing is written. "The same Declared deviation" of a replay is the same text, author, source and cited ids.

status --json lists every Declared deviation, withdrawals included, in the order they were recorded, with the two keys of the table below that the record does not hold: they are computed when status reads the Study, and never stored. A Decision may cite a Declared deviation, or a withdrawal, which no Declared deviation may.

# declare what is pending, citing the References set it concerns, on the Write sequence just read
uv run python -m hypotrail deviation --study runs/bike --text "owner review of References pending" --author proxy --artifact REFERENCES_ID --request-id deviation-a --read-sequence N
# withdraw it: a later Declared deviation that cites it alone
uv run python -m hypotrail deviation --study runs/bike --text "the owner reviewed the References" --author human --artifact DECLARED_DEVIATION_ID --request-id deviation-b --read-sequence N

The figures read

figures reads a whole Study and gives, for each of its Comparisons, the lines of the Comparison's paired-deltas file and two summaries computed from its paired deltas: the bins of a histogram and the five numbers of a box. It is a read, as status is: it writes nothing and holds no lock; it probes whether a writer holds the lock (lock.is_held) with a non-blocking shared lock taken and dropped at once, so a writer starting at that instant is refused. It records no Exposure and leaves the Write sequence where it was, so it answers while a writer holds the Study. The numbers are computed each time the Study is read and never stored: no record of the Study holds them, and no record changes.

With --json, stdout holds one object whose key paths are those of the table below, in the form a client lists them ([] for an element of a list). It holds one entry per Comparison, whatever its Track, in the order of the Comparisons of status --json. On the Regression Track a line stands for an Item, on the Agent Track for a Case; no Episode value is read. A line keeps the file's own column names, with each value as a number, and every number printed is finite. A later version may add keys to the object, or to an entry, so a client should ignore a key it does not know rather than refuse the object. Without --json, the read prints one line per Comparison: its id, its count of pairs and its five numbers, or the reason it has none.

The read refuses the Study where status refuses it, with the same code, before it reads any Comparison. A Comparison whose figures cannot be given does not refuse the read: its entry holds the first reason of the table below that applies, its three figure keys are null, and the other entries are given in full. The file of a Comparison whose main delta is withheld is not opened. A Comparison of an earlier Study revision is given as it was recorded, from its own file. The read recomputes no delta: the main delta of a Comparison stays the one status --json lists.

A module of the core computes the numbers, with the standard library only, for both Tracks: the paired delta of a line is the file's last column. The table of definitions gives each rule beside the name of the function of that module that applies it.

# the figures of every Comparison of the Study, read only
uv run python -m hypotrail figures --study runs/bike --json

The medianQUARTILESp = 0.5
The third quartileQUARTILESp = 0.75

When the paired deltas are one value, or all equal, the five numbers are that value and there is one bin whose low and high are that value, holding them all.

Limits

Read at view time, never stored

Whether a Decision still applies is computed each time status reads the Study. No record is written when it changes. The panel shows it for each Decision, as status --json gives it (chapter 18); the Workloads do not show an outdated Decision yet.

Free text, no declared question

The question and the option are the caller's words: decide records them as given and checks neither against the question and the options that the core supplies for a pending decision (above). The Study declares no question of its own, so nothing checks that an option is one of a list.

A corrected References set is another set

A corrected References set, added by a new revision (chapter 10), is a Study artifact of its own, with a new artifact_id. A declaration that cites the earlier set does not cover a side whose Assessment was scored against the corrected one; a new declaration that cites the corrected set does.

A Comparison is not updated

A Comparison written before a declaration or a withdrawal is not updated: its file stays as it was, so it holds no warning for a later declaration and keeps the warning of a declaration withdrawn since. A new compare of the same two Assessments is the remedy: it writes another Comparison, with its own id, from the declarations in force then. A new compare of public_check Items appends an Exposure entry, as every Comparison of them does (chapter 12).

Withdrawn stays withdrawn

No Declared deviation may cite a withdrawal, so a declaration once withdrawn stays withdrawn. To declare it again, record a new declaration: it gets a new ID, and covers its sides like any other.

Not Inspect's invalidation

Inspect records a change to a log as an appended update with its author, reason and time, and keeps the values as written; a Declared deviation follows that shape in a record of the Study. It departs on the withdrawal: Inspect's uninvalidate_samples (pinned inspect-ai==0.3.287) clears a sample's invalidation in the log, where a withdrawal is a later record that nothing undoes, beside the declaration it withdraws.

A withdrawal changes no Decision

A Decision that cites a Declared deviation still applies once that Declared deviation is withdrawn: a withdrawn one is still held by the Study, and no reason of the table above names it.

A record without rescoring is a repeat

A later Assessment whose record holds no rescoring key counts as a repeat, as one whose rescoring is null does: what it changed cannot be read from its record.

A null latest value has two causes

The latest value of a Metric in a line is null either because the latest record holds no Metric of that id or because the Metric's value is null in it. The listing of Assessments holds the whole Metric, with its status and its reason.

A Decision is not an Exposure

Recording a Decision reads no public_check Item and writes no freeze record or journal entry of the Exposure journal. It cites ids; it does not show what they hold.

Which ADR explains what

ADRWhat it explains here
0001Why the Decision record and its command are in the core, which imports neither Inspect nor a Track.
0003Why this chapter describes current behaviour and is updated in the pull request that changes it.
0004Why the Study is the only decision authority, why decide and deviation require --read-sequence and --request-id, why whether a Decision still applies, or a Declared deviation is withdrawn, is computed when the Study is read, and why a client also reads through figures.

The tests are tests/test_decide.py, tests/test_declared_deviation.py and tests/test_figures.py, with the rows of tests/test_stale_sequence.py and tests/test_write_sequence.py that name decide; the tables above are compared with the code in tests/test_handbook.py. They run offline and call no model.

18

Client

The VS Code client

The VS Code client is a TypeScript extension that talks to the CLI through one module, the bridge. It reads a Study and records a Decision only through the CLI, and holds no Decision, no record and no path of its own: the Study is the only decision authority (ADR 0004). It ships the bridge, the way to find a Study, and a panel that shows a Study's pending decisions with their evidence. Of the chat channels, Claude Code's is the UserPromptSubmit hook template below, and Copilot's is the two tools of the extension with the template of Copilot's instructions file, both below.

Install

The extension is installed from a checkout, in three steps, in this order. No packaged file is built, and no licence is chosen: the manifest carries no licence key. The first step is the README's own install; the second builds the extension in its folder; the third, in VS Code, picks that folder.

StepCommandWhere
1uv sync --locked --all-extrasthe checkout root
2npm ci && npm run compilethe extension's folder (below)
3Developer: Install Extension from Location...VS Code, picking the extension's folder
NeedsVersion
Node22 (node --version prints it)
VS Code^1.95.0, the manifest's engines.vscode (code --version prints it)

Where it lives, and its tooling

The choice of the client's location and tooling, which ADR 0004 leaves to L2.4, is recorded here. The extension is written in TypeScript, with the compiler as the only build step: no bundler, and the compiler's output folder is generated and not tracked. Its development dependencies are the compiler and the type definitions of VS Code and of Node; it has no runtime dependency. Its tests are TypeScript under its test/ folder and run under Node's own runner (node:test), with the vscode module replaced with a stub of that folder: no test runs inside VS Code. The second job of .github/workflows/tests.yml installs the locked development dependencies and runs them on every pull request; the job pytest runs the checks that read the client's files with no Node.

The literal lists the client uses (the CLI's program and arguments, the limits, the outcomes, the walk) are kept in the extension's contract.json, which the extension imports and the handbook's tests read: the tables of this chapter are compared with it.

WhatValue
The extension's folderclients/vscode/
The command that compiles and runs its tests, in that foldernpm test
The command that checks the types and compiles, in that foldernpm run compile

The line budget

The client stays under the line budget of the table below, C6 of chadlis/hypotrail#103, which counts TypeScript, HTML and CSS and leaves out the tests and the generated output (D-031 on chadlis/hypotrail#75, item 11). The check is tests/test_client_line_budget.py, a test of the job pytest that needs no Node. It lists the tracked files of the extension's folder at each check and holds no list of files: each file is counted, a test, refused or other, under its kind and its folder. Every line of a counted file counts, blank lines and comments included. One refused file fails the check, wherever it is. The other files (the manifest, the lockfile, contract.json, the kept CLI output) are neither counted nor refused; contract.json holds lists only, one flat object of strings, numbers and lists of them, with no string over 200 characters.

WhatValue

A counted kind, outside the tests' folder.mts
